# ROM Expansion

Enemies, mini-bosses and copy abilities ported from *Kirby: Nightmare in Dream Land* into *Kirby & the Amazing Mirror*. `KatAMExpansion.cs` applies them. Every version of KatAM works (USA `B8KE`, European `B8KP`, Japanese revision 1 `B8KJ`), with the NiDL ROM of the same region: USA (`A7KE`), European (`A7KP`) or Japanese (`A7KJ`, *Hoshi no Kirby: Yume no Izumi Deluxe*). The NiDL ROMs hold the same data, the Japanese one's frame tables `0x3A8C` further and its descriptors at `0x08742DAC`, the European one's `0x23C7EC` further and at `0x0897B678`. NiDL addresses in these notes and the code are the USA ones.

European games show the enemies' English names in every language. Japanese games show none: their HP bar names (both languages of the name table, `0x08D2C394`) are images of katakana, with no English letters to compose names from. Their name copies are the USA's code, so they get the same patch, with empty names (else the bar shows another type's name, or garbage for type `0x44`).

## KatAM versions

The code is written against the KatAM decompilation, which builds the USA version. `build.sh` links it once per version: the USA build against `katam.elf`, the others against `versions/<version>.txt`, the same symbols' addresses in that version, found in its ROM by `find_symbols.py <katam decomp> <ROM> <symbols...>` (functions by their code with call targets and pointers masked, variables through the pointers in matched functions, and the rest by the shift of the nearest symbol found). The shift is only checked against the first bytes of the function, so check what it finds when it can't match a function by its code (Japan's `sub_08034E14` is `0x08034DD8`, found from the call in Mr. Frosty's code; the shift gave a similar function). The USA link also gets the decompilation's static functions the code calls (`--just-symbols` only takes global ones; `sub_0809D060`, the mini-boss defeat). Those tables also hold what `KatAMExpansion.cs` patches in each version (sprite tables, spawn and parameter tables, the hit flash branch, the HP bar name copies, the number of anims: 938 in the Japanese ROM, 939 in the others). When the code starts using a KatAM symbol the tables don't have, `build.sh` says so: rerun `find_symbols.py` with the full list for each version's ROM.

## How they fit in

KatAM treats object types `0x00`-`0x37` as enemies all over its code, and the only free one is `0x31`. So every ported enemy is type `0x31`, and the template's behavior byte picks which: high nibble = enemy (`NIDL_*` in `nidl.h`), low nibble = its variant. What the game reads per type is shared, so `nidl.c` handles it per enemy:

| Shared per type | How each enemy gets its own |
|---|---|
| Animations (`AnimInfo` table, indexed by the object's frame) | One table holding every enemy's frames; each enemy has its own range (`animBase`) and anim ID |
| Palette key | The type has none; each enemy loads its palette with its anim ID as the key |
| Hurt frames (indexes 0 and 1) | A generic 32x32 frame drawn from the last 16 tiles of the object's VRAM, where each enemy uploads its own hurt frame |
| HP bar name | The game's two name copies (`0x08034ED2`, `0x08035B34`) are patched to call `NidlCopyName` |
| HP | Each enemy's in `gNidlEnemyHp` (the mini-bosses' `gNidlMinibossHp`, the bosses' `gNidlBossHp` by the number of players), which the randomizer's HP options write (`KatAMHP.cs`) |
| HP bar fill | HP times a factor per type (0x4000 / the HP at the start), >> 8, 64 full: KatAM works it out as an object takes the bar (`0x08034EF8`) and every frame after (`0x08035354`), both patched to call `NidlHpBarFill`, which takes the ported ones' from their own HP (`NidlMaxHp`) and KatAM's from its tables (`gUnk_083513E8`, `gUnk_08351458`, `gUnk_08351628`) |
| Behavior start after a hit | `NidlStart` calls the enemy's own start |
| Gravity | The engine pulls every enemy down unless flag `0x40` is set (KatAM's flying enemies set it); `NidlStart` sets it, and each enemy moves up and down like in NiDL (`NidlFall` is the engine's gravity, for the ones that just stand or walk) |

Mini-bosses work the same way on object types `0x40`-`0x42` (`OBJ_EMPTY_40`-`42`), which KatAM already treats as mini-bosses everywhere (they're in every `ObjType38To42` check, and `gUnk_08352D80[type - 0x38]` is the mini-boss music): see Mini-bosses below.

The hit flash also looked the palette up again by type when it ended; its branch at `0x080857EE` is patched to keep the enemy's palette (which is what it found for the game's own enemies anyway).

## Files

- `nidl.h`, `nidl.c`: the shared part (creation, palette, hurt frame, names, NiDL's frame sequences, helpers like facing Kirby and stopping at ledges).
- `nidl_data.c`: `gNidlData`, left blank and filled in by `KatAMExpansion.cs` (names, and each enemy's `animBase`, anim ID and hurt frame).
- `abilities.h`, `abilities.c`, `abilities/*.c`: the new copy abilities (see Abilities below); `abilities/*.c` has one file per ability, and `star_rod_stand.c` the Star Rod's stand in the NiDL hub and the NiDL ability room's statues (object 0x9A's subtypes 1-7: KatAM's ability statue with the NiDL enemy giving the ability shrunk for its figure, `KatAMExpansionMotions.cs`'s `BuildNidlStatues()`, on KatAM's stand, `sub_080988B0`; `gNidlStandKeys` the two anims' palette keys).
- `nidl_miniboss.c`, `minibosses/*.c`: the mini-bosses (types `0x40`-`0x42`): creation, the start of the fight, the helpers' pick against them, and one file per mini-boss.
- `nidl_boss.c`, `bosses/*.c`: the bosses (type `0x43`): creation, the start of the fight, the mirror shard they leave, and one file per boss (see Bosses). What Kirby inhales in their fights is an enemy (type `0x31`, hidden from the randomizer's options): Whispy Woods' apples (`enemies/apple.c`), Mr. Shine's falling stars (`enemies/star.c`), Paint Roller's paintings (`enemies/painting.c`); and the star King Dedede spits swallowed Kirbys out in (`enemies/spit_star.c`).
- `enemies/*.c`: one file per enemy, written against the [KatAM decompilation](https://github.com/jiangzhengwenjz/katam) headers. NiDL runs enemies as coroutine scripts; each script is copied as tables of steps.
- `build.sh /path/to/katam`: compiles everything with the decompilation's `agbcc`, links it at `0x09000000` for each KatAM version and writes `../KatAMExpansionCode.cs` (each version's code, the symbols `KatAMExpansion.cs` uses and what it patches).
- `find_symbols.py`, `versions/*.txt`: the KatAM symbols' addresses in each version (see above).
- `title.c`, `title.h`: Nightmare in Dream Land's title screen swarm (see Title screen below).
- `calm.c`: the randomizer's Reduce Flashing option: `UpdateScreenDma` and `UpdateScreenCpuSet` (the VBlank's copies of the blend registers) are detoured to it (`KatAMExpansionCalm.cs`), and copy `gBldRegs` with the screen's brightness (BLDY, while the effect is lighten) following the game's at most one level a frame up and at once down, the game's own put back after (what the screen shows is kept in `gBldRegs`' padding, the struct being 6 bytes of its 8). It needs only itself: without a NiDL ROM, the option pads the ROM and copies the expansion's code in, and allocates its trampolines in `gNidlCalmTrampolines`.
- `skip.c`: the randomizer's Skip the Tutorial option: `sbClearFunctions[1]` (`sbWorldPropsClear`, which `ClearSaveBuffer` runs after zeroing a blank, erased or unreadable file's world props: a new save) points to `NidlSkipClear` (`KatAMExpansionCalm.cs`), which runs it, then sets `gAIKirbyState` to `AI_KIRBY_STATE_NORMAL` (what the game sets once the cutscene after the tutorial ends: a new game then starts in Central Circle, `sub_080332BC`, and respawns there, `sub_08002C98`) and gives the tutorial's big chest's item as opening it would (room 0x325's chest, its template's item and treasure index as the randomizer left them, in `gNidlSkip`: a map, a spray, music, an HP up, or a mirror shard through its index 0x80 + n; its index marked, so the collection counts it). The demos clear too and set the state themselves. A file no clear touched (saved by a ROM without the option, or mid-tutorial) is caught at the single-player start, which a new and a continued game both run (`sub_08123FD4`, detoured to `NidlSkipStart`, its trampoline in `gNidlSkipTrampolines`): below 300 the state is set, the chest's item given unless its index is marked, and the file saved, before the start picks the room and spot (`sub_08002C98`: at 300, Central Circle's continue spot; room 0x321's mirror cutscene and room 0x323's intro skip themselves). Multiplayer starts elsewhere (`sub_080329C8`). It needs only itself, as `calm.c`.
- `warpstar.c`: the ported warp stars' rides (`KatAMExpansionRooms.cs`, `WarpFlights()`, `WarpRideDetours()`): four of KatAM's routines detoured to it, their trampolines in `gNidlRideTrampolines`, and the riding star's task swapped. The warp star's rev (`sub_0800C270`: KatAM's star waits 300 frames for the helpers with Kirby on it) is skipped for a ported star (subtype 6 and after), as NiDL's is off at once; the Power Orb's waits, but only until every Kirby in its room (not knocked out) is on it (`finale.c`'s `NidlFinaleAllAboard()`, the star's riders in its `+0xB5`). The Power Orb's star's flight starts with a step marked `0xFC` (`NIDL_RIDE_FINALE`): `NidlRideStep` holds Kirby there (no step run, no speed; hidden, `0x400`, when `PowerOrbRideSize()` says so) while `bosses/power_orb.c` moves him, and a step whose `+0xA` is `0xFFFF` (`NIDL_RIDE_ASK`) takes its size from `PowerOrbRideSize()` (the star's flag `0x400` hides it). A flight's room change (`sub_08055D9C`, a step flagged `0x40`: KatAM's flights change room once, to the star's room) on a step marked `0xFE` takes Kirby to the room in the step's `+0xA`, at the cell in its `+0` and `+2`, so a ride goes through several rooms. A flight's step (`sub_080534D0`): a ride's end step marked `0xFD` (not `0x80`) ends the flight where Kirby stands after his tumble, as KatAM's hop-off leaves him (the flight's flags off, facing the step's way), without KatAM's hop. Kirby's drawing (`sub_0803F46C`) and the star he rides (`sub_0808EEF4`, the task `sub_0808EDB8` starts: its one pointer becomes `NidlRideStar`, as its first instructions keep `gCurTask` in `r3`, which a detour's jump back used to clobber: the trampolines keep every register now): where a ride's step has a size (its `+0xA`, NiDL's matrix value, 256 / the size), the OAM pieces they just drew (`gUnk_030031C0`, from the count `gUnk_030024F0` had) become affine at that size around the star's middle (Kirby's spot), each moved in toward it, as NiDL draws them; matrices 7, 15, 23 and 31 (`gOamBuffer`), by the pieces' flips (KatAM's own sprites use matrix 0 only).
- `finale.c`, `ending.c`: the Fountain of Dreams finale's glue (the Power Orb's warp star, the flights out of Nightmare's room) and NiDL's ending down from the moon (see Bosses, Nightmare).
- `unlock.c`: the randomizer's Unlock the Ability Room option: Central Circle's door to the ability room (0x3CA; the NiDL hub's copy waits for its own world slot, `0x1D`, set while all 7 NiDL levels' big switches are pressed: `rooms.c`'s `BigGetStateSlot`, `NIDL_ABILITY_SLOT`, so this option doesn't open it) is a hub mirror object (`0x8C`, `special_hub_mirror.c`) of world slot `0x12`, hidden under its wall until that slot is set (KatAM sets it once the 15 big switches are pressed: `WorldMapUnlockMain`, then the door's scene). The hub mirror's init (`SpecialHubMirrorInit`, its address in `CreateSpecialHubMirror`'s literal pool) becomes `NidlHubMirrorInit` (`KatAMExpansionCalm.cs`), which sets slot `0x12` for that door, then runs the original (kept in `gNidlUnlock`). It needs only itself, as `calm.c`.
- `mirra.c`: Mirras (object `0x34`) fall, and open their door where they are: KatAM turns the engine's gravity off for a Mirra (its start, `sub_080B1174`: flags `0x40`, and `0x800`, which keeps it from moving at all), so one stood in the air once what it stood on broke, and opened its door (`sub_080B11C0`: the special block record of its template's cell) where it started. The Mirra's spawn becomes `NidlCreateMirra` (the original's address in `gNidlMirra`), which clears both flags after KatAM's `CreateMirra`; `sub_080B11C0` jumps to `NidlMirraOpen` (`KatAMExpansion.cs`, with the spawn table), which marks its encounter as KatAM's does and opens the door of the cell the Mirra is in, or of the first cell below it with one (where it's falling; nothing solid between, cells read through `GetCollisionTile`, which the bigger rooms replace), else its template's cell's: a door is a block record of the cell as it is now whose new value is a door record's of the same cell (`MirraDoor()`). KatAM's own Mirras stand on solid ground, so theirs stays KatAM's door. Installed with the ROM Expansion (`AddEnemies()`).
- `chest.c`: mirror shards in chests: a chest's opening (`sub_0800AFC8`) is detoured to it (`KatAMExpansionChests.cs`). A chest holding item `0x40 + n` (an id KatAM doesn't use) collects shard n, saves, and spawns the shard object (`0x65`, subtype n, made as the bosses make theirs) 24 pixels above itself, which runs the game's own shard pickup (cutscene `n + 1`, then the Dimension Mirror room); opened once shard n is owned (a boss's, the field's, another chest's), it spawns a Maxim Tomato there instead (KatAM's own tomato object, as an owned field shard is one); other chests run the original (its trampoline in `gNidlChestTrampolines`). The randomizer gives each of these chests a treasure index of its own, `0x88 + k` (16 at most): `HasChest` reads past `gTreasures.chestFields` into `shardField`'s bits 8-23 there, which nothing else uses (`NumShardsCollected` counts bits 0-7, `NumChestsCollected` the first 0x80), and opening one marks it, so a chest shows open once it's been opened, and only then. They're left out of the rooms' 1E8 chest lists (`sub_08002B30`, which would loop forever looking for them, isn't called). The shards the randomizer puts in rooms instead (the option off) spawn through `NidlCreateShard` (object `0x65`'s spawn table entry, `OwnedFieldShards()`): a room's own shard (its template in the ROM, subtype under 8) already owned is a Maxim Tomato made from its template as `CreateBonus` makes a room's food (so it's gone once eaten until the room is entered again); the shards the game makes as it goes (a boss's, a chest's, a NiDL boss room's way out) and the Dimension Mirror stay shards. Bosses Give Random Shards (`RandomizeBossShards()`): each of KatAM's boss defeats (`gUnk_08350E58`'s handlers) asks `HasShard` about its shard as a constant right before the call (`movs r0, #n; bl HasShard`, 9 sites with King Golem's harder rematch), each constant becomes the boss's new shard; the shard the boss makes in its room (its template's subtype the game's) is made as the game's, then given the new number (`unkD4`, `unk83`) from `gNidlShard.bossShards`, the template keeping the game's, which places the victory dance (`gUnk_08352DBE`) in that room; the pause screen's `gAreaMapRoomIdsAndShards` takes the new numbers; the NiDL bosses' templates (types `0x43`, `0x44`) take theirs in their behavior's low nibble. It also holds the Squeak Squad rooms' chests (`SqueakCreateChest`: their own open and closed looks), cannons (`SqueakCreateCannon`: fixed ones firing after the DS's wait, every shot carried on as the DS's), and fuse cannons' landing (`SqueakCannonArrival`: KatAM's without its bounces forward), described in `Documentation/Squeak Squad Notes.md`. Like `calm.c`, it needs only itself.
- `squeak/gimmicks.c`: the Squeak Squad rooms' gimmicks that talk to each other. Messages: a byte per gimmick of each loaded room's slot (`SqueakMailOf()`, the EWRAM heap's last 0x100 bytes: `rooms.c`, the heap 0x100 smaller), the kind + 1 of the message waiting, cleared as a room is loaded into the slot (`BigFillLevelInfo` calls `SqueakGimmicksLoaded`); a message waits until its gimmick is made and takes it. A task made with each such room (from `gSqueakGimmickRooms`, `KatAMExpansionSqueakRooms.cs`' lists: per room its trigger areas, delay relays and switch block groups, 24 bytes each) runs the ones with no look: trigger areas (a Kirby's bounds moving into the area send its targets kind 2, once until he's left), relays (a message starts them; each target's message goes after its wait), switch block groups (a message breaks the chain from the group's cell, `sub_08001408` and KatAM's chain breaker `sub_0800E0E4`, as KatAM's lever walls). The ones with a look are object `0x8D` (the hammer peg's type: `SqueakCreateSwitch` is its spawn, KatAM's own pegs made by the game's `CreateHammerPeg`, kept in `gSqueakSwitch`), by their template's `unk11`: the small star switch (`SQUEAK_SWITCH_OBJECT`: pressed by a Kirby touching it, it sends its targets kind 0, then stays down) and the shutters (`SQUEAK_SHUTTER_OBJECT`: the DS's states and animations, drawn with `SQUEAK_SHUTTER`'s looks, turned in `KatAMExpansionSqueak.cs`; while shut its cells are KatAM's solid block 0x0D, the spikes' its spike block 0x1C, their own values back once it's open: KatAM's solid-object flags and Gordo's harm didn't work on the hammer peg's type) and the fire columns (`SQUEAK_FIRE_OBJECT`: the DS's states, its burning, its pilot flame, its messages; it hurts while it burns as KatAM's flame column does, its `unk68` damage 3 with flag 0x100 on its box). Also the item holders: grass tufts (`SQUEAK_GRASS_OBJECT`: rustled walked through, cut by an attack, its blades `SQUEAK_BLADES_OBJECT`, grown back after 600 frames when it held something) and barrels (`SQUEAK_BARREL_OBJECT`: burst by an attack), letting out the KatAM object their template's `unk14`/`unk16` name (the room's own item or enemy record the DS hides in them; an ability KatAM's ability star). An attack is told by the mark KatAM's collision pass leaves on an object an attack reaches (`flags` 0x40000, the attacker in `unk6C`); a Kirby's plain touch is masked out (`unk5C` 0x80). And the icicles (`SQUEAK_ICICLE_OBJECT`: shake once a Kirby is under them, fall, hurt as they fall, shatter on the floor, hang again 180 frames later), the falling rocks (`SQUEAK_ROCK_OBJECT`: hidden till a message, then fall and break) and, in the room's task, the random relays (gimmick 0x17: random waits, then a message to their targets), the camera areas (0x14: a Kirby entering one has his view's bounds' targets, `unk_S32Vec2_7C`/`84` of his `gCurLevelInfo`, set to its bounds, his room's again once he's out of them; not in fight arenas' rooms) the effect emitters (0x19: lava sparks and water bubbles, `squeak.c`'s `SqueakEffectInRoom`), the streak emitters (0x18: snow and wind streaks moving at the DS's speeds, `SqueakDriftInRoom`) and the far view's light (0x12: the far view's colors, BG palettes 6-13, dim until a Kirby reaches its box, then stepped up to full in 16 steps over its frames). And the Squeak Squad ability room's pedestals (`SQUEAK_PEDESTAL_OBJECT`, `KatAMExpansionSqueakLevels.cs`'s `BuildSqueakAbilityRoom()`: still, the stand drawn with `SQUEAK_PEDESTAL`'s look, the icon by an effect sprite of its own with `SQUEAK_PEDESTAL_ICON`'s, the DS drawing their pieces in different palettes; a Kirby with no ability touching one gets KatAM's ability in its template's `unk14`, as KatAM's ability statues do, `sub_080AA588`; `unk14` 0, an ability KatAM lacks, gives none). Stone shutters shake the screen as they change. The quake triggers and the rolling boulders shake the screen (`RequestScreenShake`); the thunder clouds play their `.mca` composites, flattened into plain animations by `KatAMExpansionSqueak.cs`'s `SqueakFlattenComposites`, and zap when struck. A grass tuft or barrel holding a chest (its template's `unk14` the chest's type and item, `unk16` its mark and number, `unk18` its own number and half flag) lets out the chest itself (`LetOutChest()`, a temporary template as the game's own spawned objects use); an empty barrel hides Kirby (Down in front of it: KatAM's cannon's hold, `sub_08051C40`, then `BarrelHold` keeps him at its bottom; a direction shakes it; A lets him out into KatAM's fall, `sub_08044EA8`, hopping up; 30 frames before the next).
- `rooms.c`: rooms bigger than KatAM's 1950 blocks (see Rooms below: Limits), the game's room loader and collision functions with bigger buffers. And the enemies' palettes past KatAM's 7 OBJ slots (8-14): KatAM's loader (`sub_0803DFAC`, redirected to `BigLoadPalette`) loads an 8th palette into slot 14 over the one there, its key kept out of the table, so the two take the slot back and forth as their objects spawn, one in the other's colors (the ported rooms put more kinds of enemies, items and Squeak Squad characters in a room than KatAM's 7); here it takes the first slot no live object draws with (`PaletteSlotsInUse()`: every task's object's sprite, ObjectBase's or EffectObject's, and every sprite in the OAM, the last frame's), KatAM's way only when all 7 are in use. KatAM's own rooms never go past 7.
- `collection.c`: the Collection Room as one grid that scrolls sideways (`KatAMExpansionCollection.cs`): KatAM's 10 x 4 cells as KatAM has them, then columns of the expansion's treasures (`gCollectionGrid.items`, up to 13 columns of 4: Squeak Squad's new spray paints in the first row, the Secret Map's pieces and the areas' maps in the rows below as they come, then Squeak Squad's Ability Scrolls: the first row's columns 6-13 after KatAM's, then the other rows' 9-13; the lattice's 23 columns are 6 pixels wider than BG2's 512, which wraps them onto its first, hidden left of the grid's box). The room reads its 40 cells (`gUnk_08386A50`: id, icon, text) through 20 literals in its code (from `sub_081434F8` to `sub_08145B64`), repointed to a copy in EWRAM (the bigger rooms' area, idle in the menus, `rooms.c`'s `gNidlBigArea`; one entry before the copy, read while the cursor's last cell is -1), which holds the 10 columns shown; at the grid's start it is KatAM's table, which the Sound Player's screens need (they look the songs' sheets up in it; its cell is in KatAM's first column, so they only open there). Hooks: the main menu's step that opens the room (`sub_081428A0`, a literal in `sub_0814288C`) is `GridRoomStart` first (the grid at its start); the step after the room's image is loaded (`sub_08143EF4`, a literal in `sub_08143E6C`) is `GridRoomBg` first; the room's frame (`sub_0814405C`, a literal in `sub_08143F88`) is `GridRoomFrame`; its task's end (`sub_08143664`, a literal in `sub_081434F8`) is `GridRoomEnd`. The cursor going past the last column shown (or the first) starts a slide (`GridShift`: the cells' entries, owned flags and sprites a column along, the column leaving kept aside until it has slid out, the column coming in made: KatAM's cells by the room's `sub_0814389C`, the others with their item's anim, variant and OBJ palette), 5 frames moving the sprites and BG2 (`GridPlace`), the room's own updates for a moved cursor called with its last cell -1, the keys hidden from the room meanwhile; past the grid's ends it goes round (`GridFill`), L and R move it a screen. The lattice is on BG2 (char base 2, screen base 0x1C, 512 x 256, priority 1: the image's first column, its columns 1-8 repeated, its last, the stripes clear), BG0's grid area (tile columns 1-28, rows 3-13) put back without it (the stripes all over the grid's box, from their pattern: rows of 8 pixels of colors 4, 6, 5 sliding a pixel a row, 64 rows to a period, checked against the image all round the grid; tiles at BG0's tile 0x180) per language image; window 0 is the grid's box (all layers), window 1 the grid's rows (BG0 and BG1 only: the sprites sliding past the box's sides are hidden), the rest without BG2; `GridRoomEnd` turns BG2 and the windows off. A new item's text: the room puts up its `roomText`, then `GridText` loads the item's composed one over it (LZ77 tiles and a 30 x 7 tilemap per language). Owned: KatAM's bits, then the expansion's (`GridOwned`: sprays bits 14-18, maps bits 10-25 of the big chests' field, the Secret Map's pieces bits 16-22 of the music field, the Ability Scrolls `rooms.c`'s `SqueakScrollOwned()`).
- The areas' maps (`chest.c`, `KatAMExpansionMaps.cs`): the NiDL levels' (maps 0-7) and the Squeak Squad worlds' (8-15), owned in `gTreasures.bigChestField`'s bits 10-25 (`squeak.h`'s `AREA_MAP_BIT`; KatAM's maps are bits 0-9). A chest holding one has its template's `unk16` `CHEST_MARK_MAP << 8 | map + 1` and one of KatAM's maps 1-9 as its item (`sub_0800AFC8`'s 0xB-0x13): `NidlChestOpen` (NiDL rooms' chests and Squeak Squad's alike) sets the area's bit before the game's opening, which gives KatAM's map and makes its popup (`sub_0800B414`'s task, `sub_0800B7A4`), then takes KatAM's map back unless it was owned and saves again, and finds the popup's task (`gTasks`, its `main` `sub_0800B7A4`, its chest this one): its `main` becomes `NidlMapPopup`, which each frame puts the area's shades (`gNidlMaps.colors`) into colors 8-10 of the chest's OBJ palette the popup draws with, and KatAM's black into its color 7 (the popup's outline: some of Squeak Squad's chests have red there) (an effect's sprite loads no palette of its own; the chest reloads its palette as it opens), then runs the game's. In the Collection Room (`collection.c`): the third and fourth rows after KatAM's columns, a map's cell id 0x200 + 10 + its number (`GridOwned` reads its bit), its icon KatAM's map in 4 tile sets and 4 OBJ palettes of 4 maps (a column pair each, in slots 14, 15, 4, 7: KatAM's sprays' 4 and 7 only for the columns shown once those are off the screen).
- Squeak Squad's Ability Scrolls (`chest.c`, `rooms.c`, `KatAMExpansionMaps.cs`): its 23 (treasures 28-50), in their own Squeak Squad chests (those a barrel or a tuft holds let out by it; the 5 the DS brings out only in the chase after the stage's treasure, out once that chase is on), owned in 4 bytes of the expansion's save area (`SqueakScrollOwned()`, `SqueakTakeScroll()`; `SQUEAK_SCROLLS`; that area is the EWRAM heap's end only with the bigger rooms in, `gNidlBigRooms`: without NiDL's or Squeak Squad's rooms no scroll is owned, and the Squeak Squad abilities' B, with Squeak Squad's enemies and no rooms, leaves KatAM's heap alone). A chest holding one has its template's `unk16` `CHEST_MARK_SCROLL << 8 | scroll + 1` and one of KatAM's maps as its item, opened as a Secret Map piece's: `NidlMapBefore` sets the scroll's bit, `NidlMapAfter` takes KatAM's map back; the popup is the DS's own icon of that scroll (`collection/skill`, `SQUEAK_SCROLL_ICON`: `NidlScrollPopup`, the game's popup drawn in the scroll's palette slot, flipped to face as the DS's). In the Collection Room (`collection.c`, the owner: "Scrolls are not being saved in the collection room"): a scroll's cell id is 0x4000 + its number (`GridOwned`'s category 0x40, `SqueakScrollOwned()`), its icon the same DS icon (unmirrored, its 16 x 16 from its drawing's top left) as the maps' anim's variant `ScrollIconVariant` + its number, the 23 icons' colors packed into 3 OBJ palettes (slots 5, 8, 9: KatAM's sprays', whose cells are in KatAM's columns 1-5, never on the screen with the scrolls'), its text "• NAME SCROLL" over the language's map text with nothing under it. What a scroll does: `scrolls.c` (`KatAMExpansionScrolls.cs` hooks KatAM's ability code into it, only with the Squeak Squad rooms), each scroll's upgrade as the DS's (its `HasScroll`, `0x02020AD8`, asked with the scroll's number by each ability's code; the owner's rule: no scroll, KatAM's ability as it is; with it, the DS ability's own quirks and its scroll's, a DS base quirk only where the upgrade doesn't change it). Done: Fire and Ice (the breath's state `sub_0805D268`, its pointer set as the breath starts, is `ScrollBreath`: with the scroll, each puff of breath goes 1.5 pixels a frame up with Up held, down with Down, the DS's `prmKirbyFire/Ice.kgb` words 3 and 4, at once; KatAM's fire breath bends less, a step a frame, and its ice breath not at all); Spark (KatAM's Spark as it is, plus the DS's
charge: D-pad taps charge it, 16 each, falling 1 a frame, past 128 level 1 (the DS's lightning about Kirby) and with the scroll past 128
again level 2 (its lightning ball about him, a hit box of strength 1 every 3 frames), falling back to level 1 when the charge runs out;
B by level: 0 KatAM's spark, 1 and 2 the DS's 30-frame burst (its rings, hit boxes of strength 4 and 6 every frame, KatAM's attack held
that long, then KatAM's spark while B is held), 2 with forward held KatAM's spark and the ball flying off forward 6 pixels a frame
through everything (strength 3, then 1 every 3 frames); a glint on Kirby every 61 frames; the DS's sounds. The charge is a task per
Kirby, its number in his ability byte (`SPARK_SLOT`, Mike's and Hi-Jump's), worked each frame after his input is read
(`ScrollKirbyInput`: `sub_0803F324`, detoured); his attack's start (`sub_08074AB0`, detoured: `ScrollSparkAttack`) takes the level; a
hit ends it (`NidlKirbyHit` → `ScrollSparkHit`). Strengths: KatAM's spark's 3 for the DS's 20, so 25 is 4, 40 is 6, 5 is 1); Beam (the DS's Beam is its whip only; with the scroll
KatAM's whip, its hit box `sub_0807CFE4` and chain `sub_0807D2D0`, `sub_0807D4B0`, all detoured, from its attack state only, is the
DS's upgraded whip: its look, `beam_ext.mca`'s composite 1, sweeping from higher above Kirby to lower in front, its hit boxes on their
frames, strength 4 for the DS's 20, its sound, its 44 frames, KatAM's attack held that long; KatAM's whip is 35); Tornado (with the
scroll KatAM's tornado takes the element of the first fire or electric attack or enemy it meets in its start-up or spin: what is in
KatAM's collision lists that frame, `gUnk_02022F50`, with the fire (0x100) or electric (0x400) hit kind, not a Kirby's, inside its box;
a piece made as the attack starts (`sub_0807A10C`, detoured: `ScrollTornadoStart`) run just before KatAM's collision pass reads it.
KatAM's attack then is that element's: its kind bit, the DS's bigger box, strength 3 for the DS's 25; with the DS's sound `0x93` and
its element tornado, `tornado_ext.mca`'s composite 8 (fire) or 10 (electric), its body and sparks, drawn in place of KatAM's own
(Kirby's sprite hidden with its `unk8` bit `0x80000`, which KatAM's code never sets) till the spin ends. Not water: KatAM's tornado
stops in water); Parasol (KatAM's twirl, B held after the swing: anims 104, 105, its attack `sub_08084188`, detoured:
`ScrollParasolTwirl`; with the scroll, on land, KatAM's own twirl and Kirby are drawn as they are, with the DS's stars about him,
`parasol_ext.mca`'s composite 2 without its Kirby, made one animation by `KatAMExpansionSqueak.cs`'s `SqueakFlatten`; the DS's boxes
in place of KatAM's, one frame in three, 13 to 33-63 pixels in front, 12 under his feet to 30 over them, strength 1 as KatAM's
twirl's; the DS's sound `0xA3` at its start and every 37 frames; not in water, the DS's water parasol a file of its own); Sleep
(while he sleeps, `sub_08064370`'s anim 52, worked after his input's read as Spark's charge: a hit point each 18 frames up to his
most, the DS's `prmKirbySleep.kgb` 10 a frame toward 180; A or B pressed plays his sleep 2 frames further, the DS's cut, sound `0xA9`;
the healing's count in his ability byte, `SLEEP_HEAL`, Mike's and Spark's); Cutter (KatAM's thrown cutter, `sub_08078EFC`, detoured:
`ScrollCutterThrow`; with the scroll the new cutter, task `sub_08070580`, flies as KatAM's, drawn as the DS's big blue blade with
its sparkles, `cutter_ext`'s anims 25 and 27, KatAM's own not drawn (flag `0x400`), its box the DS's, -20 to 19 across, -14 to 13,
its strength KatAM's 4); Laser (KatAM's laser, `sub_0806A520`, its shot state `sub_08063F74` held past its end while B is held after
the shot, worked after his input's read as Spark's charge: the DS's charge, its sparkles about him, sound `0x138` every 5 frames, its orb
behind him once charged, 30 frames on; let go charged, its sparkles for 6 frames, then KatAM's own shot, `sub_0807BCE0`, at strength 3
for KatAM's 2, on through enemies as the DS's (its hit callback, `ChargedShotHit`), drawn as the DS's thicker bolt, `laser_ext`'s 42-44 as it goes across, up or down, with the DS's flash and ring for 20
frames; let go before, the laser ends; the charge's piece in his ability byte, `LASER_SLOT`, as Spark's); UFO (the DS's star circling UFO Kirby, `ufo_ext`'s 36, made
from his input each frame while he has none, its piece in `UFO_SLOT`, his ability byte: round his feet at 22 pixels, its two angles 4 and
3 degrees on a frame, in front of him or behind as the DS's (the sprite's order, `SCROLL_BEHIND_KIRBYS`); its box every other frame,
strength 2 for KatAM's UFO laser's 4, its hit's kind the UFO laser's; gone with the DS's star burst, `SQUEAK_FX_STARS`, when he isn't UFO Kirby, leaves the
room or is out of play); Hi-Jump (the DS's orange glow about Hi-Jump Kirby, `highjump_ext`'s 37, behind the Kirbys while he rises,
`HiJumpRiseNow` in `abilities/hi_jump.c`; the scroll's other quirk, the rise going on through what it hits, KatAM's Hi-Jump has already);
Wheel (KatAM's rolling wheel, its attack task `sub_0807A4F4`, takes the element of a fire or electric attack or enemy that meets it,
`ElementTouch` as the Tornado's: the kind bit added to its attack, the DS's fire or electric wheel, `wheel_ext`'s 31, 37, 38 or 33, 41,
42, drawn in place of Kirby and KatAM's disc behind the wheel, `sub_0809104C`'s effect, to the end of the roll; a piece found or made
from his input each frame); Cupid (KatAM's shot, `sub_080680C0`, detoured: the DS's volley, three of KatAM's arrows from the DS's
places, kept straight on at the DS's 6 pixels a frame by `CupidArrowMain`, KatAM's strength, one volley a shot); Bomb (KatAM's bomb,
`sub_08082380`, and blast, `sub_08074240`, detoured: a bomb meeting a fire, ice or electric attack or enemy gives his bombs that
element, `BOMB_ELEMENT` in his ability byte, till he loses Bomb; ice and spark bombs drawn as the DS's, `bomb_ice_ext`'s and
`bomb_spark_ext`'s 30; the blast's kind that element's); Sword (a cut of Sword Kirby's, his sword `sub_080753CC`, the other cut
tasks and the slide `sub_080714A0`, meeting a fire, ice or electric attack or enemy, `ElementTouchObj` with the cut's hit box `unk3C`-`unk3F`,
gives his sword that element, `SWORD_ELEMENT` in his ability byte, till he loses Sword: every cut of his then carries its kind bit and his
sword palette, 4 + his number, gets the DS's element sword's colors 2-14 each frame, `gSwordColors`, in `gObjPalette` and in `gUnk_02022320`,
the palette KatAM's palette effects put back each frame; a piece found or made from his input each frame); Hammer (B on the ground, `sub_080618C0`
detoured, out of water, not dashing nor with Up held: the DS's charge, `HammerCharge`, let go before 60 frames KatAM's own swing, at 60 the
DS's second level and then its giant swing and landing, `HammerSwing`, `HammerLand`; Kirby drawn as the DS's frames, his body in his own
palette, `SqueakEffect`'s `OnlyPalettes`, KatAM's sprite and hammer hidden; one hit a swing, KatAM's hammer's kind, strength 12); Fighter
(the charge held to the DS's 120 frames, counted by a `FIGHTER_CHARGE` piece: its aura, `sub_08094540`, and its blast, `sub_08065A0C`, both
detoured, run as with him at 1 HP, KatAM's own biggest blast and its aura's look and sound, his HP put back); Throw (the grab, `sub_0807A7E8`
detoured: a `THROW_FLASH` piece shows the DS's element flash, `throw_ext.mca`'s composite by the held enemy's element, on it for 10 frames);
Magic (B, `sub_0806898C` detoured, `ScrollMagicAttack`, out of water: the DS's three throws, Up held the jack-in-the-box, dashing three
cards, else three doves, pieces of their own, `MAGIC_DOVE`, `MAGIC_CARD`; Kirby drawn as the DS's frames by a `MAGIC_BODY` piece; B held
through the move starts the DS's roulette: KatAM's freeze, `sub_08033540`, Kirby kept going by his flag 0x2000, the background darkened by
KatAM's blend, the thought's icon going round, B stopping it; its outcomes KatAM's Magic's own: `sub_08083040`'s hit turning what's on the
screen into food or 1UPs, `MAGIC_SWEEP`, the random ability, `sub_0805C11C`, the candy's invincibility, `sub_0808324C`, and the DS's sun,
`MAGIC_STAR` star bursts and a hit on every enemy on the screen; then the ability used up but for the "?". Without the scroll, KatAM's Magic).
- The Secret Map's pieces (`chest.c`, `KatAMExpansionMaps.cs`): Squeak Squad's 7 (treasures 93-99), in their own Squeak Squad chests, owned in `gTreasures.musicPlayerAndSheetsField`'s bits 16-22 (`squeak.h`'s `SECRET_MAP_BIT`; world slot `SECRET_MAP_SLOT`, 0x1C, read as whether all 7 are owned: `rooms.c`'s `BigGetStateSlot`, the Squeak Squad hub's door to its ability room; KatAM's Sound Player and sheets are bits 0-10, and `NumMusicPlayerAndSheetsCollected` counts only those); `SecretMapWhole()` is true once all 7 are owned (for the Squeak Squad hub's door to the Squeak Squad ability room, `KatAMExpansionSqueakLevels.cs`'s `BuildSqueakAbilityRoom()`). A chest holding one has its template's `unk16` `CHEST_MARK_PIECE << 8 | piece + 1` and one of KatAM's maps as its item, opened as an area's map's (above): `NidlMapBefore` sets the piece's bit, `NidlMapAfter` takes KatAM's map back and colors the popup with `gNidlMaps.colors`' 17th entry (the parchment), and `NidlPiecePopupStart` makes the popup the piece's own icon, as the Collection Room shows it (the owner: "The secret map pieces are showing up on chests as vanilla map icons instead of their actual icons in the Collection Room"): the maps' anim (`gNidlMaps.iconAnim`) at variant `gNidlMaps.piecePopup` + the piece (`KatAMExpansionMaps.cs`' `PiecePopupVariant`: the piece's icon with its colors 1, 4-6 moved to the popup's 7-10, which `NidlMapPopup` fills), left as KatAM's map without a Squeak Squad ROM. In the Collection Room: the second row after KatAM's columns, a piece's cell id 0x2000 + its number (`GridOwned`'s category 0x20), its icon the maps' anim's variant 16 + its number, Squeak Squad's own piece (`collection/map`'s cells 0-6, as the DS draws them) shrunk to fit 14 x 14 with a black outline, its 3 shades (the pieces' pixels' brightness in 3 groups) in colors 4-6 of the maps' 4 OBJ palettes; its text "• SECRET MAP PIECE" over the language's map text. Only with the Squeak Squad rooms.
- Squeak Squad's spray paints KatAM doesn't have (SHADOW, IVORY, CITRUS, WHITE, LAVENDER; `KatAMExpansionSprays.cs`, `chest.c`). They are Kirby colors 16-20: anim 0x15's (Kirby's palette) and anim 0x1A0's (the UFO's) variant lists are copied with theirs after KatAM's 16, and the tables a color is read from get rows for them (`gUnk_082EB4B4` and the Collection Room's `gUnk_08386AF0`, one shared table of anim 0x15 and the color; the continue palettes `gUnk_0834BB20`, 32 bytes a color, the spray's body and feet in KatAM's sprays' places), their literals repointed (2, 2, 6 and 4 of them, checked). Owned in `gTreasures.sprayPaintField`'s bits 14-18 (`NumSprayPaintsCollected` counts bits 0-13: the completion stays KatAM's); a chest holding one (`chest.c`'s `NidlChestOpen`: its template's `unk16` the spray's number + 1) sets that bit before the game's opening, which gives and shows its item (KatAM's spray of the closest color: the can that rises), then takes KatAM's spray back unless it was owned, and saves again. In the Collection Room's grid (`collection.c`) the first row after KatAM's columns: a spray's cell id is 0x111 + its number, the room taking (u8)id - 1 as its color; its icon KatAM's spray can in its colors (a new anim, two cans to an OBJ palette, 12-14: the room's free ones); its text "SPRAY PAINT : NAME", made by `KatAMExpansionSprays.cs`: the language's CHALK text with its name cleared, the runs of pixels all 14 KatAM spray texts share before it kept, and the name in KatAM's letters, taken from the English texts, or, where the ROM has none (Japan), Squeak Squad's name graphic. Only with the Squeak Squad rooms (their chests).
- `../KatAMExpansionRooms.cs`: NiDL's rooms (see Rooms below).
- `debug/fly.lua`: a testing aid for mGBA (not in the ROM; the owner: "a way to move around as kirby so kirby ignores collisions and floats at fast speeds"): Kirby goes where the held D-pad takes him, 8 pixels a frame, through walls and floors, his HP kept full, so the levels can be flown through and checked.

## What `KatAMExpansion.AddEnemies()` writes into the KatAM ROM

The ROM is first padded from 16 MB to 32 MB with `0xFF` (the most the GBA maps: `0x08000000`-`0x09FFFFFF`, which emulators and flash carts load like any other ROM; the game saves to SRAM, so nothing else depends on its size). Everything new goes in that second half, so the expansion has 16 MB for its enemies and leaves the game's own free space to the randomizer. Frames whose tiles are the same (byte for byte) share one copy of them (`AddAnim`'s `sharedFrameTiles`: the ported characters repeat many frames across their animations), which keeps the European build, the biggest, about 850 KB under the 32 MB (with the Spark and Beam scrolls' effects).


| Where | What |
|---|---|
| `0x1000000` | Code, then the converted graphics, animations, names and the extended sprite tables |
| `gSpriteTables` (`0x3B909C`) | `anims`, `attrs` and `oamData` copied with more entries: anim `939` is the hurt frame, `940` on are the enemies (one variant per frame) |
| `gSpawnFuncTable1[0x31]` | `CreateNidlEnemy` |
| Object parameters (`0x351648 + 0x31 * 0x18`) | Hurt frames 0 and 1, Waddle Dee's score and HP, no ability, no palette key, VRAM for the biggest frame plus the hurt frame, `NidlStart`, the shared animations |
| `0x08034ED2`, `0x08035B34`, `0x080857EE` | The patches above |

Each enemy's HP is its own (`sNidlEnemies`, set at spawn), as KatAM's enemies': 4 for the ones that give an ability (as KatAM's Sword Knight, Hot Head or Sparky), 2 for the others. NiDL's are 1 or 2, so anything killed them in one hit.

Graphics come from the player's NiDL ROM: each enemy's descriptor (`0x0873EEA0 + (type - 8) * 4`: palette, LZ77 tiles) and frame table (8-byte OAM records). Nothing from NiDL is shipped.

## Enemies

| Enemy | NiDL type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Broom Hatter | `0x0B` | All 3 NiDL variants: 0 sweeps forward twice, back and forth, or away from Kirby, at random; 1 the same with its own sweep timing, moving once it has landed; 2 sweeps in place. Frames 4-7 with the broom dust (8-10, NiDL palette 15, drawn with the unused color 6) on top; hurt frame 3. Water defeats it, as in NiDL |
| Cappy | `0x10` | Hops forward (0.25), its sprite turning around on every landing, facing Kirby again every 40 hops; frame 4, hurt frame 3. Variant 1 is the capless Cappy (frames 4 and 6 of its own frame table, alternating on every landing); variant 2 hops in place. Inhaled, variant 0 loses its cap like in NiDL: Kirby swallows just the cap (NiDL frame 1) and a capless Cappy springs out backwards and hops on (watched from the object's pre-callback, `unk7C`, which the engine keeps calling while it's inhaled; the capless one carries its own copy of the template). Water defeats it, as in NiDL |
| Twizzy | `0x18` | All 10 NiDL variants. Flying (0-4, 8): 0 swoops forward towards Kirby's height, then climbs away; 1 swoops forever, keeping to Kirby's height; 2 ambushes from the top of the room and loops; 3 flies diagonally bouncing off walls, floors and ceilings; 4 chases Kirby for a while, then flies off; 8 bobs in place. On the ground (5-7, 9): 5 takes off when Kirby comes near and dashes at it; 6 hops up and flutters down; 7 hops around and takes off to chase Kirby when it's near; 9 just sits. Water doesn't affect it, as in NiDL. Frames 4-5 (flapping), 6 (sitting); hurt frame 3 |
| Bubbles | `0x1E` | Variant 0 bounces forward (1.0) at random heights, spinning, squashing on floors, walls (turning around) and ceilings; variant 1 bounces in place. Water defeats variant 0 but not variant 1, as in NiDL. Gives Ball. Frames 4-21; hurt frame 0 |
| Slippy | `0x22` | On land it sits facing Kirby, looks around, and jumps low or high, in place, forward or turning around, chosen at random like NiDL; jumping into water it sinks and swims. Put in water it swims: drifts, paddles and kicks towards Kirby (straight, the other way or diagonally, picked at random), and leaps out at the surface. Variant 1 just sits facing Kirby. Variant 0 joins the underwater pool too. Frames 4, 5, 6, 8, 7 (swimming), 9 (paddling); hurt frame 3 |
| Kabu | `0x15` | All 4 NiDL variants: 0 sits, spins 1-3 times, then jumps at Kirby (5 jumps picked by weight, never the same twice); 1 rocks until Kirby is near, then vanishes in a flicker, reappears next to Kirby, drops and slides at it; 2 glides back and forth to points in front of and behind Kirby, then hops; 3 spins in place. Frames 4-12; hurt frame 3 |
| Coconut | `0x17` | Variants 0 and 1 hang (facing right or left) until Kirby passes under them, then fall tumbling and explode on landing (KatAM's small explosion, like Foley's); variant 2 rocks on the ground. Frames 4-15; hurt frame 3 |
| Coner | `0x29` | Faces Kirby and scuttles in bursts (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, stopped); variant 1 scuttles in place. In water it glides down (0.25 forward and down) and scuttles along the bottom, and joins the underwater pool. Frames 4-6; hurt frame 3 |
| Starman | `0x1F` | All 4 NiDL variants: 0 walks up to Kirby, flies off the top of the screen steering towards it, follows Kirby hidden up there, dives onto it and bounces, and flies away after the second dive; 1 walks and jumps now and then; 2 flies forward bobbing, then rises away (flying pool); 3 walks in place. Frames 4-20; hurt frame 3. Water defeats it, as in NiDL. Gives Hi-Jump |
| Needlous | `0x2C` | Walks towards Kirby, and when Kirby stays level with it (or after a while) hops, curls into a spiky ball, bounces and rolls fast for a second, rolling back from walls; one time in 4 it starts walking, else curled. Variant 1 walks in place. Frames 4-16 (15 and 16 are NiDL's white spin blur); hurt frame 3. Water defeats it, as in NiDL. Gives Needle |
| Blade Knight | `0x25` | All 3 NiDL variants: 0 walks up to Kirby, raises or lowers its sword and slashes, lunging (sometimes slashing back once), then backs off; 1 walks in place; 2 stands guard facing left and slashes when Kirby comes near. Variant 0 also slashes at Kirby's attacks and thrown things that come near (anything whose parent is a Kirby, found walking the task list). The slash's hitbox is KatAM's Sword Knight's, its sound NiDL's (`0xC4`). Gives Sword. Frames 4-16; hurt frame 3. Water defeats it, as in NiDL |
| Chilly | `0x0E` | Both NiDL variants: 0 wiggles turning around until it faces Kirby, lunges at it 4 times (3.0 slowing down), then crouches shaking and freezes: a rotating ring of KatAM's Pengy's ice bursts out of it for 240 frames (NiDL's icy ring uses shared effect graphics; Pengy's ice hurts the same way; NiDL's freeze sound `0xB9` every 5 frames). Starts with any of these at random. 1 lunges in place. Gives Freeze. Water defeats variant 0, not 1, as in NiDL. Frames 4-11; hurt frame 3 |
| Cool Spook | `0x13` | Both NiDL variants (flying pool): 0 flies left at 0.5 bobbing, through walls and water (NiDL's skips every terrain check); 1 bobs in place facing Kirby. Gives Light. Frames 4-7; hurt frame 3 |
| Poppy Bros. Jr. | `0x26`-`0x28` | NiDL's three types as one enemy (they share graphics): 0 hops (0.5 forward, low hops; every other hop it turns around one time in 3, for one hop); 1 jumps in at once (2.0 straight up, untouchable for 30 frames or till it lands: NiDL's `obj+0x30`), then hops; 2 hops in place; 3 and 4 roll an apple or a Maxim Tomato (0.5), spinning, with Poppy Bros. Jr. hopping on top of it (an effect object drawing the enemy's own frames); 5 and 6 the same in place. Inhaled, the apple or tomato comes off and Kirby swallows Poppy Bros. Jr.; it bounces away from Kirby twice (2.0 then 1.0 up, NiDL's heights frame by frame), untouchable while it bounces (Kirby goes through it and can't inhale it, as in NiDL), then stops: the apple lies there, an enemy again (it hurts, is beaten, and is inhaled: `NidlInhaler()`, nidl.c, then KatAM's `sub_0809C380`, as KatAM's own inhale never caught it), and the tomato becomes KatAM's Maxim Tomato where it stopped (NiDL's makes its Maxim Tomato item, type `0x46`; the port took it for a bomb and blew it up). Defeated, the rider goes as any enemy does and Poppy Bros. Jr. jumps off (variant 1). Water defeats them, as in NiDL. Frames 4-7, the apple's 9-16 and the tomato's 9-16 (drawn with NiDL's shared object palette 15, read from the NiDL ROM: a second palette slot, key anim \| 0x2000, while the rider keeps Poppy Bros. Jr.'s); hurt frame 3. Not ported: walking instead of hopping in the rooms NiDL marks for it |
| Gip | `0x2F` | Both NiDL variants: 0 walks towards Kirby (turning its back to it when it's close) and climbs walls it walks into; on a wall it clings, then flaps up, glides down, drops off or shakes and jumps at Kirby (weighted by where Kirby is), steps onto the top or drops off the bottom when the wall ends, slides down the side of ledges it walks off, and every 120 frames of walking throws a spinning star (a KatAM attack object drawing the enemy's own frames). Starting next to a wall it clings to it. 1 walks in place. Water defeats it, as in NiDL. Frames 4-16 and the star's 4-7; hurt frame 3. The throw plays NiDL's sound (`0xE9`) |

## Mini-bosses

Ported mini-bosses are object types `0x40`-`0x42`; the behavior byte's high nibble picks the mini-boss (`NIDL_POPPY_BROS_SR`... in `nidl.h`), the low nibble its variant. An object's frame index is a byte, and the mini-bosses have more than 256 frames together, so they're spread over the three types, each with its own `AnimInfo` table (`0x40`: Poppy Bros. Sr., Mr. Tick-Tock, Grand Wheelie; `0x41`: Bugzzy, Fire Lion; `0x42`: the Meta-Knights); a mini-boss created with the other type's number becomes that type's first. KatAM's own Mr. Frosty, Bonkers and Phan Phan stay as they are (NiDL's are the same mini-bosses).

- **The fight** starts like KatAM's mini-bosses' (Mr. Frosty, `0x080CB7E8`): the mini-boss waits facing Kirby, untouchable, until Kirby is in the template's area (`unk1A`-`unk20`, the randomizer keeps the vanilla mini-boss's, or makes it the whole room for an enemy's spot, so the camera doesn't lock there), then the camera stays in that area (`Macro_081003EC`, which also copies its bounds into `unkA4`-`unkAA`; every frame `NidlUpdate` keeps the mini-boss's x inside them with `ObjXSomething`, as KatAM's mini-bosses do, and the Meta-Knights get Meta Knight's), the HP bar shows and the mini-boss music plays (`Macro_08100F18`; in a room with NiDL's own music, it keeps that: `NidlFightMusic()`). Defeat is KatAM's (the engine handles every type `0x38`-`0x52` the same way: it flies off, shows the frame after its second hurt frame, and explodes); its blink animation table (`gUnk_08D610B4`, by type - `0x38`) only had types `0x38`-`0x3F`, so it's copied with entries for `0x40`-`0x42` (none) and its one use points at the copy.
- **Shared per type**, as for the enemies: one `AnimInfo` table (its own, after the enemies'), each mini-boss's own palettes and anim ID, a 64x96 hurt frame (a 64x64 and a 64x32 piece, 96 tiles at the end of the object's VRAM, origin at its center; anim indexes 0, 1 and 2; type `0x42`'s is 32x64, 32 tiles, as several knights are out at once). A mini-boss can have several hurt frames (`NidlObject.hurt` picks one: each knight its own). A mini-boss too tall for it (Fire Lion) has its hurt frame and every frame drawn some pixels lower (`hurtShift`), and its hitbox and floor box as much lower (the engine resets `objBase55` after every frame it draws, so the sprite can't be moved back up), its own HP bar name. Each mini-boss starts on the floor under the cell NiDL puts it in (`NidlOnFloor`, nidl.h: its box's bottom on that cell's bottom; the enemies' snap, to the cell edge under the box's bottom, put Bugzzy, Mr. Tick-Tock and Fire Lion, whose boxes reach past 23 pixels, a cell lower: in the ground, or through a floor a cell thick, as in rooms 0x2EF and 0x135). Every ported object's task ends in `NidlDestroy`, which empties the HP bar and lets go of the object if the HUD shows it (the HUD only lets go once the object's flags say it's gone, `0x0803533C`, and by then its memory can hold another object: the bar stayed after the enemy died). `NidlMinibossStart` (the type's start, after a hit too) sets the mini-boss's ability and calls its own start once the fight has started.
- **Graphics**: NiDL's mini-boss descriptors are at `0x0873F0E4 + index * 4` (USA; `region.Descriptors + 0x244` in every region; index = NiDL type - `0x31`), in the enemies' format, often with two palettes: frames drawn with the second load it under the anim ID | `0x2000`. Frame tables are the `0x0875xxxx` literal each spawn function stores at `obj+0x38`. Some frames are *streamed* (Mr. Tick-Tock's body): the table entry points to 12 bytes instead of OAM records, pointers to the frame's OAM records, its own palette (`u16 0x20`, then 16 colors) and its own tiles, which NiDL uploads with the frame (rows of VRAM, 32 tiles each: `u16` byte count and the tiles, until `0xFFFF`); `KatAMExpansion.PartPieces` reads both kinds.
- **Object types `0x40`-`0x42`'s parameters** are Mr. Frosty's (score, HP), with the hurt frames, no palette key, VRAM for the type's biggest frame plus the hurt frame, `NidlMinibossStart` and the type's animations; `gSpawnFuncTable1[0x40]`-`[0x42]` are `CreateNidlMiniboss`.
- **HP bar**: KatAM's fill is HP times `gUnk_08351458[type - 0x38][players - 1]` (the table right before `gUnk_08351648`), >> 8 (64 is full), with a placeholder (8192) for types `0x40`-`0x44` that made the bar garbage; the ported mini-bosses' is `NidlHpBarFill`'s, from their own HP (`gNidlMinibossHp`: 30, Mr. Frosty's), see the table above.
- **Projectiles** (Poppy Bros. Sr.'s bombs, Mr. Tick-Tock's notes, Fire Lion's flames, the knights' axes, javelins and tridents) are attack objects, which KatAM's inhale doesn't take (it inhales objects only). `NidlInhaled()` (nidl.c) inhales them as KatAM's enemies are (`object.c`'s `0x0809C380` and `0x0809C48C`): once one is in a Kirby's inhale (KatAM's box, `0x08071FC0`: 4 to 48 pixels ahead, 18 up to 16 down, while he inhales: animations `0x1A`, `0x1B`, `0x6A`, `0x6B`) it's pulled in, harmless, faster and faster, and becomes a mouthful that gives nothing (`NidlSwallow`), for him to spit back as a star (what he fights the mini-boss with when he has no ability); if he stops inhaling first, it's gone in a puff. Not inhaled: Mr. Tick-Tock's rings (they're around it as it rings) and the Mace Knight's mace (on its chain).
- **Helpers** (the CPU Kirbys): KatAM's helper AI picks what to go for from the room's objects only (`sub_08010590`), and a helper with no ability hurts a mini-boss only with what he inhales and spits back (Mr. Frosty's ice cubes are objects), so the Normal helpers did nothing against the ported mini-bosses, whose projectiles are attack objects. The pick is detoured to `NidlHelperTarget()` (`nidl_miniboss.c`; the original runs from its trampoline, `gNidlHelperTargetOriginal`): when a helper with no ability and nothing in his mouth would go for a ported mini-boss, he goes for the nearest of the projectiles above within his reach instead (the tasks running `PoppyBrosSrBombMain`, `MrTickTockNoteMain`, `FireLionFlameMain` or `MetaKnightsWeaponMain`, not caught or going), and KatAM's AI does the rest: he inhales it, and with his mouth full spits it back at the mini-boss (his pick again). Bugzzy's and Grand Wheelie's fights were already fine (the helpers inhale the enemies they call), and so were the Meta-Knights' (the knights are inhaled alive).
- **Map tracker**: `KatAMExpansion.WriteMapTable` writes the last `0x100` bytes of the ROM: `NIDLMAP` and a version byte (1), then per mini-boss its type, index, name and an icon (its `Icon` frames, as 16-bit colors), which the map tracker reads to tell an expanded ROM and show these mini-bosses (`KatAM Map Tracker/Core/RomMap.cs`).
- **Randomizer**: with "Include Mini-Bosses" (ROM Expansion tab), types `0x40`-`0x42` join the mini-boss pool once per ported mini-boss of theirs, the behavior picking a mini-boss and then one of its variants.

| Mini-boss | NiDL type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Meta-Knights | `0x39`, `0x3A` | Meta Knight (the template's object, unseen and untouchable, in NiDL he only swoops by at the start) calls in his knights from one of NiDL's line-ups (variants 0-4: `0x08745CFC`'s 0, 4, 8, 12 and 16): four lists side by side, each calling its next knight once the last one is gone, waiting 30-240 frames, or warping one in (NiDL's warp effect is drawn from its common tiles, so KatAM's puff of smoke here). The knights are type `0x42` objects too, with their own graphics, palette (each knight's loaded under the anim ID \| kind << 13) and hurt frame, dying like KatAM's enemies (`base.unkC & 0x800`, so not as mini-bosses): Axe Knight backs off, slashes (dashing) and throws its axe, jumping before or after; Javelin Knight hops about on its jet pack, throws javelins or blasts off, or jets back and forth; Mace Knight twirls its mace and throws it along the floor (from its spot, stepping forward, or running with it); Trident Knight walks, backs off, throws its trident (or jumps and throws, or hops at Kirby), or stands and throws at 4 speeds. Meta Knight's HP (the bar) is what's left of them (6 each, the ones to come too), out of 30; the bar goes to any mini-boss that's hit, so he takes it back every frame. Once all are gone he goes down where the last one did (KatAM's defeat, `sub_0809D060`, with that knight's hurt frame). Frames: Axe Knight's 0-34, Javelin Knight's 0-57, Mace Knight's 0-38, Trident Knight's 0-62 (one descriptor each, mini-boss descriptors 9-12); hurt frames: each one's 3. Gives no ability (as in NiDL). The knights are inhaled alive, as NiDL's (an enemy's weight, 1, not a mini-boss's 3, its not-inhaled bit `0x20` and `0x108000` off: what Kirby fights the others with when he has no ability; they give none), and while one blinks in (NiDL's fade, 18 frames) it's untouchable (`flags` `0x200`: no hit, no inhale), as NiDL's; their axes, javelins and tridents are inhaled too (see **Projectiles**; the mace stays on its knight's chain) |
| Fire Lion | `0x37` | Idles, then roars (3 hops in place), rolls into a fireball at Kirby (3.0 for 48 frames), pounces at it (landing in a burst of flames), walks up and breathes fire 3 times, or walks up and blazes; after each attack it hops back. Timings, heights and speeds traced. Its flames are attack objects drawing its fire bits (52-56). Its frames (0-51) are streamed frames; hurt frame 0, drawn 12 pixels lower. Gives Burning |
| Bugzzy | `0x36` | Walks at Kirby, then charges (backs up, raises its pincers, dashes at 2.25), jumps at it (3.75 up, 1.0 forward) or calls for help (a Bronto Burt or a Waddle Doo in turn, KatAM's own; NiDL types `0x14` and `0x11`). Reaching Kirby in the dash or landing on it, it grabs it and suplexes it: lifts it over its head jumping back (1.5, then 0.5 on its back) and slams it behind itself, which hurts Kirby. Kirby is held like KatAM's Phan Phan holds it: `kirby->unk110` points at a table of positions and poses relative to the holder, which Kirby restarts whenever the holder's frame changes, so Bugzzy points it at the entry for each of its frames; hit, it lets Kirby go. Its frames (0-63) are streamed frames; hurt frame 3. Gives Backdrop |
| Grand Wheelie | `0x33` | Revs in place (rolling slowly or rocking on its tire), then rolls at Kirby (after 45 frames revving; 1.5, or 2.625, 0.9, 1.57 for variants 1-3), or hops at it one time in 4. Rolling at Kirby, within 63 pixels it may skid and turn back (one time in 3); past Kirby it skids and rolls back; a wall knocks it back with a quake, tumbling and bouncing twice. Every 4th time it hops twice spinning and spits out a small Wheelie (KatAM's own Wheelie enemy). Its frames (0-55) are streamed frames, played with NiDL's animation tables; hurt frame 35. Gives Wheel |
| Mr. Tick-Tock | `0x35` | Idles, then decides (every 4th time a hop in place, one forward and its alarm; else by where Kirby is, more than 128 pixels away or not, 64 above or below or not: hops, backing off, a pounce or a charge, from NiDL's 16-entry tables). The charge (back up, then forward) turns into a run and a slide ringing its alarm when Kirby is more than 32 pixels away, or a hop and ringing in place; ringing, rings spread around it (an attack object drawing its rings, 17-22); the alarm after the jumps throws 4 notes (attack objects falling in an arc at one of 4 speeds, hopping once where they land). Variant 1 walks and charges faster. Its body frames (0-16) are streamed frames; frames 0-28; hurt frame 13. Gives Mike |
| Poppy Bros. Sr. | `0x32` | Hops at Kirby (back when Kirby is within 39 pixels, then the other way every hop), 4, 2, 2 or 2 hops (variant 1: 4, 6, 2 or 0); jumps straight up and throws a bomb from over its head near the top (holding it longer when Kirby is within 63 pixels), the bomb bouncing once and exploding (KatAM's small explosion); 2 hops; a jump forward and one back; a long jump forward and back (or straight up when Kirby is far); and again. NiDL draws its head and its hands as separate objects: here every body frame (4-9) is drawn with every head frame (12-19, the head's own animation, `0x08743A00`), and the bomb it holds is an effect object. Frames: the 48 body and head ones, the hands (20-25), the bomb (26-30, the second palette); hurt frame 10. Gives Crash |

## Bosses

Ported bosses are object type `0x43` (and `0x44` once `0x43`'s 256 frames are full: Paint Roller, Heavy Mole, King Dedede and both of Nightmare's, under 256 frames together: King Dedede keeps only the frames he uses; `OBJ_EMPTY_43`, which KatAM already treats as a boss everywhere: `ObjType43To4D`, the boss music `gUnk_08352D80[type - 0x38]`, a hit only flashes it, and its defeat: it freezes, explodes and goes, `sub_08088528`, then runs `gUnk_08350E58[type - 0x43]`, KatAM's bosses' shard handlers, NULL for `0x43` until `KatAMExpansion.cs` makes it `NidlBossDefeat`). The behavior byte's high nibble picks the boss (`NIDL_WHISPY_WOODS`... in `nidl.h`), its low nibble is the mirror shard it leaves: its NiDL level's (0-7). The graphics are built as the mini-bosses' (one `AnimInfo` table for the type, the 64x96 hurt frame), the type's parameters are Kracko's (`0x45`) with the bosses' functions. Their HP is KatAM's bosses' by the number of players, as `gUnk_08351530` has it (`nidl_boss.c`, `NidlBossHp()`): from NiDL's 60 (a spit star takes 10 of it: 6 stars) to Kracko's and King Golem's 60, 80, 100 and 110 for 1-4 players (`gNidlBossHp`, which the HP options write), its HP bar's fill worked out from it (`NidlHpBarFill`). Mr. Shine and Mr. Bright have NiDL's 30 each on that bar's 60 (each hit takes twice its HP: 3 stars each, as NiDL's); Heavy Mole and Paint Roller have 40's worth, KatAM's bigger mini-bosses' (the owner: too much HP), on that bar's 60: a hit takes half again its HP, rounded up (`NidlBossHitMore()`: a spit star's 10 takes 15, 4 stars; a sword's 1 takes 2), and one that would take them out starts KatAM's own defeat as its killing hit does (`sub_0809AF38`: `sub_0809F974`, `sub_08088528`); the Power Orb counts its hits itself, a Star Rod star each (its spin's speed by its share of its starting HP, NiDL's out of 60), and Nightmare's moves change under a third of his starting HP (NiDL's 20 of 60). NiDL's screen shake (`0x080261D4`: 1-4 timed, 14, 18, 24 and 24 frames, 5-7 the same steps looping until 0 stops it) is `NidlShake()` (`nidl_boss.c`): KatAM's `RequestScreenShake` 1-3, NiDL's 4 as KatAM's 3, 5-7 as KatAM's 4 (KatAM's 4 loops until stopped, `sub_080700D8`: asked for NiDL's timed 4, Mr. Shine & Mr. Bright's wall hits and King Dedede's shook the screen until the fight's end). A hit a boss lives through shakes the screen a little, as one on KatAM's own bosses does (`sub_0809AF38`'s `RequestScreenShake(3)`: 24 frames, a pixel or three each way): KatAM's hit gives it, but not to an object with `unkC` 0x800 (Mr. Shine and Mr. Bright) or for an attack with `unk68` 0x800000, so each boss's hit asks for it too (`NidlBossHitShake()`, `nidl_boss.c`; asked twice, the shake just starts over); not the Power Orb's, whose hits shake its foreground instead.

- **NiDL's bosses** are room objects of category 2 (0 King Dedede, 1 Paint Roller, 2 Meta Knight, 3 Heavy Mole, 4 Mr. Shine & Mr. Bright, 5 Whispy Woods, 6 Kracko, 7 and 8 Nightmare; NiDL types `0x3B`-`0x43`, spawns in the type table `0x0872FF34 + type * 8`), in their levels' last stage (the boss rooms, ported with the levels). Their graphics descriptors follow the mini-bosses' (mini-boss descriptors 30-38, boss index + 30), and their frame tables are each spawn's `+0x38` literal. `KatAMExpansionRooms.RoomBosses` maps the ported ones: a boss is created where NiDL's spawn puts it, with an arena (`enemies/arena.c`) holding the camera on its screen and the room's doors shut until it's beaten, as a mini-boss fight's; a Kirby beaten while another is still in the room, brought back there by KatAM's respawn (`sub_08055D9C`, his level's bounds the whole room's again), has his camera put right back on the fight's screen once he's back, still locked while he falls beaten (it eased back over the room's height: 56 pixels below Kracko's tower top for 900 frames); a boss's screen may reach the room's hidden rows, down to where KatAM's camera stops (its floor shows). Each fight's area holds a CPU helpers' zone point (`BuildNavigation`): a called helper looks for one inside the camera's bounds (`sub_08010034`) and, with none there, never stops looking (the game froze as Heavy Mole's fight started); a helper off the screen looks for one he has a way to, so every room's zones are joined (groups apart, blocks without a floor between them, linked by their nearest two: Kracko's tower froze the game as its camera locked at the top with the helpers below). A fight whose camera moves off its screen has a zone in each block down its middle as far as the camera goes, so its bounds hold one all the way: the Power Orb's sinking screen, and King Dedede's room from its top, where the camera rides as Kirby drops in (a helper in the room as he did froze the game).
- **A boss room has no door out** (`KatAMExpansionRooms.bossExitsOf`): taking the shard its boss leaves is the only way on, as in KatAM. KatAM's shard cutscene ends in Kirby's state `sub_0804C614`, which sets `kirby->roomId = 0x321` (Central Circle) and the spot; the expansion patches those stores (`+0x174`: `adds r0, r4, #0; ldr r1, =NidlShardExit; bl _call_via_r1; b` past them, the `0x321` literal holding the address) to call `NidlShardExit` (`nidl_boss.c`), which looks the room up in `gNidlShardExits` (u16 the boss room, u16 where to, u8 x, y: the landing + 1, as a door's; u8 left, right: the cells of its ground either side, up to 2, the NiDL hub's, `HubGroundBeside()`) and goes to the NiDL hub, onto that level's door there, as KatAM's shards go to Central Circle (NiDL's empty walk-out rooms after a boss are left out of the port), else Central Circle as KatAM's. Each Kirby lands on his own cell of that ground: the first (the player) on the landing, the second left of it, the third right, the fourth further on, or with the third where the ground ends (KatAM put them all on the one cell and they pushed each other apart, the first furthest left: off a pillar's three-cell top in the hub, Kracko's door, falling as if through it). Before that, after the shard's cutscene, the victory dance: KatAM stands every Kirby in the room at its table's spot for the shard (`gUnk_08352DBE`, made for that shard's KatAM boss room: in Meta Knight's room shard 5's was past its corner, and Kirby died); the shard's step after its cutscene (`sub_0801C558`) jumps to `chest.c`'s `NidlShardDone` whenever the NiDL rooms are in (`KatAMExpansion.cs` calls `HookShards()`, as the shard options do), and a NiDL boss's (its room not one of KatAM's 8 boss rooms) dances on the ground under the shard, each Kirby on the ground under him (`NidlShardDance()`, `FloorRow()`; none there, KatAM's dance), KatAM's bosses at their table's spot as before. The finale's bosses leave no shard (`finale.c`): the Power Orb its warp star to Nightmare's room, and Nightmare's end, NiDL's defeat, flies every Kirby in his room out (`NidlEndingFlight()`: the fade to white), through NiDL's ending down from the moon in the Power Orb's room (`ending.c`), to the way on of his room's entry, the Fountain of Dreams hub. A boss fought again with its shard owned leaves a Maxim Tomato, never the shard: its template's gone bit kind is `NIDL_EXIT_KIND` (4, its template slot in unk3, the room in unk12), so when it's eaten `rooms.c`'s `BigSub_08001678` calls `NidlExitTomato()`, which frees the template slot and gives every Kirby in the room the victory dance (`sub_0804BD98`, as a warp star's end does), then the same way on as the shard (`sub_0804C614`, `NidlShardExit()`). The map tracker reads the table from the map table (`+0xD8`: "SX", u16 count, its address) and draws each as a One-Way line out of the boss room.
- **The fight** starts like the mini-bosses' (it waits, untouchable, until Kirby is in its area; then the HP bar and the boss music). Its defeat is KatAM's; then `NidlBossDefeat` puts its mirror shard (`0x65`, subtype the shard, made as KatAM's bosses make theirs) in the middle of the screen, which Kirby takes as a boss's (the shard cutscene, the shard flying to the Dimension Mirror), or a Maxim Tomato when that shard is owned already (as KatAM's bosses fought again).
- **NiDL's shared object tiles** (its effects, drawn with palette 15) are the LZ77 tiles right after palette 15 (USA `0x080D07C8`): its frames' tiles 256-511 (`KatAMExpansion.CommonTiles`, parts with `Common`). In the European NiDL ROM the frame tables from `0x08753E3C` on (Mr. Shine & Mr. Bright's, Whispy Woods', Kracko's...) are moved further than the others (`0x23CA5C`, `LateShift`).

| Boss | NiDL type | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Whispy Woods | `0x40` | Vegetable Valley's boss room (0x9F). The tree is the room's own tiles and collision; the boss is its face, which NiDL draws as three frames stacked (eyes, nose 26 pixels lower, mouth 42 lower: one frame each here, its faces 0-17), on NiDL's spot (206, 256), facing left. NiDL's script (states `0x0874C12C`): its face, a blink, a long look and a blink, then the next attack by its phase (`0x080B2664`: 2 or 4 puffs, or 3 apples, a quarter of the time the other one); a puff is faces 14-16 and a puff of air from its mouth along the ground (NiDL object `0x7E`, from the shared tiles: an attack object here); the apples drop from the branches over one of 5 columns (one time in six the one over Kirby). Hit, it's surprised, then startled; beaten, it cries until it explodes. Its box is its trunk and a little in front of it (the trunk is the room's wall), its touch harmless. The apples (NiDL object `0x7D`, `enemies/apple.c`) are type `0x31` objects (behavior `0xF1`): they flicker in the branches, fall spinning, bounce at Kirby twice and roll on in little hops; Kirby inhales them and spits them back at the tree. 60 HP, shard 1 (index 0) |
| Kracko | `0x41` | Grape Garden's boss room (0x1D1, NiDL's cloud tower, 256 x 2304), at the tower's bottom (NiDL's spot: 200, 2168), where Kirby comes in; NiDL's fight 1:1, both its parts (`bosses/kracko.c`, from its code: its eye's two states, scripts `0x08749158` and a function every frame after them `0x08749160`; its cloud's seven, `0x08749168` and `0x08749184`; its globals `0x02007D00`; each NiDL object an Actor, run in NiDL's order, by group then slot; drawn as sprites of their own at NiDL's depths: its eye, its child `0xC3` (the eye's four orbs, then the cloud: two sprites, its top's palette and its bottom's), its lightning's bolts `0xC5`-`0xC7`). Its eye, no HP bar, chases the closest Kirby up the tower as the camera follows him (KatAM's camera: NiDL's by its own rules): still a while, drifting at him along one axis, turning at him, by chance its lunge (a dash away, a pause, then at him: past him in the air, to his spot and its loops there landed), homing on him after 120 frames of him in the air; it stays near the screen; touching it hurts (its own attack box, -10, -10 to 10, 10: KatAM's object untouchable then, as NiDL's eye takes no hits and KatAM's HUD would show what a Kirby touches), it takes no hits. As the camera's top comes to 367 it rises off the screen; as it comes to 69 the camera locks on the tower's top (its arena, `enemies/arena.c`'s subtype2 3, waiting for `ArenaGo()`; held after the fight while a Kirby's on that screen, as NiDL's lock is the room's camera's); there, every Kirby up, its cloud forms over the side across from him (the HP bar shown then), and the fight proper starts (`0x080A8970`): the lightning, then the swoop; then by turns its bob on its hover (y 48, x 72 or 168), half the time a Waddle Doo (KatAM's, two out at most, always after 5 attacks), else its next attack by where Kirby is (`0x087490B4`; floating high up: the swoop; never the lightning twice): the sweep across, the swoop (down to the floor in a loop and back up across), the lightning (the screen darker: KatAM's BLDCNT darken, NiDL's 8 of 256 a frame to half; it backs off, dips, crosses wavering, a bolt chain every 4 frames to the floor, attack objects of their own, -7, -7 to 7, 7 each; back up). Its random numbers are NiDL's generator, its own seed. Hit (KatAM's attacks: KatAM's bosses take only the stronger ones), NiDL's hurt (`0x0806619C`, its handler `0x080A9760`): frozen 13 frames, jittering, its eye spinning, flashing its hurt colours (the 48 after its descriptor) 2 frames in 4, its cloud puffing fast (its child's mode 2); no hit stars (NiDL's handler makes none); no more hits for 31 frames. KatAM sees a hit a frame after NiDL would, so its hurt's first two steps come together and its cloud's last frame is redone by the new mode; the hurt's step runs after every object's turn, as NiDL's. Touching the cloud hurts (NiDL's box: -32, -20 to 32, 20). Beaten, its defeat frame (NiDL's 0) through KatAM's explosion, then the shard in the middle of the screen. Its frames are NiDL's (`0x087543E8`'s 0-36: eye 0-11, cloud 12-14 cut by palette, `Part.OnlyPalette`, puffs and orbs 15-23, bolts 24-36), `EffectOnly` (`KatAMExpansion.Kracko()`, `WriteKrackoFight()`). Sounds: NiDL's own (its lightning `0x228`, swoop `0x229`, a Waddle Doo `0x1FA`). Checked frame by frame against NiDL's recorded fight (3000 frames up the tower and through the cloud's attacks; a hit; see Open Requests 43). A player beaten in the fight on the tower's top while another Kirby is still up in the room (KatAM keeps the room then, the fight with it, and brings him back where he came in: the tower's bottom, his camera down there and the fight's moves, the screen's, with it) comes back on the tower's top where he was (`Respawns()`: his spawn there till he's back, then his own); alone, KatAM loads the room again and the fight starts over from the bottom, as NiDL's stage. 60 HP for one player (NiDL's 60; more with more players), shard 4 (index 3) |
| Mr. Shine & Mr. Bright | `0x3F` | Butter Building's boss room (0x164; its two copies, 0x165 and 0x166, NiDL's other times of day, stay empty), NiDL's fight 1:1 (`bosses/shine_bright.c`, from its code: the controller, type `0x3F`'s first object, `0x080A3114`; Mr. Shine `0x080A3298` and Mr. Bright `0x080A3C28`, 18 states each, each a script, `0x08748644` and `0x087486D4`, and a function every frame after it; Mr. Bright's flames; their shots, type `0x7F`: the crescent, the fireball, the falling star, the beam; the beam's sparkles and sparks, the dash and fire trails: each NiDL object an Actor, in NiDL's slot order, drawn as a sprite of its own). The intro (they drop in, the HP bar fills: 128 frames), then one goes up into the sky (by chance: Mr. Shine and the night, or Mr. Bright and the day) while the other fights on the ground, and they trade places as NiDL's do once the one in the sky is done waiting (the one on the ground flies up, the other comes down). On the ground: steps or hops at Kirby, high hops, a jump back and a dash across the screen (Mr. Shine rolling with his trail, Mr. Bright in fire leaving four fire trails) into the wall, knocked back; Mr. Shine's crescent (thrown, it comes back), Mr. Bright's fireballs (punched); which move and how long they wait by NiDL's random numbers (`0x03000FB4`'s generator, their own seed) and their HP's half. In the sky: the moon chases Kirby high up and drops eight falling stars 45 frames apart (enemies, `enemies/star.c`: Kirby inhales them and spits them back; NiDL's spin, a step every 3 frames; a puff where they hit Kirby or the floor); the sun goes over Kirby, shines 60 frames and fires its beam 120 frames (its last 96 after Kirby), sparkles and sparks where it meets the floor, the screen darkening under it (`0x080657F8`: the BG palettes blended toward black). The room's sky fades as NiDL's (`0x080275CC`: day, sunset, night, 16 frames from one to the next, day to night through the sunset; NiDL's three colour sets for the room's background, KatAM's BG palettes 6-13). Their colours are NiDL's: Mr. Shine's glow going round four palettes every 12 frames, both flashing their hurt colours as they warn (2 frames in 4) and once beaten (4 in 8), their hurt (13 frames frozen, jittering, flashing, the room's layers in mosaic). Their shots hit Kirby in NiDL's box (7 pixels each way, from his spot to 10 below). Only the one on the ground can be hit (KatAM's object is it, with its box; one HP bar, the ground one's HP). Beaten, the room holds still 27 frames as NiDL's (KatAM's freeze), a shake, its burst; the first beaten goes up into the sky for good and the other comes down; with both beaten they go to the screen's middle and shiver, then KatAM's boss defeat (its explosion over them) and the shard. Checked frame by frame against NiDL's recorded fight (their states, frames, spots and facing, every shot's and effect's kind, frame and spot; screenshots side by side). Not as NiDL's: their bursts when beaten are KatAM's hit stars (NiDL's effects `0x95` and `0xA2`) and the last one is KatAM's explosion (NiDL's eight stars), so shots still in the air fall on instead of popping; KatAM holds Kirby still during the explosion (NiDL doesn't); NiDL's 31 frames without hits after a hurt (its +0x75) aren't modelled.
