Beams: a hop back (5.0), landed, the screen shaking, his swing with a beam (2.5 a frame, 240 frames or a wall); three: the swing again. Wave: a hop, raised, the wave where he stands (39 behind to 49 ahead, 60 frames). His touch and his sword's reach (his `.rca`s' boxes per frame: an invisible object kept on him) hurt; nothing hurts his things. Hit: 20 frames safe after a hit (KatAM's damage undone), else in modes 0-2 and 4-7 dodged (none taken: a hop back, or in the air) 1 in 2, or 1 in 4 and taken, then a hop away, while Kirby is low; more often every third hit and when he's low. His stars (`SQUEAK_STAR_SWORD_AHEAD`/`_BEHIND`) give Sword when inhaled. HP 80 (Squeak Squad's 400). Checked against DS recordings in his stage (`a7s7s2`, loaded by repointing the stage table: his intro, moves, drop, beams, wave, tornado, thrust) and in KatAM (USA, room 0x323). His sounds: Squeak Squad's (`SqueakSfx`). Not ported: his hit flash, his flinch when a spat star hits him (KatAM's hits don't say what hit), guarding the attacks Squeak Squad flags, his own defeat (KatAM's), the intro's camera, his boss music. |

Meanwhile it's untouchable and harmless and shoots nothing, and the Kirbys neither move nor shoot. Then they fly (see Abilities). The fight's screen (its arena, from (8, 8)) sinks 0.2 pixels a frame as they fight, 881 pixels down to the ground (`ArenaSink()`; the cameras on it every frame as NiDL's camera is, 8 + the sink: KatAM sets them as its frame starts, so the arena's lock is held on the next frame's screen; only those of the Kirbys still in the room: a helper lost in the fight comes back in another room, and held to the arena's bounds there, KatAM's helper AI looked for a zone inside them forever, `sub_08010034`, the game frozen), the orb, its shots and the Kirbys sinking with it; not beaten by the time the ground's reached, every Kirby in the room loses a life; its background's cloud bands drift sideways as NiDL's (its line scroll, from the room's first frame: each row of 8 pixels at its own speed, 0 to 4 pixels a frame, times a frame count that wraps every 512, NiDL's starting 6 frames after the room comes in here; NiDL's table, `gNidlCloudSpeeds`, read from the player's NiDL ROM, `region.CloudSpeeds`; `finale.c`'s `CloudsMain()`, a task after the level's, fills KatAM's HBlank DMA table, `gUnk_03002484`, with BG0's scroll for each line, and leaves it off while the screen's all starry sky, so the fight's heavy frames don't run late), in the ending too, the other way as NiDL's moon room; its own colors come back as its fade in from the door ends (`RoomColors()`, from KatAM's palette effects' base, `gUnk_02022120`: KatAM's fade stops a step short of them, NiDL's ends on them); the room's helper navigation has a zone in each block down that path, as KatAM's helper AI looks for one inside the camera forever otherwise (`BuildNavigation()`). Its moves were traced frame by frame in NiDL's fight: from the right of the screen, NiDL's script (hover, roam, charge, roam, zip, hover, charge, roam, dash) twice through, then it flies off over the screen: it hovers and roams (240 frames each: bobbing; a figure of eight over the right), charges (120: 4 stars spread into a column with a gap, then they dash left at 6 pixels a frame), zips (up and down to 5 rows, lunging and shooting a crescent at each) and dashes at the Kirby it's after (high, in the middle or low by where he is). Hovering and roaming it spins (faster as it weakens) and shows its attack look 1-4 times by chance, the first with a timed shot if its last was 140 frames ago or more: in turn a stream of 3 stars, a fan of 3 and a pair. Its shots leave NiDL's afterimages and sparkles behind them (`OrbTrail()`: 16 frames each, at the shot's speed eased by its way's 16ths; none once NiDL's 31 objects would be full, as in its charge's dash; drawn from 13 shared sprites, one OAM piece each, so their dozens don't make KatAM lag) and are gone 64 pixels off the screen; its colors pulse every 24 frames. It, its shots and their trails are drawn where NiDL draws them: a pixel up from KatAM's enemies' (the frames are built a pixel lower, to stand as KatAM's), the way NiDL's face, its shots over it and their trails under it. Its frames are NiDL's (`0x087544B4`'s 4-11, its shots' 20-29 and 39-42 from their own tiles, the intro's slash 14-19, its shots' afterimages 30-38), type 0x44. Touching it or its shots hurts (1 HP, NiDL's knockback). Each Star Rod star takes 1 of its 60 HP, as NiDL's (KatAM's star kind, `0x40`, which its hits need; the orb's -2 for it keeps the hit at 1). Beaten, it leaves no shard (the finale, `finale.c`): KatAM's freeze and flash (its colors pulsing on through them, as NiDL's), then 29 frames after the last hit its burst, NiDL's (`OrbDeath()`): its shots and their trails gone, eight stars flying out and the sparkle in their middle (NiDL's shared effects `0x0874CC60` and `0x0874CA78`, palette 15; their sprites made on their first frame, once the trails' tiles are free), everything moving again 24 frames later; then NiDL's end of the fight, traced frame by frame (`PowerOrbEnd()`, a task of its own, the orb itself gone with the burst): 24 frames after the burst the orb is back, wobbling (its shrink's frames and two of its spin's every 8; its colors still pulsing, from where the fight's were: `OrbEnd.pulse`), and flies off up to the right (`sFleeY`), and a warp star (KatAM's own, object `0x84`, `finale.c`'s `NidlFinaleStarAt()`) comes 11 pixels under where it was, to Nightmare's room (`gNidlFinale`: the rooms, the landing, its flight, `KatAMExpansionRooms.FinaleMirrors()`); the helpers fly to it by themselves (`abilities/star_rod.c`'s `AI()`, the star found by `NidlFinaleStarSpot()`: each waits just left of it, in a row, until the player is on it, then boards), and it waits for them as KatAM's (`warpstar.c`'s `NidlRideRev`) but only until they're all on it (KatAM's 300 frames at most). Its flight (`WarpFlights()`, the last) starts with a step marked `0xFC` (`NIDL_RIDE_FINALE`), where `warpstar.c` holds every Kirby on it while `PowerOrbEnd()` moves them, NiDL's ride: its launch (`sLaunch`) up off the screen; 21 frames in, the screen rises to the room's top (1.25 pixels a frame, a 16th more each frame, no more than a 16th of the way left nor under an 8th: NiDL's camera), the star kept 34 pixels over it, hidden and its Kirbys kept on the screen (KatAM points at helpers off it); 2 frames after the screen's at the top, the orb, NiDL's own picture of it for this (48x48, in its full colors, the pulse over: the orb's last frame, `FRAME_MOON`, `Part.OrbMoon`, from `KatAMExpansion.OrbMoonGraphics()`: its frame table, tiles and palette found in the player's NiDL ROM), a quarter of its size and smaller every 20 frames (`sMoonSizes`, affine: `warpstar.c`'s `NidlShrinkPieces()`, each piece placed as NiDL's sprite code places it, `0x08001E38`: its corner's offset scaled, half a pixel toward the spot, less half of how much it shrinks; matrices 6, 14, 22, 30), flies into the moon from (137, -16) on the screen (`sMoonOrb`, NiDL's object's spot frame by frame), then a black dot (NiDL's 8x8 dot, its VRAM's tile 28, rebuilt as the orb's frame 28, `KatAMExpansion.OrbDot`, drawn a pixel lower than the frames are, as NiDL's; drawn only by this effect: type 0x44's 256 frames are full, `EffectOnly`); 60 frames behind it the star with its Kirbys, the same way (`sMoonStar`; their size asked of `PowerOrbRideSize()` by the step's `+0xA`, `0xFFFF`), then its dot (the Kirbys and the star hidden by their flags `0x400`); 410 frames after the orb came in, the flight goes on to the fade to white (18 frames, Kirby hidden), then KatAM's room change into Nightmare's room, every Kirby keeping the Star Rod for Nightmare's fight. Until the ride the screen is held where the fight left it, the Kirbys' bounds with it (the arena gone, its bounds would take the camera back to the fight's first screen and the Kirbys flying with the Star Rod with it, the star out of reach below). Kirby lost in the fight comes back in the room and flies again |
| Nightmare | `0x43` | The Fountain of Dreams' last fight (room 0x004), on the floor at the room's bottom (he starts at (113, 280), where NiDL's appears; NiDL's room object stands at (215, 72)). The Power Orb's warp star brings the Kirbys in (its flight's step after the room change is `NIDL_RIDE_FINALE` again: `warpstar.c` holds them, `finale.c`'s `NidlFinaleHold()` starts his intro, a task of its own, `NightmareIntroRider()`), NiDL's, traced frame by frame: the screen from the room's top (34) down a pixel a frame for 158 frames, then a pixel every 4 frames to its bottom (216, the fight's screen: `NidlFinaleHoldCamera()`), the star flying down into it with the Kirbys (`sIntroStar`, its moves on the screen; hidden and kept on it until it shows, as KatAM points at helpers off it), then about it; 910 frames in, a blue star hits it (see below), and the fight starts after his entrance; until then he waits hidden (`NightmareIntroOn()`: while the intro runs or a Kirby rides a warp star in his room). On the way, NiDL's cutscene, traced frame by frame and drawn by sprites of the intro's own (`IntroSprite()`, one task each, with his frames after his beams: `EffectOnly`, see `KatAMExpansion.cs`): his comet far off in the sky (NiDL's own cutscene graphics, found by `CutsceneGraphics()`: frames 27-32, palette keys `animId | 0x8000` and `| 0xA000`), drifting left as it wakes up, then off right (`CometFrame()`, `CometSpeed()`); the screen flashing (`FlashLevel()`, `Flash()`: BG palettes 0-13 brighter and every sprite palette darker, by lookup tables as the level changes, the game's own colors kept under it; NiDL's flicker, 2 frames on and 2 off; each level's colors made ahead in one of two buffers, a quarter a frame from 4 frames before they show, `FlashAhead()`, `FlashMake()`, so a change is only a copy and no frame runs late; steady with "Reduce Flashing", `gNidlCalm` set); the 12 blue stars it throws (frame 33, `sBlueStars`/`sBlueStarRuns`: NiDL's speeds and size and turn steps, all drawn from one 64x64 sprite with an affine matrix each, 1-5: `Spin()`) and the bursts on them (34-56, NiDL's shared effect `0x0874CA78`, palette 15: `sBursts`). Then the hit: the star's gone (`NightmareRideSize()`: `NIDL_RIDE_ALONE`, `warpstar.c` drawing the Kirbys without it), a puff where it was (57-73, NiDL's shared effect `0x0874C9D8`), and the Kirbys are knocked off, held still (`Knock()`: NiDL's tumble, bounce and slide, in NiDL's frames of Kirby with the Star Rod: tumbling, the rod turning with him, then fallen, his back to us, the rod held out, the motions `STAR_ROD_MOTION_TUMBLE` and `STAR_ROD_MOTION_FALLEN`; the helpers spread to the player's right); 260 frames on the flight ends (its last two steps, `KatAMExpansionRooms.cs`: a frame standing, then the end, no hop) and they're the players' again, as his light grows into his white shape (74-81: his 67 down to 60) where NiDL's appears, (113, 280), where the fight starts (`NightmareInit()`). He's fought as NiDL's with the Star Rod on the ground (every Kirby in his room keeps it: `NidlFinaleKeep()`, `StarRodKeep()`, see Abilities). His fight is NiDL's, ported from its code (`NightmareStart()`, see `bosses/nightmare.c`) and checked frame by frame against NiDL's own (its states, frames, facing and spots over the 2490 frames recorded: the same random draws give the same fight): his 12 states, each a script and a frame function, his moves in NiDL's order: the cape (up, then his cape thrown open and a fan of five stars), the dash (back to the screen's edge on Kirby's side, then across it), then the hand (4 stars from his hand, 6 under a third of his HP) or the charge (his hand raised, then 6 stars down at Kirby), the tornado (down at Kirby, steering after him; under a third of his HP up off the screen and down through it) and the swoop; between them he hovers, vanishes and reappears. His stars stop where they hit the floor and burst into a puff, as NiDL's. His room's background drifts left 4 pixels a frame, as NiDL's (its line scroll, `0x080B6C40`: round its 512 pixels every 128 frames, from the room's coming in, 4 frames behind here, `DRIFT_AHEAD`, as KatAM's room comes in that much sooner; a row up, where NiDL draws it, `KatAMExpansionRooms.cs`' `backgroundDown`, KatAM bottom-aligning it in a room a row taller than NiDL's: both checked against NiDL's screens through his intro and fight; `finale.c`'s `DriftMain()`, through KatAM's background offset, LevelInfo `+0x3C`, so KatAM draws the columns it brings in). A Star Rod star takes 10 of his 60 HP (NiDL's numbers: the star's strength 8, the Master Sword's, plus his star modifier 2), only while his cape's open, his tornado's up or his hand's out (his frames' boxes), and knocks him back facing the Kirby who shot it; touching him hurts (1, KatAM's damage). His graphics are an anim of their own (`KatAMExpansionNightmare.cs`, `gNidlNightmareFight`): NiDL's body frames with his top half over them, cut by palette into three sprites (his collar, his tornado, his cape or light), NiDL's four palettes written each frame (his tornado's two by turns, his cape's blended to and fro); his stars, sparkles and effects are NiDL's frames too. No shard (the finale): beaten, NiDL's defeat (`NightmareEnd()`, traced frame by frame; KatAM's freeze and burst first, as the Power Orb's): 24 frames after the burst he flies off right from where he was (NiDL's frames 68-71, 3 frames each; 16 frames each at 4, 2, 1, 1/2 and 1/4 pixels a frame), turns to light there and comes back as light at (136, 282), caped (50-51, with his top half, 96, over them as NiDL draws his body frames), then spreads out and breaks up, blinking (76-88, `sDefeat`: NiDL's runs of frames), a light over him meanwhile (0x08754738's 13-17, `sDefeatLight`), then bursts along the floor (`sFloorBursts`), drawn mirrored as NiDL draws him, the screen held, the Kirbys free; 840 frames into his flight off, every Kirby still in his room is flown out where he stands (`NidlEndingFlight()`: the ending's flight, `KatAMExpansionRooms.cs`' `WarpFlights()`, `gNidlFinale.ending`: KatAM's fade to white, then a room change into the Power Orb's room for NiDL's ending down from the moon, below, then another to the Fountain of Dreams hub, onto its floor's left, his room's way on, `bossExitsOf`), the Star Rod gone and every Kirby Normal (`StarRodEnd()`: NiDL's Kirby has no ability after; KatAM's HUD's ability card goes with it), and no pause on the finale's flights (`finale.c`'s `NidlFinaleFlight()`: `warpstar.c`'s `NidlRideStep()` takes START before Kirby's update checks it; KatAM's pause menu, opened as a flight's fade changes rooms, loaded over the new room's graphics, garbled). The ending (`ending.c`, a task of its own; NiDL's moon room has the Power Orb room's cells, its background the same picture mirrored, which isn't): the flight's step after the room change is `NIDL_RIDE_FINALE`, where `warpstar.c` holds the Kirbys while `finale.c`'s `NidlFinaleHold()` hands them to `NidlEndingRider()`, which moves them, NiDL's moon scene traced frame by frame (its camera, its cutscene objects, its OAM) without its narration: the screen held at the moon (8, 8), shaking (`sEndShake`); the moon's burst (`sEndBurst`: 8 sparkles flying out from the bite's middle and the middle's glow, frame by frame with NiDL's flicker; NiDL's shared effect `0x0874CEE8`) and the crescent 12 frames in (`sCrescent`: NiDL's 33 tilemap entries, their palettes 2 down as the room's, written into the room's RAM tilemap, `unk180[0].tilemap`, the foreground redrawn by its `prevScroll` set to `0x7FFF`); a twinkle, then the warp star with every Kirby out of the bite (each given KatAM's riding star, `sub_0808EDB8`, facing left as NiDL's), a 16th of their size, 4/256 bigger every 16 frames (`NidlEndingRideSize()`, asked by the steps' `+0xA`), hidden off the screen's top and right as NiDL's, along `sEndStar` (its moves on the screen frame by frame), at their size from frame 931, trailing NiDL's sparkles (shared effect `0x0874CF28`'s 0-14: the trail, a puff every 10 frames, twinkles 3 times in 63) and throwing small stars out behind every 48 frames (15-26, faster and faster); the screen scrolling down 3/16 of a pixel a frame from frame 930; from there on four times as fast as NiDL's (`RIDE_SPEED`: four of NiDL's frames a frame (twice at first; the owner found it slow), the star's and the screen's moves with them, the trail and small stars at their own pace; NiDL's has its narration to read); King Dedede flying by at NiDL's own speed (sped up, the owner found him far too fast: his own clock, a frame a frame, his flight timed to end as the star settles, `END_SETTLE_T`; `sEndDedede`, frames 2291-3022: the ending's own frames 64 and 66-68, from its descriptor, `KatAMExpansion.EndingGraphics()`, palette keys `animId \| 0x6000` and `\| 0x8000`); at NiDL's frame 4259 (1762 here), the star still after its loop (NiDL's Kirby jumps off it next, to throw the Star Rod), the flight goes on: the fade to white (on the star, facing left), the room change to the hub, where `warpstar.c` lets the stars go (a ride's room change with the riders standing). The Power Orb waits hidden meanwhile (`NidlEndingOn()`, `NidlBossWait`). Its sprites are Nightmare's type's frames 121-166 (effect only: King Dedede, then the shared effects with palette 15 by the spit star's key, as his defeat's bursts). Its frames (effect only, past the type's 256) and palettes: KatAM loads 7 sprite palettes a room (slots 8-14, keys in `gUnk_02022E60`), past them always into the last; his intro's fill them (its comet's two, his light's), so as his fight starts his fight's palettes take slots of their own (`FightPalettes()`: his collar's, his tornado's and his cape's in the comet's two, his light's, his stars' and effects', the puffs'); the defeat loads his third where his cape's was, his second where his tornado's was, and its light's, and its glow's where his stars' was, as they're first needed (`DefeatPalette()`; a key with bit 12 set is KatAM's use-once kind, never used); the arena (never drawn) loads none |

## Abilities

NiDL's Needle, Freeze, Light, Ball, Hi-Jump, Mike, Backdrop and Star Rod are KatAM abilities 27-34 (`NIDL_ABILITY_*` in `abilities.h`). Needlous, Chilly, Cool Spook, Bubbles and Starman give the first five; Mike and Backdrop come from the mini-bosses Mr. Tick-Tock and Bugzzy; the Power Orb's fight gives the Star Rod, and so does its stand in the NiDL hub, there once the game is beaten (as the Master Sword's).

| Ability | B button |
|---|---|
| Needle | Kirby sticks his needles out (10 frames) and keeps them out while B is held, then pulls them in. In the air he falls with needles all around, and keeps them out on landing if B is still held. The needles hurt what they touch (a cutting hit, strength 2: NiDL's 3 was too strong), and Kirby can still be hurt meanwhile |
| Freeze | Kirby wraps himself in ice crystals for as long as B is held (16 frames at least). What touches the ice freezes, with KatAM's Ice's freezing hit; Kirby can still be hurt meanwhile |
| Light | Kirby hovers and holds up a light (28 frames), which shines for 65 frames; then the ability is used up. NiDL's Light only lights up dark rooms. KatAM has none, so the light flashes the screen white (when the room isn't already blending) and hurts every enemy on the screen, like a weak Crash. With the Squeak Squad rooms, its flash lights Squeak Squad's candles (4-3-7, 4-3-8, 8-1-6) as Fire does (`squeak/gimmicks.c`'s `SqueakCandleMain`) |
| Ball | Kirby hops (up to 20 pixels) and curls into a ball, which bounces once and then rests. B again launches it (up to 37 pixels), and it ends on landing. It can be steered in the air and hurts what it touches; Kirby can still be hurt meanwhile |
| Hi-Jump | As NiDL's, frame by frame (`abilities/hi_jump.c`, from its code and recordings): Kirby crouches for 4 frames, dust puffing out both ways, then rockets up 4.16 pixels a frame for 23 frames, slows (148 pixels in all) and falls, somersaulting; steerable sideways (up to 2 pixels a frame, his cape leaning more the faster he goes: NiDL's five sets of poses), glowing yellow for the first 33 frames (not with Reduce Flashing), with NiDL's star, sparkles and take-off burst. B again stops him with a puff; a ceiling or the room's top ends the rise at once. He hurts what he touches the whole rise, and can still be hurt meanwhile; in the fall after it, B does nothing till he lands |
| Mike | Kirby sings, a different song each of the 3 times: into a megaphone, a microphone, then a microphone while jumping 26 pixels. Frame by frame as NiDL's (its script `0x080455C8`, recorded in NiDL with B pressed for each use): from the press, the start (25, 33 and 43 frames), the song (32 frames each) and the end (5 frames: Kirby is back on the 62nd, 70th and 80th frame); the third use's jump is NiDL's heights frame by frame (26 pixels up from the 14th frame, back down by the 42nd). While he sings, every enemy on the screen takes a heavy hit and the screen flashes (a task of its own, `NidlFlash`, abilities.c: NiDL's flashes, on its 1st-3rd, 5th-7th, then every other frame to the 15th, then the 19th and 23rd frames of each song; the player's Kirby only, and not while the room blends already). After the third song the ability is gone. (The flashes went on for minutes, through rooms, after the ability was gone: `TaskGetStructPtr` is a macro that reads its argument twice, so `TaskGetStructPtr(TaskCreate(...))` made two tasks, one with whatever was in its memory for its levels and count) |
| Backdrop | KatAM's Throw (its closest relative: grab with a dash, then throw, a backdrop when thrown backwards) with NiDL's red headband: Throw's body draws its headband in colors 12-14 of the body palette, and Backdrop's palette has NiDL's red there. NiDL's own grab couldn't be measured (its Waddle Dee never came within reach) |
| Star Rod | In the finale's fights (`abilities/star_rod.c`, see Bosses), for every Kirby in their rooms, and from its stand in the NiDL hub (below). In Nightmare's, NiDL's last, it's held on the ground, as NiDL's: Kirby walks, runs and floats with the rod held up (its hat: NiDL's Star Rod set, frames `0xFED`-`0x1025` matched to KatAM's body poses by shape, `KatAMExpansionHats.cs`; behind his body where NiDL draws it behind him, and turning with him as he tumbles off a warp star: NiDL's `0x1060`-`0x106F`, see Hats below), jumps as NiDL's Kirby with the rod jumps (spinning from his jump's first frame till he lands, the rod sweeping a rainbow arc: NiDL's whole frames `0xFE5`-`0xFEC`, a frame each, as recorded in its fight, played at KatAM's jump's rise and flip, animation indexes 15 and 16, which `NidlAbilitySetup` points to them; the flip's anim loops, so KatAM's fall, 17, never comes before he lands, `sub_08044FD4`; walking off a ledge is the fall at once, no spin, as KatAM's; the arc keeps its own colours, as NiDL's never brightens: `NidlDisplaySprite()` leaves the spin's frames out of the glow and the palette cycle, from `glows[1].plain` on), and B swings it (NiDL's swing, traced frame by frame: 16 frames, `0x1026`-`0x1035`; a star on the 5th, 20 pixels ahead the way he faces and 4 down, flying on at 4 pixels a frame; once a press, standing still on the ground, falling in the air); he keeps it there: KatAM's hit handler (`sub_0804A728`, which would drop it as an ability star) is detoured to `NidlKirbyHit` (it runs with him Normal, the rod back after), and whatever else takes it (his death, Select) gives it again (`StarRodKeep()`). In the Power Orb's fight, every Kirby in its room flies with it: Kirby holds the rod up and flies over the screen facing right, 2 pixels a frame the way the D-pad points (a quarter of that for 5 frames when let go), kept on the screen (x 16-224, y 18-132). A or B swings the rod (21 frames) and shoots a star on the 10th, 28 pixels ahead, flying right at 4 pixels a frame, spinning, a sparkle behind it (the player's only: the helpers' fly without, fewer effects for four flying Kirbys), until it hits something (it bursts) or leaves the screen (no hit box of KatAM's, `0x200`: the orb takes their hits itself, `StarRodHits()`, and KatAM's collisions checked them every frame); held, it shoots every 31 frames. A hit knocks him back for 10 frames and takes 1 HP. In the fight's intro the riders fly every Kirby up to the fight's screen as NiDL's Kirby flies, frame by frame (`StarRodIntro()`), the helpers behind him. His frames, the star's and the HUD card are NiDL's (card 25; the swings' sparkles in NiDL's shared palette 15, as NiDL draws them), and the rod shines as NiDL's Kirby's (NiDL's Kirby drawing, `0x0803DE6C` and `0x0803DB74`; here `abilities.c`'s `NidlDisplaySprite()`, KatAM's `DisplaySprite` detoured, for the Star Rod's hats: only the rod's own frames, as every new ability's moves share one hat anim, so Mike's songs and the others' moves keep their colours): one frame in four its star glows (each Star Rod frame's glowing version, NiDL's frame + `0x83`, its tiles loaded with the frame's: `KatAMExpansionHats.cs`' `AddGlows()`), and its colours brighten on a cycle of 10 frames (bright for 2, halfway for 1, its own for 7: `StarRodPalettes()`), on KatAM's frame counter (`gUnk_0203AD40`); the card shows the Star Rod's name alone, its picture would be in the way of the fights (KatAM shows an ability's name and picture as long as Kirby has it), and Normal's name alone when it's taken away, as KatAM shows it once an ability's lost. In Nightmare's room a helper doesn't swing it until the fight starts (`NightmareIntroOn()`): he's up while Nightmare's light still grows into his shape. Then the helpers swing it at Nightmare by an aim of their own (`StarRodAim()`; KatAM's helper AI only goes for what's within 48 pixels of a helper's height, never Nightmare over the floor): on the floor, when their star would meet him where he takes hits (`NightmareTarget()`, asked of his fight as `NightmareFightIn()` finds it once a frame: walking the task list for each of the up to 8 heights each helper tries took 7% of a frame with three helpers, and his fight lagged every other frame as his stars flew; with three helpers and every Kirby shooting, the scripted fight's lag frames went from 350 to 100), now and then; when it would on the way up a jump (54 pixels, A held 22 frames), not right under him, and the swing there; pressed through KatAM's helper AI's action slot for those frames (`AIShoot()`, `AIJump()`), as a player presses B and A. Toned down so the fight is the player's (the owner's): a swing a 32nd of a chance a frame (a jump's a 64th, in a jump of his own a quarter), 120 to 183 frames between one helper's swings (`NightmareAimWait()`, his fight's, counted down by it), and each swing 0 to 31 frames after he sees him in line, at wherever Nightmare is then (`AIM_REACT`): he takes hits only now and then, and a helper's swing seen in line took it every time; three helpers beat him in 50 seconds with the player looking on, now in about 2 and a half minutes. The ability he had before is given back after the shard's dance. Each flying Kirby has a rider task that moves him (his state, `StarRodHeld`, does nothing); KatAM's own hurt would drop the rod as an ability star, so he's guarded from it while the orb is alive and the orb's attacks hit him themselves. Helpers fly by the rider's own AI (`AI()`), engaged in the fight: always on the move to spots before the orb (40 to 134 pixels left of it, 32 up or down from its row, a new one every 20 to 51 frames, never nearer than 40), shooting when in line, a swing once in 64 to 127 frames (`AI_FIRE_WAIT`; held, the rod swings every 31, and three helpers took the orb's 60 HP in 20 seconds with the player looking on: the owner's, the fight should be the player's; now about 55), and half the time dodging a shot of the orb's coming at them (16 frames up or down away from it: `PowerOrbShotNear()`), with KatAM's helper AI held still. **The NiDL hub's stand** (`abilities/star_rod_stand.c`): the rod upright in the golden pedestal where Central Circle has the Master Sword's stand (the hub has its tiles), there once the game is beaten, as the Master Sword's (`gAIKirbyState` from `AI_KIRBY_STATE_UNK1` on, set by KatAM's ending; before, the pedestal is empty and does nothing; the "Unlock the Master Sword" option is the sword's alone), working as the Master Sword's stand works (`sub_080AA618`, `sub_080AAA64`): a player's Kirby (not a helper) with no ability who touches it takes it, in KatAM's pose for getting an ability (`sub_08054C0C`; the card with its name and picture, as any ability's outside the fights), and it's gone while a player's Kirby has the Star Rod or it's out as an ability star (Select; the Master Sword's stays away for its star too), back with KatAM's puff (effect `0x292`) once neither is. It's object `0x9A`, which KatAM leaves empty: its spawn is `NidlCreateRodStand`, its parameters the Master Sword stand's (`0x98`) with the Star Rod, its own anim and VRAM (`KatAMExpansion.cs`); the anim (`KatAMExpansionMotions.cs`' `BuildRodStand()`) is NiDL's rod held straight up (frame `0x1023`'s second layer, the rod alone) in the Star Rod hat's colors, its foot 2 pixels into the pedestal, and its glowing version (`0x1023`'s own, + `0x83`, is the same frame: the held rod's glow ring, `0x1070`'s pixels round its star that `0xFED`'s aren't, laid round the upright one's star); it glows and its colors cycle as on Kirby (`glows[2]`). Taken there, the rod is his everywhere: B swings it on the ground and in the air as in Nightmare's fight, its stars hit enemies and bosses as an attack of Kirby's (KatAM's star kind), as hard as the Master Sword's slash (strength 8, `sub_08075DB0`'s, plus the target's star modifier; the sword's fire and cut types add the target's modifiers for those instead, so a target weak to fire, as Mr. Frosty, takes 3 more from the sword), a hit doesn't take it (`NidlKirbyHit`), nor does a boss's shard dance (KatAM puts his ability back after it, `sub_0804C614`, and `StarRodRestore()` gives back only the ability the Power Orb's fight took, marked `STAR_ROD_FROM_FIGHT` in `STAR_ROD_SAVED`; a Master Sword it takes the place of is put away, KatAM's flag that one is out, `gUnk_0203AD34`, down as when Kirby loses it, and up again with it in `StarRodRestore()`: left up, never given back after Nightmare, it kept the sword of KatAM's final fights from dropping, Dark Mind's and Shadow Kirby's, and the Master Sword stands hidden); his death, Select and Nightmare's end (`StarRodEnd()`) do. Flying into the Power Orb's fight with it, he's given it again there (`StarRodStart()`: its card without the picture) and keeps it after. Where NiDL's set has no frame for KatAM's pose (climbing a ladder, swimming, a wall bump, the end of a hurt roll, riding a warp star...) the rod blinked out or was gone; it's held there too now (`KatAMExpansionHats.cs`' `StarRodPoses`, see Hats below): only calling the helpers hides it, as every new hat |

The timings and speeds were measured in NiDL. NiDL's Needle, Freeze, Ball and Hi-Jump also made Kirby untouchable (KatAM's guard, as its Burning's): not here, so no ported ability is a shield. Mike and Backdrop were measured by patching the enemy Starman to give them: an enemy's ability is byte 12 of its variant data (Starman's: `0x08741918`), copied to `obj+0` of its extra data at spawn. In water, every ability's B button does what KatAM's other abilities do (the base ability's entry). CPU helpers fight like Normal Kirby.

Squeak Squad's Ninja, Animal, Bubble and Metal are abilities 35-38 (`SQUEAK_ABILITY_*`), built only with both the NiDL and the Squeak Squad ROMs (without them, `SqueakStart` gives none: `gSqueakAbilityBodies` is empty). Kirby with them is drawn from the DS's own frames in every motion, in the player's spray colors (`KatAMExpansionSqueakAbilities.cs`: the body anim and the hat anim per ability, `SqueakAbilitySetup`); their moves draw him with `ScrollPiece`s (`scrolls.h`), as the scroll moves do (`gSqueakAbilityLooks`, `SQUEAK_LOOK_*`). Their cost per frame, measured in the emulator (dropped frames: a frame with no game frame run, `TasksExec` never entered), cut for the owner ("When hitting floating platforms with non cutting powers, the game lags.", with Needle and Bubble): his moves' piece is kept by its task's number in his ability byte (`MOVE_SLOT`, abilities.h's `NIDL_MIKE_USES`, as Spark's charge; `SCROLL_MOVE_NONE` once it's gone, `ScrollPieceDestroy`), not searched for among the 128 tasks each time it's asked (up to 7 times a frame); a look or an effect starting asks `SqueakVramSpare()` (squeak.c) whether KatAM's sprite heap still has its tiles and the spare ones, counting its free segments only till there are enough, 4 at a time where they're free, not `VramGetTotalAllocatedTiles()`, which walks the whole heap (some 5000 cycles a call, up to 4 a frame); and Squeak Squad's ropes have a hit box only while a Kirby who cuts is in their room (`squeak/gimmicks.c`), so other attacks pass through them as the DS's (Ninja's kunai stuck in them 200 frames each, piling up, and Bubble's bubbles burst on them). Room 0x584 (8-2, nine floating platforms), 240 frames of B held 30 then let go 10: Bubble 21 dropped frames before, 5 after; Ninja 12, 0; room 0x488 (3-3-7, two rope platforms): Bubble 14, 7; Ninja 41, 2; room 0x401: Bubble 6, 0; Ninja 8, 0. Needle drops none in any of them, before or after; Bubble's last ones are its bursts' sprites, drawn. Their HUD portraits are built from the DS too (`SqueakAbilityPortraits()`: the name band in the narrow letters of KatAM's own cards, then a burst in the ability's colors behind the DS's standing Kirby, his colors merged to fit one palette); the pause screen's help shows KatAM's general text with them, as with NiDL's.

| Ability | B button |
|---|---|
| Ninja | From Bio Spark (the DS's). As the DS's (`abilities/squeak_moves.c`, from its code, `PrmKirbyNinja`'s words and its emulator): standing, a kunai (7 pixels a frame, through walls for 5 frames, sticks in a wall); dashing or holding a direction, a dash (sound 0xDF) that grabs what it touches in its first 4 frames (carried up 8 pixels a frame through the ceiling for 10 frames, then slammed down for 10, Kirby bouncing back) and else ends in a slash; in the air, a kunai, or with Down a dive (4 pixels a frame both ways, 135 frames at most); falling against a wall while holding toward it, he clings (B a kunai from it, A a wall jump with a flip); he runs on water. With the scroll (16), B held charges the DS's art (30 frames): let go, pillars of flame (a geyser on water) rise from 7 spots before him. Damages are the DS's over 5 (kunai 2, slash, dive, bounce 4, slam 10, flames 8) |
| Animal | From Gaw Gaw (the DS's). As the DS's (`abilities/squeak_moves.c`, from its code, `PrmKirbyAnimal`'s words and its emulator): standing, a scratch (22 frames, its claws hurting 13 to 53 pixels ahead in frames 4-6); dashing, a crouch (7 frames, slowing 0.1 a frame) and a leap (2.0 ahead, 1.5 up, 0.1 less a frame, 53 frames at most) whose claws grab what they touch: a flurry of hits (67 frames, the enemy held 16 pixels ahead), then a throw. He digs through Squeak Squad's dirt (`SqueakDigCells()`, the DS's diggable cells, kept solid and redrawn bare as they're dug: `gSqueakDigRooms`, each room's list of block records): B with dirt ahead digs ahead, Down and B (or B crouching) over dirt digs down, Up under dirt looks up and B digs up, 16 pixels in 20 frames. With the scroll (20), B in the air rolls (4.0 back, then 0.3 more ahead a frame up to 4.0, level, 48 frames) or, with Down, spins up (4.0 up, 0.3 less a frame, falling at 4.0 at most, 48 frames); into a wall or onto the ground, a bounce (1.2 back, 3.0 up, KatAM's shortest quake). Damages are the DS's over 5 (scratch and digs 4, flurry, throw, roll and spin 2) |
| Bubble | From Bubble Head (the DS's). As the DS's (`abilities/squeak_moves.c`, from its code, `PrmKirbyBubble`'s words and its emulator): B starts the wand (9 frames), then while B is held he blows ahead, or up or down with the pad (the wand turned each way), a bubble every 9 frames (two kinds by turns: 2.03 ahead slowing, or 0.4 ahead speeding up and sinking; Up or Down send them 1.7 up or down), through walls, each popping when its animation ends (17 or 18 frames, then an 11-frame pop) or gone when it hits something; his spray hurts too ahead and down (not up: the DS's own), and he doesn't move or turn while blowing (sliding to a stop on the ground, falling in the air). Let go, a 4-frame end. With the scroll (21), B while moving blows one big bubble instead (29 frames; 1.7 ahead, 0.4 up, gaining 0.12 and 0.04 a frame), and the blowing sound is the DS's other one. Damages are the DS's over 5 (spray 2, bubbles 4, the big one 8); their hit is KatAM's kind 0x80 (Magic's), which an enemy's hurt state shuts out, so a beaten enemy isn't held in its hurt by the next bubbles |
| Metal | From Metalun (the DS's). As the DS's (`abilities/squeak_moves.c`, from its code, `PrmKirbyMetal`'s and `PrmKirbyMetalKirbyBase`'s words, checked in its emulator): heavy. The movement words Metal's file changes from Kirby's own, Metal takes; KatAM's own for the rest (KatAM's rules for when he jumps, floats, lands and swims; KatAM's walk, air steering, float, jump and dash detoured to Metal's, `MetalWalk` and the others, and his fall wrapped, `MetalRise`/`MetalFall`): he walks 0.03 faster a frame up to 0.5 and stops 0.15 a frame; there's no dash (its double tap starts a walk); the jump is 2.68 up whether A is held or not, gravity 0.17, falling at most 4.5 (about 21 pixels high, the DS's 20); in the air 0.05 faster a frame up to 1.23; floating 0.03 faster a frame up to 0.35, flapping up at most 0.3 and sinking at most 2.03. His steps (every 30 frames, the DS's walk animation's frames 16 and 46 of each 60) shake the screen (KatAM's shortest shake) and thud. Hard: touching enemies doesn't hurt him and his body doesn't hurt them either (the DS's: a Waddle Dee pressed against him, neither hurt), KatAM's enemies' own hit kind (0x80, an enemy's body and the attacks that keep it) shut out of his `unk5C` and taken out of his body's `unk68` while he's Metal; their other attacks (fire...) and spikes still hurt him; Squeak Squad's currents don't move him. B: in the air with Down, the stomp (stopped 24 frames, then straight down at 6, his body's box hitting for 4, the DS's 20; on the ground the shake, the big puff and a bounce, 0.5 ahead and 1.34 up for 16 frames); else, with the scroll (22), the roll (12 frames at the speed he had, then rolling: on flat ground slowing 0.04 a frame, down a slope faster by 0.16, 0.22 steep, up to 3.0, up one slower by 0.09, 0.14 steep, turning back when it stops; in the air its air words; a thud and a shake on landing and into a wall; its box while rolling fast or in the air; B ends it, 4 frames); else nothing (no inhale). His moves are drawn from the DS's frames all in metal grey (`metal_ext`'s, in their own palette). Not ported: the DS's Metal sinking in water (its water words 40-51 and `metal_wat`: he swims as KatAM's Kirby, his fall into water cut to KatAM's 2.5), pushing enemies along, its other changed words (the slide's, 20-25), the 30-frame timer the stomp's landing sets (+0x5C, its use not found) |


How they fit in:

- **Ability field.** KatAM keeps the ability Kirby is getting in 5 bits of `transitioningAbility`, with 3 flags above it, so 27-31 were all the room there was. It's widened to 6 bits:
  - the "random" flag moves from `0x20` to `0x40`;
  - the old `0x40` flag ("several things in the mouth", which makes the spat star bigger) moves to `unkF1`, which the game only ever clears;
  - every `& 0x1F` read of the field becomes `& 0x3F` (18 places, found by their code, the same in every version);
  - swallowing (`sub_080547C4`) is replaced by `NidlSwallow`.
- **Ability tables.** Every table KatAM indexes by ability (B on land and in water, setup, body palette, hats, the CPU helpers' AI, the state after getting an ability, the pause world map's Kirby) is copied with 34 entries, and the game's code is pointed to the copies. The new entries come from `gNidlAbilityTables`, or from Normal's.
- **Swallowing.** `sub_080547C4` took abilities `0x1B` and `0x1C` to mean "random" and "none". They're now `0x3B` and `0x3C` (`NIDL_SWALLOW_*`), and the one object type whose ability was `0x1B` (`0xCE`) is changed to match.
- **HUD and pause portraits** are NiDL's ability cards. `sub_080356AC` and `sub_08088640` are detoured to `NidlAbilityPortrait` and `NidlAbilityHudTimer`, and the special portraits (`0x1C`-`0x1F`) move to `0x2C`-`0x2F`.
- **Hats** (`KatAMExpansionHats.cs`). KatAM draws Kirby as a body plus a hat sprite, animating in step. The new abilities use Normal's body table (`gUnk_0834C5D4`), and each one's hat anim has a variant per entry of it (185): the body's commands, with NiDL's hat for the same pose.
  - `BodyMatches` pairs each KatAM body frame with one of NiDL's plain Kirby poses, by silhouette, checked by eye. Mirrored body frames (attributes `| 0x4000` / `| 0x8000`) are matched unmirrored, and their hat is mirrored the same way.
  - `HatFrames` pairs each plain pose with the same pose in the ability's block. Its body pixels match exactly, or match outside the hat where the hat hides part of it.
  - Poses with no match (inhaling, a mouthful, Kirby facing the camera with something on his head) get no hat (but the Star Rod's, below).
  - NiDL's Kirby is always pink, so its hats (Needle's) draw some of his head in his colors. Those pixels are left to the body sprite, which has the player's colors, where KatAM's body frame covers them (`BodyCoverage()`: the body frame drawn from its attributes, OAM entries and the tiles its GetTiles loads, mirrored as KatAM mirrors it). Where it doesn't (KatAM's Kirby is shaped a little differently), they're drawn in the hat's nearest other color: the room showed through Needle's spikes. Body frames whose hats come out the same share one frame.
  - Calling the helpers (animation indexes 97-99: taking out the phone, dialing, ringing; `CallAnimations`) shows no hat: it looked odd on Kirby holding his phone.
  - The Star Rod's tumble (animation indexes 74-89, KatAM's anim `0x2C`: hopping off a warp star, tumbling in cutscenes) is NiDL's own, the rod turning with him (`StarRodTumble`: frames `0x1060`-`0x106F`, the ones Nightmare's intro plays; KatAM's step v is NiDL's `0x1060 + v - 1`, both turning clockwise, the same body to the pixel), not a matched pose's: the matched poses held the rod still, and one of them hid it. KatAM's anim `0xB` (animation index 55, tumbling with his eyes shut) is the same bodies (its frames 0-3 and 4-14 are `0x2C`'s 0-3 and 5-15) and turns the rod the same way; its matched poses' rod jumped about and was gone in 4 of its frames.
  - The Star Rod's poses where the matches leave it out (`StarRodPoses`): every KatAM body frame of animation indexes `0x00`-`0x66` with no rod where KatAM's own hats show (the Sword's cap as the reference; 36 frames) gets one of NiDL's Star Rod frames, its Kirby fitted to the body by silhouette (the offset of the best overlap, as `BodyMatches`' were found; the existing matches come out the same) and the pose picked by eye to go with the frames either side: climbing a ladder (anim `0x9`, animation indexes 45-46, the rod blinked in and out every few frames) holds it straight up, as its one matched frame did (`0x1023`); the wall bumps (indexes 22-24) and swallowing hold it up (`0xFF8`), squashed flat as crouching (`0xFEE`) or NiDL's squash (`0x1019`); the swim strokes hold it out ahead; the hurt roll's last frame (index 39) is NiDL's own, the same body to the pixel (`0x103D`, its hurt's sitting up).
  - The warp star ride (animation index 90, `WarpStarRide`): KatAM draws the Kirby riding it with the body's and the hat's variant + his number (`sub_0803F790`: the body's anim `0x2D`, a frame per spot on the star), and the new hats' variant for an index is the index, so the helpers got the next indexes' hats (out of place, or none) and the player none (the rod was gone the whole ride). Their entry for it points to 4 variants of their own after the rest, one per spot, and the Star Rod holds the rod up on each.
  - Layering: KatAM always draws the hat over the body, but NiDL draws the Star Rod behind Kirby in 33 of its frames (walking, running, jumping, floating...: its pieces after Kirby's in the frame). Those pieces are cut where KatAM's body frame covers them (`HatFrame()`'s `layered`, the Star Rod only; the other hats have 1-4 such frames each and are left as they were).
- **Attack poses** (`KatAMExpansionMotions.cs`) are NiDL's frames, played at animation indexes `0x67`-`0x76`, which `NidlAbilitySetup` points to them (the Star Rod's spin also at the jump's, 15 and 16, its hat table there too). These are KatAM's copy abilities' attacks, which only their own states play. They were at `0x4A`-`0x59` before, but that's KatAM's tumble, which a warp star's hop-off and cutscenes play for every ability, so a new ability's Kirby tumbled in its attack poses (the Star Rod's swing, held up, flying...). That range is also the only one KatAM's Kirbys standing on one another's heads leaves out (`sub_08038B34`, every two Kirbys in a room, each frame; with flags `0x3800F00` too, as riding a warp star), so the Star Rod's flying Kirbys in the Power Orb's fight flew through one another, as NiDL's; they still do, the function detoured to `NidlKirbyStack` (`abilities/star_rod.c`), which marks them (`StarRodHeld`) with one of those flags while it runs. The new abilities' attack poses elsewhere take part in it now, as KatAM's own attacks do.
  - Kirby's own pixels go to the body sprite, which has the player's palette and 16 tiles of VRAM.
  - The rest (ice, needles, the cape) goes to the hat sprite, which has 48 tiles of VRAM. Frames are cut into 8x8 cells on the grid (of the 64 offsets) needing the fewest: Needle's spiked ball in the air needs 50 on the top left's, which lost its bottom, and 48 on its best. The cells are drawn in OAM pieces as big as they can be (each piece's cells all with pixels, the biggest shapes first): the same tiles in fewer pieces, as KatAM's `DisplaySprite` takes its time per piece and NiDL's frames on four Kirbys made dozens each.
  - KatAM loads the hat sprite's palette from the ability's hat, so the poses use the colors that palette has free. Needle's hat leaves few, so its needles are a duller yellow than NiDL's.
- **Attacks** (`NidlAttackCreate`) are Burning's attack box: an object following Kirby while he's in the ability's state, with the hit's kind (`unk68`, as in `gUnk_082DDFC8`) and strength.

## Rooms

The rules every door, mirror, hub and transport follows are in [Map and Door Design Rules](../Documentation/Map%20and%20Door%20Design%20Rules.md): read them before changing how the map is laid out.

NiDL's level rooms (275, each one KatAM room) are converted from the player's NiDL ROM into KatAM's free room slots (rooms whose `gRoomProps` entry is all `0xFFFF`, ascending from 1), by `KatAMExpansionRooms.cs`, after the enemies and abilities ("Include Rooms"). `KatAMExpansion.PortedRooms` lists which NiDL room became which KatAM room, `PortedDoors` their doors. Left out: the levels' hub lists (hub, museum, arena, boss tower, boss room: no gameplay of their own), level 7's stage 1 (the boss rush's copies of the boss rooms) and stage 2 (11 unused test rooms), the 7 placeholder 2x2 hub entries, and 3 empty 16x11 floors no exit leads to (levels 2 and 3's stage 5: `(2,5,6)`, `(3,5,2)`, `(3,5,6)`). The boss arenas (each level's last stage) and the Fountain of Dreams are kept, for the bosses to come.

- **NiDL's rooms** (the same data in every region, only the table moves: USA `0x087E1D58`, Japan `0x087E60DC`, Europe `0x08BFD8E8`): per level (0-7), 8 stage lists (`table + level * 0x20 + stage * 4`), then the levels' hub lists (`table + 0x100 + level * 4`: hub, museum, arena, boss tower, boss room). Each list holds room records (null where a room is missing) until one whose index (`+2`) isn't its place. A record: `+8` the cells (LZ77, `u32` per 16x16 cell: metatile, attribute, whose high byte is the collision), `+0xC` the breakable blocks' numbers (LZ77, `u16` per cell), `+0x10` the object data (a `u32` per block number: the cell it leaves when broken, metatile and collision as the cells', until `0xFFFFFFFF`: mostly air, sometimes a wall, a floor, a ladder, water or the block again, which stays), `+0x14` width, height (cells), `+0x18` the foreground (palette: `u16` byte count, then the colors, loaded at palette 2; LZ77 tiles at `0x06004000`; LZ77 metatiles, 4 tilemap entries each), `+0x28` the background (palette loaded at palette 8; LZ77 tiles; the map: `u16` byte count, width, height, then `0xFFFF` and the LZ77 map in GBA screen block order (32x32 blocks, left to right, then top to bottom: 64x48 for the Fountain of Dreams'), or the map row by row), `+0x34` the background's scroll rates (x, y, 16ths), `+0x44` the exits (12 bytes: room, 0, door x, y in cells, landing x, y), `+0x48` the objects (8 bytes: category + 1, index, variant, flags, x, y) up to the record itself, `+0x50` where Kirby starts.
- **Collision**: the high nibble is the kind (0, 5, 7 solid; 1 one-way floors, `0x10` a door; 2 breakable blocks; 3 ladders and climbing walls; 4 air currents; 6 spikes; 8 and C water; 9 water with one-way floors; E spikes in water), the low nibble the shape in NiDL's height maps (`0x087328F0`: empty, full, 45° and gentle floors, ceilings). `RoomCollision` maps each value to KatAM's (`gCollisionAttributes`): full `0x0D`, floors `0x0B`/`0x0C` (45°) and `0x05`-`0x08`, ceilings `0x01`-`0x04`, `0x09`, `0x0A`, thin floors Kirby drops through with Down as in NiDL (`0x0E`, slopes `0x10`-`0x13`, `0x18`, `0x19`: KatAM's own one-way `0x0F`, `0x14`-`0x17`, `0x1A`, `0x1B` don't let him, so a way on down through one would be shut, as in Orange Ocean 3-2; but a room where, from each place Kirby arrives, dropping through leads to no door, warp star or cannon he doesn't reach from there anyway gets KatAM's (`ThinFloors()`, each arrival on its own: from all of them at once, a door one reaches counted for all, and Yogurt Yard's 0x23E shut its only way on down; Rainbow Resort's 0x304, where a bomb dropped Kirby through its platform), ladders (NiDL's top `0x31` KatAM's top `0x20`, a ledge Kirby stands on and climbs down from, its body `0x30` `0x21`: swapped, Kirby fell through the top and climbed on past it), spikes `0x1C`, water `0x48` and its slopes `0x34`-`0x43`, spikes in water `0x6D`. Breakable blocks become KatAM's special blocks (the solidity map's list: kind 1, value, x, y, the 4 tilemap entries left after, the collision left: NiDL's, from what the block leaves): star blocks `0xF0` (NiDL `0x24`; Rainbow Resort's few `0x20`), bomb blocks `0xF1` (`0x2E`), the blocks they set off `0xF2` (`0x2D`: touching the bomb block and each other, which KatAM breaks as a chain) and `0x2F` (NiDL's grey metal blocks) KatAM's silver metal blocks `0xF5` (as room 0x134's), which only some abilities break, as KatAM's own (tested: Hammer, Burning, Fighter, Wheel, Missile, Master, Smash's forward+B, and Stone from above; Throw with something held, by the owner; not Beam, Laser, Fire, Ice, Spark, Parasol, Sword, Cutter, Cupid or Bomb) (its `0xF4` blocks break with any ability's attack); a room with more than `MetalBlocks` (64) of them gets star blocks, but Heavy Mole's dirt is `0xF4` blocks (`DirtBlock`: any ability digs through it, Kirby can't inhale it). The door planner counts metal blocks as walls (`OpenCell()`), so no room needs an ability to be reached; what they guard is optional. NiDL's `0x34` is a thin floor too (clouds and bridges: Grape Garden's boss tower), but where a group of them touches a breakable block they're blocks a bomb block sets off (Ice Cream Island's sand path in stage 2, room 0xEF: it was solid, a softlock). NiDL's `0x35` cells touching a chain (and the `0x34` cells among them) are its invisible links: the chain goes on through them, as KatAM's own `0xF3` (not solid, with the chain attribute `0x40` that `code_0800E0E4.c` follows from block to block; each with a special block record that leaves the cell as it was), so one bomb block sets off all NiDL links to it (Rainbow Resort 3-4's ceiling and pits, 0x2FD; 3-7's goal door from the bottom, 0x304). Bomb blocks are only where NiDL has them (Kirby coming in on a chain's far side leaves by the two-way door he came through); a chain whose bomb block starts broken (a door lands Kirby there, 0x27C) or that has none is star blocks. Shotzos standing on breakable blocks are kept (NiDL's fall when the blocks go; KatAM's go with them), as 0x2FD's cannons under its ceiling. Air currents are plain air for now.
- **Graphics**: the foreground tiles are NiDL's own LZ77 (it decompresses them to VRAM too), at KatAM's `0x06008000`; its palettes 2-7 are KatAM's 0-5, so every tilemap entry's palette goes down by 2; the metatiles become KatAM's 8x8 tilemap (LZ77). The background keeps only the tiles its map uses, renumbered: KatAM's HUD tiles follow the background's 900 (most of NiDL's backgrounds use 900 or fewer; three use more: the Power Orb's sky 956, the Fountain of Dreams' 932, Nightmare's 908. Past 900, one at a time, the tile that changes the picture least, in its own colours and times how often it's drawn, shares the kept tile most like it, `BuildBackground()`: near copies among the stars go, where taking the least used first garbled whole rows drawn once, the foot of the orb sky's trees in its intro and the Fountain of Dreams' floor's last rows); its palettes 8-15 are KatAM's 6-13 (a shorter palette ends at palette 15, as NiDL loads it: Grape Garden 2's sky, 0x1B9-0x1BE, has 7 palettes and its map uses 9-15; read from 8, every tile took its neighbour palette's colours, `BackgroundPalette()`). Rooms sharing a background share its tiles and map, but each set of colours NiDL gives it is its own (`RecolourBackground()`: a copy of its `RoomTiledBG` with its own palette): eleven of NiDL's backgrounds come in two colourings, as Ice Cream Island's sea and Orange Ocean's at sunset (0x0F0 and 0x27B), and the later rooms showed the first's colours. It pans across the whole background once as the camera crosses the room, both ways (`BackgroundRates()`: `gRoomProps` `+0xE`, `+0x10`, 8.8; KatAM draws it at the camera's x times the rate from its left, and bottom-aligned at the camera's distance from the room's bottom times the rate, its camera showing x from 8 to the width - 8, y from its top to the height - 40): NiDL's own rates are for NiDL's camera, and KatAM repeats a background past its edge (room 0x74's showed its seam). A room the camera hardly moves in (less than `StillRange`, 48 pixels: one that fits the screen, or a screen and a bit) keeps its background still, as does a background no bigger than the screen. The Power Orb's room is NiDL's instead: its background is drawn at the camera's spot (both rates `0x100`, `+0xC` its height less the room's, so its top is the room's top; measured in NiDL, the background's row at the screen's top is the camera's y through the intro and the fight), so its cloud band rises under the room's starry sky (the foreground's, opaque, ending in a straight edge) as in NiDL; bottom-aligned, it came 176 pixels higher and the sky's edge cut off its dark top. NiDL's cloud bands also drift sideways, each at its own speed; the port's don't. The camera's top (`CameraTop()`, `gNidlRoomTops` in `rooms.c`, which `BigFillLevelInfo` applies to `unk_S32Vec2_6C`/`7C`) is the room's top, so NiDL's whole top row shows (KatAM's camera hides 8 pixels, cutting Vegetable Valley 3-4's trees, 0x9E), or a row down where NiDL's top row only repeats the one under it (4 cells or more, besides the row's sky: filler NiDL never shows), never lower than the camera can go; a split room's lower parts keep KatAM's 8, and so do the level hubs, the switch rooms (a screen's rooms: their cameras stay put as KatAM's hub rooms' do, where from the room's top they rose 8 pixels as Kirby flew up). (`gNidlRoomTops` holds a code, 0 KatAM's 8 pixels, 1 the room's top, 2 a row down: the converter stored pixels / 8 + 1, so a room meant a row down started two rows down, 0x147's top cut Kirby off, and a split room's lower parts started 16 pixels down.)
- **Objects**: enemies in both games become KatAM's (Waddle Dee, Noddy, Pengy, Laser Ball, Sir Kibble, Waddle Doo, Gordo, Bronto Burt, Bomber, Shotzo (NiDL `0x19` and `0x2A`), Sparky, Twister, Squishy, Scarfy, Hot Head, Glunk, Blipper, Sword Knight, Flamer, UFO; NiDL's headband Waddle Dee is a Waddle Dee), the others the ported ones (type `0x31`, NiDL's variant as the behavior's low nibble); mini-bosses (category 6, `Miniboss.NightmareIndex`: 0 Bonkers, 1 Poppy Bros. Sr., 2 Grand Wheelie, 3 Mr. Frosty, 4 Mr. Tick-Tock, 5 Bugzzy, 6 Fire Lion, 7 Phan Phan; KatAM's or the ported ones, with NiDL's variant when the port has it) and Meta Knight's event (category 7: the Meta-Knights, its variant the line-up) fight where NiDL puts them, on NiDL's screen (see **Mini-boss fights**); items (category 3: 1-Up, Maxim Tomato, Invincibility Candy, Energy Drink) become KatAM's 1UP, Maximum Tomato, Lollipop and Pep Brew. Warp stars, cannons and fuse lighters (gimmicks 4, 6, 2) become KatAM's (see **Doors**), and no enemy waits where a door lands Kirby. An enemy or item NiDL put inside a wall or on the top row stands in the first open cell below (up to 4), or is left out; a Shotzo on blocks that break is left out (NiDL's falls when they go, KatAM's would hang in the air: room 0x2FD). An enemy or item on the top row resting on a wall or blocks stays there, trapped as NiDL has it until the blocks go (room 0xFA's above its bomb chains); one inside a wall, or hanging from the ceiling, moves down to the first open cell. A Bomber's spot is the top of the floor under it (one-way floors skipped, up to 6 cells), as KatAM's own Bombers' are: `InitObject` looks for a floor once, at the template's spot, and `bomber.c` falls when there's none and blows up on landing, so from NiDL's spots (a cell's middle, 8 pixels up) every one went off before Kirby saw it. The randomizer does the same for an enemy it turns into a Bomber (a place in a cell's middle moves 8 pixels down). A walking enemy (Waddle Dee, Waddle Doo, Sir Kibble, Sword Knight, Hot Head...) over a fall starts on the floor below (up to 8 cells): spawned in the air it fell onto one-way floors it sank through (room 0x153's Waddle Doo, under its ceiling). Breakable blocks a door would land Kirby inside (NiDL's secret doors hidden in them, 0x294's chamber) start broken (`clearedBlocks`): KatAM lands him on the door, inside the ground. A room whose doors all lead to one room (a pocket off a stage, 0x278/0x279) has its last door thrown into another stage of its level instead, a branch. The other gimmicks and NiDL's big switches and camera locks (5: each level gets one KatAM big switch instead, in its switch room; index 0, a boss fight's camera lock, and index 2, a room's camera stop, place a fight's screen) aren't ported.
- **Chests** (`PlanChests()`, made with the rooms, whatever the randomizer does after): a few per level as KatAM has (a chest per 8 rooms, 2 to 5, and one in each dead end with nothing else in it, as 0x298; 30 in all), one a room at most, at a reward spot: a loose item behind breakable blocks, a dead end (a room with one way in and out), far from where Kirby comes in. A loose item there becomes a small chest (`0x80`) holding it (KatAM's chest items 0-5: 1UP, Invincible Candy, Maximum Tomato, Meat, Pep Brew, Cherry); a dead end with none gets a new chest, as far into the room as Kirby goes, holding KatAM's chest items in turn (KatAM's 41 consumable chests: 18 1UPs, 7 Pep Brews...). Never in a boss room (none in the Fountain of Dreams), a switch room, the NiDL hub or a mini-boss's fight area; always on a floor the camera shows. Their treasure indices (template `+0x11`) are 84-127, after KatAM's 84 chests (0-83), so each opens once and stays open; the completion counts only KatAM's (`NumChestsCollected` is redirected to `rooms.c`'s `NidlNumChestsCollected`). Each is in its room's doors list first (kind 1, its spot; the list's count, `+4`, its chests'): opening a chest looks itself up there (`sub_08002B30`, until it finds it), and `FinishRooms()` rebuilds those entries from the rooms' objects once the randomizer has moved, removed or emptied chests. `PortedItems` (the ported rooms' items and chests, but in boss rooms, switch rooms and mini-boss fight areas) is what the randomizer's ROM reader adds to KatAM's, and `DoorsTable` where the doors lists' table went (the randomizer's chest lists go there too).
- **Batteries** (object `0x62`, KatAM's own; `PlanChests()`): a battery per 8 rooms of a level (2 to 5; 19 in all), one a room at most and never in a chest's room, on the farthest floor behind breakable blocks, else the farthest of a dead end. They aren't in the doors lists or `PortedItems` (the randomizer leaves them be).
- **World slots past KatAM's** (`rooms.c`'s `BigGetStateSlot`, replacing `GetStateSlot`): KatAM's world state slots (`gUnk_02028C10`, 0x20, saved: its big switches 1-15, Central Circle's last mirror 0x12, Dark Meta Knight 0x13, the NiDL levels' 0x14-0x1B) leave 6 free; Squeak Squad's 8 worlds' big switches use 0x20-0x27, kept in the visited area before Squeak Squad's chests (`SQUEAK_WORLD_SIZE`), so they're saved with the visited doors.
- **Items are taken once per visit**: the ported rooms' items and batteries use a gone bit kind of their own, `NIDL_TAKEN_KIND` (3; template `+0x2`, its number in `+0x3`), a 0x20-byte table at the end of `rooms.c`'s visited area. `BigSub_08001678` sets and reads it as KatAM's global items' table (kind 1), and `BigSub_080027A8` clears it when KatAM clears that one (a new game, a game over), so eating one in a room and coming back doesn't bring it back, as KatAM's own.
- **Visited doors in the save** (`rooms.c`): the ported rooms' doors lists keep their visited bits (doors used, room seen) in `rooms.c`'s area after the EWRAM heap, not in `gVisitedDoors` (0x120 lists, all KatAM's); they're saved with each file's world props, in SRAM after KatAM's (its file info and 6 world props copies end at 0x1290): from 0x2000, a copy for each of KatAM's (3 files, 2 copies), 0x3E4 bytes (a magic word, a checksum, the bits). `WriteSaveSectionByOffset` and `VerifySaveByOffset` are detoured (`NidlWriteSave`, `NidlVerifySave`: a world props copy written or read, this one with it; a copy read without a good one of these clears the bits), and the world props' clear (`sbClearFunctions[1]`, run by `ClearSaveBuffer` and `InitSaveBuffers`) points to `NidlClearSave` (Skip the Tutorial's clear runs it in turn). KatAM's own copies stay as they are, so saves load across ROMs with and without the expansion (the ported rooms unvisited when the save has none). The items' taken bits aren't saved (KatAM's aren't: a visit clears them).
- **Beatable, with a clear route**: Central Circle's NiDL mirror leads to the NiDL hub, whose golden mirror (as Central Circle's to Rainbow Route, the one way in) leads to Vegetable Valley's first room; each level is laid out as KatAM's areas (see **Structure**), the last (boss) stage leading to the next level's first room; the Fountain of Dreams is the finale, reached through Central Circle's and the NiDL hub's Dimension Mirrors (once KatAM's 8 shards are found: the checks take its way in as open), the Power Orb's warp star to Nightmare's room, his end to the Fountain of Dreams hub, its Amazing Mirror out to KatAM's final boss (with the Squeak Squad ROM, into its final stage instead, Dark Nebula's end to the Dark Nebula hub, its Amazing Mirror out to KatAM's final boss); each level's switch room is reached from a spot hidden in it, and its big switch opens that level's door in the hub. Checked by flooding every room from where Kirby arrives (he flies: any cell that isn't solid, a star or bomb block, or a chain a reached bomb block sets off; never down into a one-way floor), following the doors and pressing the switches: every room is reached, every big switch pressed, and from every arrival the hub can be reached again; with no switch pressed (the hub's doors all hidden), every room is still reached from that one entry. What it took: thin floors Kirby drops through, NiDL's `0x34` clouds thin floors (Grape Garden's boss tower) or bomb chains (Ice Cream Island's), `0x2F` star blocks only where a room has more than 64 of them (Heavy Mole's dirt is solid to the checks: its room is entered at its pocket and left by its shard), a chain bordering an area none of its bomb blocks does gets a bomb block there (`RoomCells()`; a two-way door could bring Kirby in on the wrong side: Butter Building 1-1), a chain without a bomb block is star blocks (Rainbow Resort 3-7), and NiDL's rides (the revolving towers' warp star) land Kirby out of the chamber of walls they stand for (`Land()`).
- **Structure** (`PlanDoors()`, from `LayoutSeed`: the same in every expanded ROM), from KatAM's own map (280 rooms: 518 doors, 40% one-way, 28% between areas, about a loop per 7 rooms in each area, many rooms with 3 or more ways out, branches ending in goal mirrors, areas entered from 5 to 24 rooms of others) instead of NiDL's line of stages:
  - a level's regular stages are a tree from its first: every other stage is entered through a two-way branch door in a room of another (not its first room; deeper stages likelier; a stage branches 1 to 3 times, more the more rooms it has: big stages afford more branching paths), in a room's middle or hidden away (the farthest floor from where Kirby comes in, behind blocks he breaks), half and half, its way back in the first of the stage's rooms that takes a door (`EntrySpot()`); where no room takes both, from a stage further up, or one-way into the stage (`LandingIn()`), or another stage's goal throws Kirby in;
  - only the deepest stage's goal leads to the boss stage; the others' goals end their branch one-way, as KatAM's goal mirrors, in turn: into one of KatAM's goal games (rooms `0x3D4`-`0x3D6`, object `0x85`: the mirror shows KatAM's goal look by itself, `mirror_door.c`, and the game sends Kirby back to Central Circle after), into a stage of a level next to it, into a goal game again, or into a room of the KatAM area paired with the level (`KatAMArea()`: Vegetable Valley's is KatAM's area 5 ... Rainbow Resort's 11, by `gRoomProps` `+0`);
  - loops and links, few and each for a reason (KatAM connects sparingly, rewarding exploring over map bloat), all one-way throws, as KatAM's mirrors throw Kirby around: shortcuts home from the deepest stages (a third as many as the level has stages, at least 2: to a stage at least two branches shallower, else any shallower one but its own parent), a hidden door to the next level, and one throw into another level; each lands Kirby on a floor of a room of the stage away from its doors;
  - KatAM links: 2 a level with rooms of its KatAM area (rooms with few doors, far from them, without objects that own the room: big switches, scroll locks, bosses, hub doors, shards, goal stars): one throwing Kirby one-way from NiDL into the area, one from the area one-way into NiDL; the KatAM rooms get KatAM's mirror object (`FinishRooms()`). KatAM's own rooms keep every door and object they had (all 518 doors): the expansion only adds mirrors to them;
  - stages unlinked from themselves: pairs of NiDL's own two-way doors of two stages (a door and the one back) swap their far ends (a-b, c-d become a-d, c-b, still two-way), as many swaps a level as it has stages, one of them with the next level; not a door in a pocket Kirby is only in after coming in through it (it would lead only back where it came from: Ice Cream Island 4-5); A swap can leave rooms nothing leads into (a stage's far end leading only into another's): once every door is placed, the rooms Kirby reaches from the hub are flooded (`Reached()`: from each cell he arrives in, through open cells and breakable blocks, a bomb chain's only once he gets to its bomb block, as the logic checks have it, to the doors, warp stars and cannons there; not through KatAM's rooms: counting a chain's blocks as broken from either side, it took Grape Garden's first stage as reached through room 0x1B2, whose way back from 0x1C5 lands Kirby walled in by a chain with its bomb block outside, and once a change of fight areas moved the doors, no door led into that stage), and each group of rooms none leads into gets a one-way throw into its first room from a room reached, of the same level if one takes it; then every place Kirby gets to gets him back out: a place (a room's cells flooded from one arrival, through all but walls, never down through a thin floor, which needs Down and B) none of whose doors, warp stars and cannons lead out of NiDL's rooms (to the hub or KatAM's, which lead back) nor to a place that does gets a one-way throw out, from a spot in that place (`RoomSpot()`'s `within`), into the first stage of its level Kirby gets back from (else onto the hub's pink mirror): room 0x1BC, which 0x1B8's cannon shoots Kirby into, throws him back to Grape Garden's first stage. Door spots keep clear of warp stars and cannons as of doors (they're the ways on to other areas and secrets);
  - **Mirras** (object `0x34`), as few as KatAM's (9, rooms `0x75`, `0x8D`, `0xAF`, `0xD1`, `0xE4`, `0x263`, `0x2CF`, `0x32B`): one a level, on the way into its switch room (see **Switch rooms**), one-way, a room never holding two nor one leading to another, on a floor; KatAM's best Mirras are the ones never expected, and lead to big progress: a switch room is its level's door to the hub. It stands on the door's cell (x the cell's middle, y its top), the cell open until it's broken (hit it from afar: near Kirby's attacks it melts away, the decompilation's `mirra.c`), when its cell's special block record is applied (`sub_080025AC()`, `sub_08001408()`: kind 1, the cell's collision, its own tiles, then the door's value), and the door's mirror (subtype 1) shows. KatAM looks for that record and loops until it finds one, so a Mirra stands only on a door with it. It isn't remembered (the template's `+0x14` is 0, as KatAM's), so it's back each visit. Mirras fall (the expansion's `mirra.c`): one standing on breakable blocks (room `0xF6`'s star blocks, `0x248`'s bomb chain) drops once they break, onto what's under them, and its door opens there: `MirraLandings()` (in `BuildSolidity()`) builds a door at each cell below it where it can land (on what holds a falling object, `gCollisionAttributes`: solid, a floor, thin ones too, a slope; down to the first that can't break), the same door records as its own cell's (the cell as it is then: a broken block's look), marked `ML` after the door record's 14 bytes so the map viewer leaves them out, put before the room's other records (a broken block's own record stays its cell's last, as the logic checks read them), with a hidden mirror (subtype 1) on each in `BuildObjects()`;
  - KatAM's rules, each a lag or a mess in NiDL's rooms once (`MaxExits()`, `ViewFits()`, `Hosts()`): a room has 3 doors at most, 2 if small (2 screens or less), and the camera never shows 3 at once (no 3 within 15 x 10 cells); NiDL's own rooms of doors (Butter Building's tower floors, Rainbow Resort's and Orange Ocean's rooms of doors) keep theirs past that as NiDL's doorways, drawn in their tiles, without mirrors (`Door.Plain`); no door is added in a mini-boss's fight area, nor a two-way one in its room; a goal room keeps its goal mirror alone: its doors back towards its stage's start go, the doors into it there become one-way (NiDL's goal rooms that lead on keep those doors); boss stages, switch rooms and split rooms' parts take none. Every spot is a floor cell the camera shows a mirror on, on a floor at least 3 cells wide (Kirby landing on a ledge 2 wide drops off it), with room above, not water, 8 cells from other doors and from where other doors land Kirby (4 in small rooms, when nothing else is left), reached from where Kirby comes into the room. A door is two-way when the door it lands Kirby on leads back onto it (NiDL's over a fall: the door beside the landing back beside it), and drawn as KatAM's one-way mirror otherwise (template `+0x22` `0x100`, `mirror_door.c`); a one-way door never drops Kirby on a door nor in front of a two-way one (4 cells across, 2 up or down): its landing moves to a floor nearby (`Clear()`). No enemy stands within 3 cells across (2 up or down) of where a door lands Kirby (`BuildObjects()`: KatAM lands him on the door, NiDL beside it). The logic check counts a door into KatAM's rooms or a goal game as a way back to the hub (KatAM's map leads to Central Circle). `fix/rules.py`-style checks: 8 Mirras in 8 rooms, no room over its exits, no 3 doors in a view, every two-way door leading back (USA: about 630 doors, a third one-way).
- **Mini-boss fights**: NiDL holds the camera on the fight's screen and its edges are walls until the mini-boss is beaten. KatAM's arenas are rooms a screen big, so nothing in KatAM does that: each NiDL mini-boss gets its screen as its template's area (`MinibossScreen()`, where NiDL's camera is held in that fight, measured in NiDL for every one: sideways, the room's camera stop (category 5, index 2: NiDL's camera goes no further right than its x, the screen's left, and stays there once the fight is on), else the middle of a room up to 18 cells wide, and none in a wider one; up and down, the camera follows Kirby in a room up to 12 cells tall (8 pixels from its top to 8 from its bottom: Fire Lion's pit, the Meta-Knights' rooms), and in a taller one shows the screen on the mini-boss's floor; inside what KatAM's camera shows). Before, it was the screen around the room's index-0 camera lock, which in most rooms is only the trigger NiDL's camera reacts to, and fights locked mid-room (Poppy Bros. Sr.'s in 0x07C 49 pixels right of NiDL's, Fire Lion's in 0x242 and 0x244 48 and 56 off) and, before it, an arena object (type `0x31`, behavior `0xF0`: `enemies/arena.c`, unseen and untouchable). Once a Kirby is on that screen while a mini-boss (types `0x38`-`0x52`) is alive in the room, the arena holds the camera there as KatAM's scroll locks do (type `0x77`, `scroll_lock.c`: the level's camera bounds, `unk_S32Vec2_7C`/`84` and `unkA0`-`unkAC`, the camera's what it may show, the fight's screen, so it stays put as KatAM's 15x10 scroll locks do, and Kirby's that plus 39 pixels below, so he falls off its pits (the camera's used to have the 39 pixels too, and drifted down past the room's bottom rows during fights), eased in by `sub_0811C8BC`'s first-frame clamps) and keeps every Kirby in the room 8 pixels inside it; when none is left it puts the bounds back (`ScrollLockDestroy`) and goes away. After a boss's fight (`NidlIsBoss()`) the bounds it puts back are its own screen's, so the camera stays where the fight had it, as NiDL's (the room's put it 16 pixels higher, KatAM's rows padded below, as Whispy Woods was beaten, and King Dedede's put it over the room's top where his drop-in lock had ridden, Kirby held in the air under it, his shard's dance never ending); boss rooms have no door out, the shard is the way out. Kracko's still lets go once no Kirby is on its screen. In a screen's room (16x11 cells: Meta Knight's, Mr. Shine & Mr. Bright's and the 16x11 mini-boss rooms) NiDL's camera never moves from (8, 8), so there the arena's template has subtype2 2 (`OneScreen()`): it holds the camera right on its screen (not easing there) whenever a Kirby is in the room, before the fight, through it and after it, its walls only while the fight is on (KatAM's room is taller, padded below, and its camera sank 16 pixels with Kirby: Meta Knight's at 24). While the mini-boss is alive, the doors on its screen and a cell around it (up to 4) are shut: their cells aren't doors (`0xFF`, `0xFD` in water: air, water, through `sub_0800255C`), and their mirrors (subtype 1, `BuildObjects()`: the same cells) hide with them; they open once it's beaten (the room's collision comes back fresh when it's left).
- **KatAM's tables** (per version, `KatAMExpansionCode`'s `RomSymbols`): `gForegroundInfo`, `gForegroundPalettes`, `gForegroundTilemaps`, `gSolidityMaps`, `gRoomTiledBGs`, `gLevelObjLists`, `gUnk_08D63C28` (CPU helpers' zones: one zone per room) and `gUnk_08D640A4` (doors per `doorsIdx`) are copied with the new entries and every literal pointing at them repointed (each copied up to the last entry a room's props name, but `gRoomTiledBGs` whole, its 36 entries: KatAM's code loads entry 34, the credits' second background, itself for Dark Mind's last room, 0x397, and a NiDL background there garbled that fight and broke its screen on unpausing); `gRoomProps` and `gUnk_08D6CD0C` (per room: routes, area; area `0x0A` as KatAM's special rooms) get the new rooms in their free entries. Foreground tiles, palettes and backgrounds are shared between rooms as in NiDL. Song: one KatAM song per NiDL level. Flags `0x79` (as KatAM's own rooms: the phone works).
- **Limits**: KatAM's rooms have at most 1950 blocks (16x16): each loaded room (up to 4 at once: the player's and the CPU helpers', one slot each, `gCurLevelInfo[].unk65E`) has its collision, CPU helpers' zones and tilemap in buffers that big (`gUnk_02024ED0[4][1950]`, `gUnk_02026D60[4][1954]`, `gUnk_02028EE0[4]`: 1950 * 4 tilemap entries). `rooms.c` gives each slot room for 3584 blocks (NiDL's biggest, Grape Garden 3-2's 120x28 room, is 3480 padded): slots 0 and 1 in those buffers (they hold two each), slots 2 and 3 at the end of the EWRAM heap (`gEwramHeap`, 128 KB; `EwramInitHeap`'s size, `0x20080`, becomes `0xE878`, 58 KB: the game uses 1-2 KB of it, at most 1.5 KB measured in a Meta-Knights fight and the pause menu). Every function using the buffers (all decompiled: `FillLevelInfo`, which loads a room into its slot, `GetCollisionTile` and the other collision reads, `sub_0800255C`, `sub_080025AC`, `sub_08002624`, `sub_08001408`, `sub_080015A8`, `sub_080026A8`) jumps to `rooms.c`'s copy, the same code with the new buffers (`KatAMExpansionRooms.BigRooms()`: `bx pc` to an ARM `ldr ip`/`bx ip`, which keeps the 4 argument registers). The room tables `FillLevelInfo` reads are the moved ones, so the expansion's code's literals are repointed too. So no NiDL room is split. The objects a room keeps gone are 32 bits per slot (`gUnk_02023358`), so a room has at most 31 objects (27 in the biggest). Every new room uses `doorsIdx` 287, the one `gVisitedDoors` entry (`0x120` of them) no vanilla room uses.
- **Animated tiles** (`TileAnimations`): water, waterfalls and the like are NiDL foreground tiles its scripts copy anew: the room record's `+0x40` picks a set (0 none) in the table at the room table `+0x1C8` (every region), a null-terminated list of scripts (8 bytes per step: u16 command, u16 parameter, u32 argument; 0 shows a frame for the parameter's frames, 3 loops, 4 stops); a frame is u16 its first foreground tile, u16 its bytes, the raw tiles. They become KatAM's (`ForegroundInfo` `+4` source base, `+8` list, `+0xC` count; per animation: VRAM offset from `0x06008000`, bytes per copy, stride, steps of {source, frames}, how many, copies), the frames copied once. Not ported: the scripts' palette animations (command 1) and their other commands (2, 6).
