- **Camera**: KatAM's camera keeps its view between (8, 8) and (width - 8, height - 40) (`unk_S32Vec2_6C`/`74`, set from the room's size when it loads): its rooms hide 40 pixels below what's seen, and 8 on the other sides. NiDL's don't, so each room gets its last row repeated once below (the camera then stops 24 pixels above the NiDL room's bottom, 2 rows higher than NiDL's, so the repeated row never shows), and its last column and row repeated up to 16x13 cells (smaller rooms put the sprites' viewport off the room while the background stays on it, and the two drift apart). The padding's breakable blocks are solid (KatAM looks a breakable block's special block record up and would never find one). The splits count the padding.
- **Doors** (`PlanDoors`): KatAM's doors are a collision value (`gCollisionAttributes` with `0x4000`: `0xFF` a door, `0xFD` one in water; `0xFE`, `0xFC` an edge Kirby goes through as he touches it) and an 8ROM record in the room's special blocks (kind 3, the value, x, y, size 14, `0xFFFF`, `u16` room, x, y, facing (1 right, 2 left, `0x80` no respawn), `0xFF`): Kirby lands at x * 16 - 8, y * 16 - 7, so the record holds the landing cell + 1. KatAM also looks every door Kirby goes through up in the room's 9ROM list (`gUnk_08D640A4[doorsIdx]`: 8 bytes each, kind 2, size 8, `0xFFFF`, room, x, y; `0x0000FFFF` ends it) and loops until it finds it, so a door missing there hangs the game: each ported room has a list of its own (`doorsIdx` `FirstDoorsList` + its number, `0x120` on: one shared list made every mirror scan hundreds of entries every frame, and rooms with mirrors lagged; the NiDL hub's is 287, the one `gVisitedDoors` entry no vanilla room uses), its doors' destinations, whose count byte is 0 (the map counts none). `gVisitedDoors` (a `u16` per list, `0x120` of them, saved) keeps which of a list's doors were used: `rooms.c` keeps the ported rooms' after the EWRAM heap instead (`NIDL_VISITED`, not saved; the heap shrinks by its 1 KB), in its copies of the functions reading and setting them (`sub_08002A5C`, `sub_08002A80`, `sub_08002AD0`, `sub_08002B30`, `sub_08002BA8`, `sub_08002C18`). A mirror (object `0x6F`, at the cell's center) stands on each door. NiDL's secret doors (14, behind a star, bomb or chain block whose broken metatile is the door) stay that block, which leaves the door's collision when broken (the door's 8ROM record goes before the blocks' records: a later record of a cell is what the cell is at load), and their mirror is KatAM's hidden kind (subtype 1, `CreateMirrorDoor`: unseen while its cell isn't a door, then it shows with a sparkle). They're made from NiDL's exits (12 bytes: room, kind, `u16` x, y, landing x, y in pixels):
  - kind 0, a door to a room of the same stage: where it was, to where it led;
  - kind 30, a warp star or cannon (x: the room): KatAM's own object where NiDL's gimmick stands (`Transport`, `BuildObjects()`). A warp star (object `0x84`, `warp_star.c`: `+0x1E` the room, `+0x1A`, `+0x1C` the landing + 1) to where Kirby starts that room (NiDL's landing is in the air past a fall), on a floor above the room's hidden bottom rows (`Land()`: else the nearest such floor; room 0x150's star landed Kirby in a pit in 0x152's hidden rows, where he died and came back forever); warp stars stand where NiDL's do (a cell's middle: they float), cannons on their fuse as KatAM's own (`TransportSpot()`: rooms 0x7E, 0x143, 0x1F6, 0x33B put a cannon at the right edge of the fuse cell its rope enters, on that cell's top), so their cell is (y - 1) / 16; its flight (the subtype) its own, added to KatAM's six (`gUnk_08D60A84`, moved, its pointer with it; `WarpFlights()`): each of KatAM's is Kirby's speed frame by frame (`sub_080534D0`: 12-byte steps, x and y speed, frames, animation 90 on the star, an effect, flags `0x40` a room change, `0x80` the end), up into the sky, then to the room (the step flagged `0x40`: he arrives where `gUnk_0893CB44` says for that room), then along a path drawn for that one room, which took him out of NiDL's. A ported star flies NiDL's own ride instead (`NidlRides`, recorded from NiDL frame by frame; the 6 of the 7 warp stars Kirby gets on there): NiDL's star, as Kirby gets on, jumps 16 pixels up, bobs 32 down under him, and is off along its own path (each star runs a script of its own in NiDL) out of the room, through the sky rooms its ride goes through (each entered with a room change marked for `warpstar.c`, then a frame to where NiDL's star comes in), into the room it lands in, where it dives in; there the orb bursts where the star goes and Kirby tumbles off as NiDL's (up 4 pixels a frame, slowing, then down, one bounce: drawn with KatAM's hop-off animations, 74-89 then 20) to where NiDL's Kirby stands, the flight ending there (`warpstar.c`). Moving 2 pixels a frame or more, the star sparkles (effect 12). Before each room change it flies its moves in NiDL's 18-frame fade, with KatAM's own fade to white started on the first (effect 0, `sub_0803CA20`, as KatAM's flights start it before their room change: the next room loads under the white). NiDL draws Kirby on its star as one 32x32 sprite centred on the star, as KatAM draws its rider on Kirby, so the star's spots are Kirby's. The star grows as Kirby gets on by its frames (frame 13: the 32x32 rider); Ice Cream Island 2's and Orange Ocean 3's rides also shrink it with Kirby as they fly off into the distance (affine, NiDL's sizes frame by frame: `NidlRides` "scale"), drawn so by `warpstar.c` from the size each step carries (a step carries the size of the frame before it, as KatAM draws Kirby after his step moves on). Grape Garden 3's star (stage 3-2, room 2) has no ride of NiDL's (Kirby couldn't get on it there): it flies `WarpFlight`, flight 0 up to its room change, then he lands where the star says and hops off. Every Kirby on the star (the helpers riding with him) takes the same flight. A cannon (object `0x8A`, `kirby_cannon.s`, as room `0x1F6`'s: `+0x12` the room, `+0x18` the landing x << 8 \| y, `+0x14` 2, `+0x22` 2) shoots Kirby to the top of the room NiDL's exit lands him in, once its fuse burns to it: NiDL's fuse lighter (gimmick 2) becomes KatAM's fuse object (`0x78`, `cannon_fuse.c`, lit by fire), and NiDL's fuse cells (`0x38`-`0x3D` inside walls) KatAM's (`FuseCollision`, `0x22`-`0x27`, solid), each with two special block records (`BuildSolidity()`: the nearest NiDL wall's metatile, which the fuse applies as it burns, then its own rope, when lit again);
  - kind 34, the stage's goal door: to the next stage's start (where Kirby starts, `+0x50`, dropped to the floor);
  - kind 26, out of the room past its edge (x: the room, `0xFFFF` none; the sky Kirby falls from in 1-2, the towers he climbs out of): edges on the top and bottom rows' open cells;
  - kind 21, the secret doors to the hub's museum and arena: not ported;
  - the boss stages (each level's last, and the Fountain of Dreams): their boss rooms have no doors (the shard takes Kirby to the NiDL hub, onto the level's door; the Fountain of Dreams' bosses leave none, see Bosses); a room before the boss gets a mirror where Kirby lands, on to the stage's next room; one-way, as KatAM's boss rooms: no mirror back into a boss stage, nor out of one;
  - a room bigger than rooms.c's slots would be split, its parts leading into each other through the open cells on their shared edge (none is now).
  - two-way, as KatAM's: NiDL's own doors are pairs, and one whose landing has no mirror back within 2 cells (NiDL's few one-way doors) gets one there, or on the first free floor cell beside it (up to 10 away, no other mirror within 3 cells: none side by side; the mirror then lands there; with no floor, the landing itself, in the air, if no mirror is that close), where the room takes it (its exits, the camera, not a goal room); a room with a door back already, elsewhere, gets no second one, and a door next to one to the same room (NiDL's doors 2 cells wide) is one mirror; a mirror with one back lands Kirby on it (NiDL's doors land him beside theirs: up to 2 cells across, 3 up or down). Left one-way: NiDL's own exits that land on another door or a fall edge, and the boss stages';
  - every mirror where the camera shows it whole (`Shown()`: 2 cells tall, rows 2 to the room's height - 4, columns 1 to its width - 2; KatAM's camera shows from 8 pixels in from a room's sides and top to 40 above its bottom): one past moves in through open cells (NiDL's doors on a room's top row; four stand on a ledge there, their frame's top 8 pixels unseen);
  - every mirror looks its door up in its room's 9ROM list every frame (`sub_08002BA8`, from the list's start), so each list holds its room's doors alone, mirrors first, edges last.
- **The way in**: Central Circle (`0x321`) gets a mirror on the left end of its platform (cell 11, 14: its tilemap gets the right one's tiles, flipped) to the NiDL hub, recolored pink (`EntryMirrorColor`: its gold, orange and brown to pink; new tiles in the ones neither its tilemap nor its special blocks use, each in the palette that shows it best, the pinks in the colors no tile uses nor its palette cycles change: `ForegroundPalette_4pp`, `unk4[1]` the first color, `unk8` how many). Central Circle's collision, objects, 8ROM and 9ROM list are what the randomizer writes, so `KatAMExpansion.FinishRooms` adds the door after it's done, into copies that table entries reserved by `AddRooms` point to; its 9ROM list keeps its count, so the map's doors don't change. The KatAM rooms the NiDL levels lead to get their doors back the same way (`EditedRoom`).
- **Areas**: each level is an area of its own (`gUnk_08D6CD0C` `+0x46`: 11 + the level), its rooms numbered from its hundred (the NiDL hub and the Fountain of Dreams 1-99, Vegetable Valley 100-199 ... Rainbow Resort 700-799). The HUD's area plates (`AreaPlates`, per language: 0x200 bytes per area from `+0x5300`) and the CPU helpers' routes (`gUnk_08D6DCAC`) are copied with the new areas; the plates' names are composed from the letters of KatAM's English plates. Every NiDL room, the switch rooms and the NiDL hub included, has room `0x397`'s CPU helper entry (the Dimension Mirror's): no routes (`+0`, and `+0x48` their count: 0) and flags `0x81` (`+0x47`: a roaming helper doesn't go through a door into such a room, and one in it, called there with the phone, heads back to the room it came from). The routes only know KatAM's rooms: a helper in the NiDL hub with Central Circle's routes (the hub's entry was once a copy of Central Circle's) looked for its way to Kirby through doors the hub hasn't, got room 0xFFFF, and loaded it, jumping into garbage (`sub_08013F48`, `sub_080141EC`). The Map Viewer's stage and room number go in the entry's padding (`+0x4A`, `+0x4B`), not `+0x48`.
- **The NiDL hub** (`KatAMExpansionHub.cs`): Central Circle again (its props, tiles, collision and CPU helpers' zones), at night. As in Central Circle, its golden mirror (`MainHubEntry`, 19, 14: Central Circle's to Rainbow Route) is the way into the levels, to Vegetable Valley's first room (onto a mirror back there); a pink twin on the platform's left end (`MainHubReturn`, 11, 14, drawn as Central Circle's, `MirrorTwin()`, in palettes 3-5) leads back to Central Circle's, and Kirby arrives on it. Central Circle's Dimension Mirror (its mirror shard object `0x65` of subtype 8, `CreateShard`: whole once the 8 shards are found, and the way in then) stands under its arch as there, leading to the Power Orb's room as Central Circle's own now does (the finale: its template's `+0x1E` the room, `+0x1A`, `+0x1C` the landing + 1, written in place in Central Circle's, `FinaleMirrors()`). Under it, where Central Circle has the Master Sword's stand, the Star Rod stands in the golden pedestal the hub has from Central Circle's tiles, there once the game is beaten (`RodStandTemplate()`: object `0x9A`, see Abilities). Its background is NiDL's final boss background (the Fountain of Dreams' first room's: stars, the pink and cyan nebula, the violet sky): its rows 296-376, 376-520 and 520-584 (the sky and galaxies; its hills and trees are left out) stacked into 32x36 tiles, each of 3 bands of rows quantized to 15 colors (palettes 6-8, k-means), its tiles deduplicated; its scroll rates (props `unk0E`, `unk10`, 8.8) are set so its stars show at the room's top (KatAM aligns a background's bottom with the room's), its y rate for the camera's whole climb (KatAM's camera stops 40 pixels above the room's bottom: at the top the background stood above its first row, and KatAM wraps that at 65536, not at its 288 rows, a garbled band at the screen's bottom). Central Circle's scenery (palettes 4 and 5) is tinted to the night. Each level has a door where one of Central Circle's is (special blocks, kind 1, that its hub mirror objects `0x8C` break once their world slot is set, drawing the door over the 3x3 cells above its door cell; hidden until the level's big switch is pressed, all of them, as KatAM's), in one of KatAM's door designs Central Circle doesn't use (`NidlDesigns`: the frames of object `0x97`'s door, anim `0x2C6`, variants 11, 3, 5, 7, 13, 2, 6 and 1 (none a KatAM room a level's door stands in already has: Yogurt Yard's, beside 0x2B2's door to Cabbage Cavern West, isn't its 4), over its mirror, anim `0x2C5`, decoded from `gSpriteTables` and drawn as foreground tiles). A door's tiles are written into the tiles Central Circle's tilemap doesn't use (KatAM's foreground tiles end where the BG maps start, `0x0600E000`, 768 tiles, as many as Central Circle has), its colors quantized to 15 in a palette of its own: palettes 1 and 2 (Central Circle's doors used them), 0 and 9-13 (the background's palette block carries them).
- **Switch rooms** (`BuildSwitchRooms`): each level's entry, laid out as KatAM's rooms with 2 hub doors (`LayoutB`): A as `0x122` (hub doors top left and right, the big switch on a wide platform; Vegetable Valley, Butter Building, Yogurt Yard, Rainbow Resort, the Fountain of Dreams) or B as `0x250` (hub doors top and bottom middle, the bottom one in a pit, the big switch on a raised middle with ledges; Ice Cream Island, Grape Garden, Orange Ocean; Ice Cream Island's ledges and pedestal are one floating platform, so all of it is a thin floor Kirby jumps up through, `ThinPlatforms`: the ledges ones he drops through too, 0x0E, kept from `ThinFloors()`, the pedestal KatAM's one-way floor, 0x0F, the big switch put 4 pixels above it so it lands on it, as a one-way floor only holds what comes down on it: from its top, its feet on it, it fell through to the ground), in the level's own look: its first stage's tileset, or, where that's mostly sky (under 27% ground: Grape Garden's clouds, Yogurt Yard's and Rainbow Resort's skies), the one in 2 rooms or more with the most ground times the root of its rooms (Grape Garden's castle, Yogurt Yard's purple caves); the Fountain of Dreams' as below. Its tiles are copied from the level's own stages, not guessed: each cell takes the metatile of a cell in one of the level's rooms with that tileset that has the same shape of ground around it (5x5 cells: ground, air, one-way floor; past a room's edge ground; plain ground only, 0x01 and Rainbow Resort's ice 0x70, never next to water, slopes, doors, ladders or blocks where it can help it) and the same metatiles as its neighbours already taken, so whole stretches of the level's walls, floors, corners and fills carry over as the stage draws them. It's drawn after one room of the level first, its model (`ModelRanks`, picked by eye per level from rooms ranked by how many of the switch room's shapes they have, in the switch room's palette first: each room has its own look, Yogurt Yard's is its sixth stage's second room), others only for shapes the model hasn't; and as the level uses each metatile: a grass top where there's air above it, not under more ground; not rock the level draws under water; deep inside the ground (2 rows under a floor's top) the level's usual fill, the row under a floor's top what the level draws there (Vegetable Valley's dirt under its grass); what most of its rooms draw over what one draws once. Small things standing alone on a level's floors (a flower, a crystal) stand on the main floor, off the doors, mirrors and pedestal. Pieces join as the level joins them: a pair of metatiles it never draws side by side (or one above the other) costs, and so do edges where one is drawn and the other not (seams). The room is symmetric as KatAM's hub rooms (both layouts, B's pedestal 4 cells wide and its walls alike, its doors drawn at x 128; A's walls end above the floor mirrors): one half as above, the other its exact mirror image, each metatile there the tileset's one drawing its pixels flipped, else one made for the room (its 4 tiles swapped and drawn flipped, appended to the room's own copy of the tileset's metatiles: the converter reads each room's table through its record). Their dressing needn't be symmetric: `SwitchDecorations` puts the stages' own decorations (flowers, boulders, palms...) in air cells after the room is mirrored, each side its own (blocks `x y rows`; `!` first: over the terrain too, its collision kept, as flowers in grass), and `UnmirroredFill` levels keep the ground's inside unflipped across the room (its texture runs on, only the edges mirror). Rainbow Resort's is drawn in its last stage's original Game Boy tiles (`SwitchLook`: a level's switch room in a room's tileset picked by eye), design E (after that stage's fifth room) with its flower-grass floor, the Game Boy forest of its second room behind it: canopy, a pale trunk each side (the left the right one's mirror image) and thin dark trunks between, down to the floor, with tall grass and leaves along it. Grape Garden's has no ledges beside its pedestal (`NoLedges`) and its first stage's castle keep behind it, spire to walls, with its clouds on the side floors (`ForeignDecorations`: that room's own tileset; with more colour rows than are free, a row shares the room's nearest). Rooms picked by eye are kept as they are (`SwitchExtraGround` can add ground cells beyond the layout's; `SwitchDesigns`: the left half's metatiles row by row, `~` flipped left to right, `^` upside down, `@` both): Ice Cream Island's (after its third stage's third room, its grass ends curling under as its stages', the ledges and pedestal one floating platform with rounded ends, red flowers left and blue right, sand hills in the pit, a palm leaning in from the right, its cobbles not mirrored), Vegetable Valley's (built from its raised hedges, as its stages have no ceilings: their rounded tops upside down make the ceiling and the wall ends, their sides the walls, the joins at their feet turned round the inner corners; its first stage's pedestal, its second stage's dirt on the side floors, dressed as its stages: grass along the floors, flower beds, white tusks framing the big switch), Yogurt Yard's (after its sixth stage's second room, its fills and moss joining best, its brown boulders different on each side: one clinging to the left wall, a cluster in the right ceiling corner, a heap by the left mirror against the pedestal's curved corner, a pile on the pedestal, a small heap against its other side), Butter Building's (after its sixth stage's eighth room, its floor after its first stage's third, its first stage's skyline behind as it is there, its columns 14-25, 2 cells right so its tall tower goes on behind the right wall: a railing, a tall pale pillar, crenellated towers, the walkways across them and the small tower's piece left of the pillar left out) and Orange Ocean's (after its fifth stage's first). The Fountain of Dreams' takes Grape Garden's castle in its sixth stage's dusk colours (`FountainLook`), the castle's circle pillars as its walls' inner faces, dressed in King Dedede's arena's light (lines under the ceiling, a cyan streak left, a pink one right: `ForeignDecorations`, `*` metatiles from another room, their tiles appended to the room's own and their colours put in colour rows the room's metatiles don't use), and layout A without the platform in the middle, so the Fountain behind it shows whole: King Dedede's arena's background (`FountainInBack`, rows 15-34, a column left: the Fountain's middle at x 127, the room's middle as the camera shows it). Rows 0-11: the floor's top on row 9 and ground under it, so KatAM's padding (13 rows) repeats ground. `SWITCH_ROOM_DUMP=<dir>` writes each switch room's metatiles and its metatile table, `SWITCH_ROOM_MODEL_RANK=<n>` draws them all after the n-th ranked room (-1: every room alike) and `SWITCH_ROOM_MODEL=<record>` after one room, `SWITCH_ROOM_NO_DESIGNS=1` ignores `SwitchDesigns`, `SWITCH_ROOM_DESIGN_<level>=<rows split by />` tries one `SWITCH_ROOM_DECOR_<level>=<blocks split by ;>` tries decorations and `SWITCH_ROOM_UNMIRRORED_<level>=1` an unmirrored fill, `SWITCH_ROOM_FOUNTAIN=<record>` and `SWITCH_ROOM_FOUNTAIN_BACK=0|1` try other looks for the Fountain of Dreams', `SWITCH_ROOM_LOOSE=1` drops the adjacency and seam costs: to compare by eye. It's reached one-way from a Mirra hidden in the level, as KatAM's big switches are found by exploring (`SecretSpot()`: in the second half of its stages, not the boss's, the room farthest from its stage's start by doors, the floor cell there farthest from where Kirby comes in, best behind blocks he must break; where the camera shows a mirror, no door within 2 cells, not water, in a room that takes a door), landing Kirby off its floor mirrors (layout A on the pedestal's left end, B 2 cells right of the left mirror); its 2 floor mirrors (KatAM's switch rooms have one on each side) are its 2 ways into the level, both two-way, so the level branches from its hub: the right one to the start of the stage leading to its boss (a shortcut for finding the room), the left one to the start of a stage on another branch (the deepest off the way to the boss's, not the Mirra's); a hub's doors are always two-way, and none leads to a Mirra; two ability statues stand on its top row as in KatAM's hub rooms (objects `0x92`-`0x95`, `SwitchStatues`, the level's abilities). The big switch (object `0x86`: `+4` the world slot, 0x14 + the level; `+0x14` what pressing it runs, see the world map) reveals its 2 hub doors (object `0x97`, as KatAM's: it breaks its cell's special block, making the door, and shows its mirror once the slot is set, in the design of where it leads: `LinkDesign`): `SwitchHubDoor` back to the NiDL hub (Kirby arrives on it from the hub) and `SwitchLinkDoor` where the level's world map path leads (`LevelLinks`, chains of at most 2 links): Vegetable Valley to Rainbow Route East `0x316`, Ice Cream Island to the Moonlight Mansion door `0xBE`, Butter Building to Rainbow Resort's switch room, Rainbow Resort to the NiDL hub (its hub door), Grape Garden to Peppermint Palace East `0x122`, Yogurt Yard to Cabbage Cavern West `0x250`, Orange Ocean to Cabbage Cavern East `0xBF` (KatAM's rooms of its world map doors are `sWorldMapDoorRoomIds`'). The Fountain of Dreams' switch room, the finale's end, has none of these: no Mirra, no floor mirrors, no hub doors (the NiDL hub's door to it and its world map path never open) and no big switch; Kirby comes in only from Nightmare's end, onto the floor's left, and its only way out is an Amazing Mirror in the middle of the room (as the camera shows it, high up under the ceiling, above the Fountain's bowl, where the other switch rooms' ability statues stand; a copy of Central Circle's Dimension Mirror as KatAM has it, to `0x38D`, KatAM's final boss, whole once the 8 shards are found; Kirby floats up into it); under it, on the raised platform at the room's bottom middle, KatAM's Master Sword stand (object `0x98`, Central Circle's, 16 pixels over the platform as there: it gives Master once the game is beaten, or from the start with "Unlock the Master Sword", whose patch is in the stand's own code), a Maximum Tomato on the floor's right, where Kirby lands on its left (its glint off, template `unk22` 4: with the statues in the room KatAM's food glint, `sub_0809513C`, showed as a door's blue ring around it), and two ability statues on its sides, the owner's: Cutter on the left, Sword on the right (object `0x95`), as high as the other switch rooms' (48) where layout A's hub doors stand in the others, cells 3 and 12 (`BuildObjects()`, `FinaleMirrorAt`, `FinaleSwordAt`, `FinaleTomatoAt`, `FinaleStatues`). Both ends get a door, as KatAM's paths (`KatAMLinkDoors`, the level's slot and design): Rainbow Resort's link door leads back to Butter Building's; the KatAM rooms get theirs where KatAM's hub doors are, every room kept symmetric as KatAM's own (`0x122`: doors at cells 3 and 12, statues at x 96 and 160 between them): `0x316` and `0xBE` take `0x122`'s layout, the level's door at cell 3 and Central Circle's moving from the middle to cell 12 (`MovedDoors`: its collision, special block records and door object move, where it leads stays, and every door landing on it, Central Circle's, has its landing moved with it in both its 8ROM record and its 9ROM entry, `LandsOn()`), their statues to 96 and 160 (`DecorationMoves`); `0x122`'s stands in the middle (cell 8, drawn at x 128: `DoorDrawX`, Kirby landing on its floor, off its big switch); `0xBF`'s stands beside its own on the lower floor (cell 5; cell 11 was the Fountain of Dreams', whose links are gone), and `0x250` takes that layout, its own lower door (to Mustard Mountain's hub `0x2B2`, whose door lands on it) moving from under its big switch to cell 11, the level's at cell 5. A level's door is never in a design its KatAM room already has (Yogurt Yard's isn't `0x250`'s 5).
- **World map** (`KatAMExpansionWorldMap.cs`, `worldmap.c`): pressing a big switch runs `gUnk_08357B8C[template +0x14]`, which runs `sub_08039600(9 + door)` and so `gUnk_0834BD94[9 + door]` (`WorldMapUnlock*`: `CreateWorldMap(door)`, the animation drawing the door's path dot by dot, then `WorldMapUnlockSave` sets its slot). Both tables are copied with 8 more entries (`gUnk_08357B8C[17 + level]`: a thunk calling `sub_08039600(27 + level)`; `gUnk_0834BD94[27 + level]`: `NidlWorldMapUnlock<level>`, `CreateWorldMap(16 + level)`), and the tables the animation indexes by door (`sWorldMapLineCoors`, its dot counts, `gWorldMapDotCoors`) with 24 doors, doors 16-23 from `gNidlWorldMapDoors` (where each NiDL door is, where its path leads, its dots: 4 bytes each, as agbcc pads `struct WorldMapDotCoor`). The NiDL doors are placed so their paths are clear of KatAM's paths, doors and the map's captions (Vegetable Valley right of Rainbow Route East, Ice Cream Island right of the Moonlight Mansion door, Butter Building up from Rainbow Resort, Grape Garden left of Peppermint Palace East, Yogurt Yard left of Cabbage Cavern West, Orange Ocean right of Cabbage Cavern East and the Fountain of Dreams below it (which never opens: its switch room has no big switch); the dots evenly spaced, about 6 pixels apart, about 8 pixels off both doors' icons (15 off Central Circle's); a door's icon is centered on its 8 pixel tile, a dot shows 1.5 pixels right of and below its coordinates) (a door's icon shows about 4 pixels right of and below its coordinates; the map's captions cover the top middle and the bottom corners). `WorldMapUnlockSave`, `WorldMapGetCoorFromRoom` (Kirby on his NiDL level's door; in the NiDL hub, on Central Circle), `WorldMapDrawKirbys` (the pause map draws the unlocked NiDL doors' tile and paths' dots, which KatAM's map tilemap hasn't) and `GetKirbyRoomFlagIndex` (the NiDL rooms have no area map: the pause menu takes them as the tutorial's, Select switching between the help and the world map) are detoured to the C code (`Detour` relocates the literal loads of the prologue it copies). Doors 16-23 are the NiDL levels', 24-31 Squeak Squad's worlds' (their big switches' `+0x14` 25-32: `NidlBigSwitchId(8 + world - 1)`), each to a KatAM door (the owner's mock-up); the Fountain of Dreams' has no slot, off the map. In a Squeak Squad room (`gSqueakWorldRooms`: each world's first room, the hub, the first switch room), Kirby is drawn on his world's door, in its hub on Central Circle; the pause menu takes those rooms as the tutorial's (the world map with Select). Installed by the NiDL rooms, or by Squeak Squad's without them (once).
- **Map tracker**: the map table's rooms block (`+0xE0`: `NR`, the NiDL hub's room, the relocated room tables) lets the map tracker read the new rooms. The switch rooms show as big rooms, as KatAM's mini hubs (a NiDL room with a big switch).
- **Not yet**: the bosses' AI (their rooms are there), air currents, animated palettes, the museum signs' English text (the tiles are NiDL's Japanese ones).

## Sounds

NiDL's songs and sound effects are KatAM's own songs here (`KatAMExpansionSound.cs`): KatAM's song table (`gSongTable`, 620 songs) is moved and grown, and NiDL's whole table (579 songs) is copied after KatAM's, each song with its tracks, its voicegroup (the instruments its tracks pick, key splits and drum kits with theirs), its samples and wave shapes; NiDL's song n is KatAM's 620 + n, its music player kept (0 the music's, 1-3 the effects'). `gExpansionSongs[0]` (`rooms.c`, written by the randomizer) holds that first number: `nidl.h`'s `NIDL_SE(n)` and `NidlSound(obj, n)` play NiDL's sound n by its NiDL number. The NiDL rooms play their own room's music (its record's `+4`), the switch rooms their level hub's; a ported mini-boss or boss in a room with NiDL music keeps it (`NidlFightMusic()`), else KatAM's fight music plays. The ported enemies, mini-bosses, bosses and abilities play NiDL's sounds where NiDL's code does (found from its sound calls, `0x080031B8` and `0x0803E34C`: see `Documentation/Nightmare in Dream Land Notes.md`'s Sounds); Freeze's sound and Mr. Tick-Tock's alarm loop, and stop when their move does (Freeze's also when Kirby is hit or in water: `NidlKirbyHit()`; the alarm when Mr. Tick-Tock is hit or gone: `MrTickTockSilence()`). The bosses' too: King Dedede's, Paint Roller's, Kracko's, Heavy Mole's, Mr. Shine & Mr. Bright's, Meta Knight's, Nightmare's (his appearing and vanishing, the charge, the stars, the swoop) and the Power Orb's dash and crescents. Still KatAM's: Light's and Ball's sounds (not found in NiDL's code), Whispy Woods' puffs and the Power Orb's other shots (NiDL plays none there), the bosses' hit and defeat sounds (KatAM's own for its bosses), Kirby's own common sounds, the NiDL hub's music.

Squeak Squad's music and sound effects are compiled from the owner's Squeak Squad ROM (`KatAMExpansionSqueakSound.cs`: `sound/sound_data.sdat`'s IMA-ADPCM waves to 8-bit samples, its banks to voicegroups, its sequences to MP2K tracks, each note waiting as the DS's note-wait mode says: on unless a track's `C7 0` turns it off, as the music's do and the sound effects' never; a loop back with no wait in it gets one, MP2K running a track till it waits) into a second grown song table after NiDL's: its 43 music ids first (the DS's table at `0x02159770`), then its 760 sound ids (`0x02159DDC`). `gExpansionSongs[1]` holds the first; `nidl.h`'s `SQUEAK_SE(n)` and `SqueakSound(obj, n)`, and `squeak.h`'s `SqueakSfx(s, n)`, play the DS's sound n by its DS number. Each Squeak Squad room plays its map's music (its header's byte 0x0B; 0 keeps the one before); a world's boss keeps its arena's music, the DS's boss theme (`SqueakBossMusic()`), the mid-bosses KatAM's mini-boss music. The gimmicks (`squeak/gimmicks.c`) and every ported Squeak Squad character play the DS's sounds where its code does (every call of the DS's `0x02013EE8`, `0x02013E6C` and `0x020B0604`, matched to the port's states by their DS addresses: each file's header lists them); the ones the DS keeps playing go through `SqueakLoopSfx()` and `SqueakStopSfx()` (one a character, stopped too when a hit starts it over, `SqueakStart()`, and when it goes, `SqueakDestroy()`). Each time the song table grows (`GrowSongTable()`) the expansion's code is repointed at it too: its `PlaySfx()` reads `gSongTable` (linked at KatAM's address) for the song's player and priority.

## Title screen

With "Random Title Screen: KatAM's Intro or NiDL's Kirby Swarm", the seed picks KatAM's intro or NiDL's title animation (`KatAMExpansionTitle.cs`, `title.c`).

- **The NiDL enemies in KatAM's intro parade** (Random Intro Parade, `KatAMTitleScreen.AddNidlTypes`): the parade's two type tables (`gUnk_08387348`, 0x10 bytes a type: palette slot, lift, width, update; `gUnk_08D62530`, 6 bytes: anim, variant, tiles; 70 types each) move to the free space with a type more per enemy in `ParadeLoops` (types 0x46 on: Twizzy, Bubbles, Starman, Needlous, Blade Knight, Cool Spook, Gip), the intro's pointers to them (its literal pools) changed. Each new type is a plain walker (type 0x0C's update), its own palette slot, as wide as its widest frame, lifted from the parade's line by its frames' lowest point (height - origin's y); its sprite is an extra variant of the enemy's anim, after its frames: its NiDL walk or flight as its C code plays it (each step's GetPalette, GetTiles and ShowFrame, then JumpBack to the first). The moved tables follow a header ("PK", how many, each one's kind: small walker or flier), which the randomizer reads to add them to its kinds; the 16-palette and VRAM check counts them as any type.

- **NiDL's swarm** (USA addresses): 115 pink Kirbys run, walk, float and tumble across the screen from the right after the HAL Laboratory / Nintendo logo. It's data: `0x080CAA3C` sets each up from `0x08757440` (script, delay, x, y; the draw order is 15 - ((y - 1) >> 4)), and `0x080CAAB8` runs them every frame. After its delay a Kirby runs commands of its script (`0x087577D8`, by index), 14 bytes each: a mask, then 6 slots (velocity x and y, acceleration x and y, frame, delay; 8.8 pixels), the slots whose bit (`0x01`-`0x20`) is set taken; `0x40` plays the sound in slot 0, `0x80` calls script slot 0, `0x100` jumps to it, `0x200`/`0x400` loop (slot 0 the count, forever when 0), `0x800` restarts the script, `0x1000` returns, `0x2000` ends the Kirby. Commands run until one sets a delay. Then velocity += acceleration, position += velocity, and a Kirby with a frame is drawn there (frame table `0x087554B8`: one 32x32 sprite each, 16 pixels up and left, from NiDL's 2D sprite sheet, some flipped), ending off the screen. NiDL's logo script plays sound `0x10D` (the crowd) 129 frames in, turns its title's background on at 164 and wipes the logo away behind the swarm from 185 to 271. The first Kirby's running steps are sound `0x9C`.
- **Found in the NiDL ROM by its code** (the data moves between regions, and the European entries differ a little): the setup by its loop's end (`cmp r5, #114; ble`) and its literals (entries `+0x68`, scripts `+0x70`, count `+0x5C`), the frame table from the update (`+0x288`), the sprite sheet's two LZ77 blobs (the left and right 16 tiles of each row) from the graphics load script (`0x02020000, 0x00400003, 0x02020000`), and the Kirby palette from the literal before the code loading it (`0xB530 0x1C05 0x205C 0x223E`). The 32x32 poses (55, the leading run of the frame table) are copied as 16 tiles each in 1D order.
- **In KatAM** (`title.c`): KatAM's intro (`sub_08145B64`, LogoEnd's only call, detoured to `CreateNidlTitle`) is replaced: KatAM's logo stays up and the swarm runs over it, frame for frame as NiDL's (checked against NiDL's sprites), drawn with KatAM's OAM slots (`sub_08156D84`) in palette 15. At frame 210, when the swarm covers most of the screen, KatAM's own title screen starts under it (`CreateTitleScreen`; KatAM's logo and title share VRAM, so there's no wipe), its fade from white leaving the sprites out. A, B or Start before then skip to the title, as NiDL's waits do. The swarm ends with its last Kirby, when the title screen's task is gone, or when it takes A or Start (after its 0x4A frames). NiDL's `0x9C` is KatAM's dash sound; `0x10D` is the stampede KatAM's own intro plays (`SE_INTRO_AMBIENT_2`, every 5 frames while 24 Kirbys or more are on the screen).
- **VRAM**: the European title screen's sprites take 416 tiles (the others' 28), so the 55 poses (880 tiles) don't fit beside them. At most 36 are on the screen at once, so they go through 36 slots (576 tiles): a pose takes the slot drawn longest ago (none this frame), loaded through KatAM's VBlank DMA queue (`gUnk_03002EC0`, as `bg.c`'s), so it changes when the frame's sprites show.


## Squeak Squad characters

Characters of Kirby: Squeak Squad (Nintendo DS) are ported the same way, from the player's own Squeak Squad ROM (any release; the
graphics are never shipped): `squeak.h`, `squeak.c`, `squeak/*.c`, `KatAMExpansionSqueak.cs`. Research notes (formats, addresses,
how Squeak Squad's enemies work): [Squeak Squad Notes](../Documentation/Squeak%20Squad%20Notes.md).

- **Object type 0x2C** (KatAM's last free regular enemy type): the template's behavior byte picks the character (`SQUEAK_*`), its
  `unk12` byte the variant. Its spawn, parameters and start are written as type 0x31's are (`WriteSqueak`). The randomizer's
  "Nothing" (what it takes out) was type 0x2C: it's type 0x53 now, as empty in the three releases (`Utils.Nothing`).
- **With or without NiDL** (the owner: either game can be on alone): `AddEnemies` takes a NiDL ROM, a Squeak Squad ROM or both.
  Without NiDL's, only the code and what Squeak Squad's characters need go in (type 0x2C, the mini-boss types its mid-bosses take,
  their hurt anims and VRAM sized for its biggest mid-boss of each type, the HP bar, helper and blink patches); NiDL's enemies,
  mini-bosses, bosses, abilities, rooms, title, Star Rod stand, Mirras and shard hooks stay out, and the pause screens stay
  KatAM's. With NiDL's, the ROM is byte for byte what it was before Squeak Squad could be added alone.
- **In the randomizer** (the ROM Expansion tab's "Kirby: Squeak Squad" group; `KatAMExpansionSqueak.cs`'s pools,
  `KatAMEnemiesBase.cs`): Include Enemies puts type 0x2C in the enemy pool once per character (as NiDL's type 0x31), the
  behavior byte the character and `Properties[1]` (the template's `unk12`) one of its variants for the spot: ground spots, flying
  enemies' (Perara, Cret, Uja, Starman 0) and underwater ones' (Gussa, Gold Waddle Dee 0 and 5). Include Mini-Bosses (Big Metalun,
  Gao Gao, Tedhaun, Buboo) puts their mini-boss types (`MidbossType`) in the mini-boss pool, behavior `SQUEAK_MIDBOSS | index`,
  each as likely as NiDL's on the same type; Spinni, Doc and Storo (`SqueakMidbossPool`) join it on type 0x40
  (`SqueakAsMidboss`), fighting as a mini-boss (`SqueakMidbossUpdate`: the HP bar, the camera, the defeat). Include Bosses puts
  the bosses (`SqueakBossPool`: King Dedede, Mrs. Moley, Mecha-Kracko, Yadgaine, Bohboh, Daroach, Dark Daroach, Meta Knight,
  Dark Nebula) in it the same way, on type 0x40; outside their own rooms the ones that stand on the DS's floor height take the
  bottom of KatAM's locked arena as their floor (`SqueakBossFloor()`: the template's area, `unk1C` + `unk20`, less 16 pixels),
  so they fight on screen. Include Behaviors adds `natives.c`'s new variants (Waddle Dee 5-7, Glunk 5-6, Gordo
  5, Snooter 5) to those enemies' behaviors. Left out of the pools: what a character throws or brings in, Daroach's stage scene
  and the dark star (its route is a stage's). Add Chase Sequences to KatAM: see **The chases** below. The HP options take
  `gSqueakEnemyHp` by pool (`KatAMHP.cs`, only with the characters in the ROM: `HasSqueakSquad`; NiDL's tables only with NiDL's:
  `HasNightmare`); the spoiler log names each "(Squeak Squad)".
- **The Squeak Lairs** (`KatAMExpansionSqueakLevels.cs`' `PlanSqueakLevels()`, `SqueakLairPlan`; the rulebook's rules 5 and 8): each
  stage's thief on the DS (Spinni, Doc or Storo: a chase record with flag 8 too) fights in its own hideout (the DS's: one of the
  stage's rooms after its goal), made a mini-boss room between two of the stage's rooms. The DS door into the room the thief waits
  in (else the nearest to it) leads into the hideout, onto its DS arrival; the hideout's own DS doors aren't doors; its Squeak is
  a mini-boss (type 0x40, `SQUEAK_MIDBOSS` | the character, as the randomizer's), its area the screen over the floor Kirby lands on,
  with up to `LairSqueakers` of its Squeakers; its one way out (`DoorKind.Lair`, KatAM's one-way door) is built shut (its record
  alone, its mirror hidden), on the hideout's DS doorway, where its black doorway was (else, a hideout with none, on a floor away
  from where Kirby lands), leading where the door led; the room's gimmick list has an entry for it (`GIMMICK_LAIR_EXIT` 0xF2, its cell's spot:
  `squeak/gimmicks.c`'s `LairExit()`), which writes its record's collision once no mini-boss (types 0x38-0x52) with HP is left in
  the room, 30 frames after it comes up, with or without the NiDL ROM (`arena.c`'s `OpenDoors()` too, with it). The hideout's
  third map layer (only its black doorway, blocks 604 over 796) isn't drawn. The template carries `SQUEAK_LAIR_FIGHT` (`unk22`): a
  boss the randomizer puts there stands on that floor (`SqueakBossFloor()`: its area's bottom less 32). One fight a lair, kept in
  it (the owner: "Squeak lairs have double minibosses, that is a huge no-no. In one instance one clipped through the ground,
  creating a soft-lock. Room 0x4FE"): a fight whose DS spot is in the ground (the Squeakers' hideouts' first Squeaker's, 12
  pixels into the treasure pile's floor) stands on that floor's top; a lair's fight, and Spinni, Doc or Storo as a mid-boss
  anywhere, is held within KatAM's bounds across (`squeak.c`'s `SqueakHoldAcross()`: the edge a wall it turns at; KatAM's
  terrain reads past the room's edge as its edge cell, so the hideouts' floor running out of the room was no wall, and Storo,
  heading for Kirby on a ledge over it, walked on off the room's left side, its fight never over); and a lair room brings out no
  chase Squeaks (its Squeak records are its fight's and its Squeakers': in a stage with a chase, the chase brought its Squeak
  out a second time beside its mini-boss). The randomizer takes the
  lairs' mini-bosses with KatAM's (`SqueakLairMinibosses`, `KatAMEnemiesBase.cs`' `AddSqueakLairMinibosses()`). Each lair's door
  gets the DS's own nest, drawn into the room's foreground (`KatAMExpansionSqueakNests.cs`' `SqueakNests()`): every DS room's front
  block table holds it, 5 blocks by 3 (0x110-0x114, 0x130-0x134, 0x150-0x154), in its world's front tileset's look (a stump, a
  grassy mound, a rock pile, a castle, an igloo, a submarine, a factory, a UFO...), its doorway on the door's cell, its feet on the
  door's floor. 38 lairs (USA, Europe, Japan the same). Randomize Squeak Lairs (the window, with Include Rooms; `AddEnemies()`'
  `squeakLairShuffle`, `PlanSqueakLevels()`' `squeakLairSeed`): the lairs come from the seed instead (their own generator, the rest
  of the map the same): each of a stage's hideouts with its Squeaks one time in `LairShare` (3), its Squeak the fight (the Squeakers'
  hideout: one of `LairFighters`, Spinni, Doc or Storo, from the seed, on its first Squeaker's spot, `SqueakObjects()`), its door
  any DS door that can be one (as above) in a room that draws the DS's nest (`SqueakNestKey()`: the rooms' usual nest blocks, not
  8-3-9's; `SqueakNestDrawn()`: a front tileset whose nest covers 30-80% of its blocks, not 20_f's, 29_f's, 2C_f's, 2D_f's, 3B_f's,
  3E_f's or 3F_f's), picked from the seed; the spoiler log's "Squeak Lairs" lists them.
- **The goal doors** (`KatAMExpansionSqueakLevels.cs`' `PlanSqueakLevels()`, `KatAMExpansionSqueakRooms.cs`' `SqueakGoalDoorways()`):
  each stage's goal door is a mirror wherever it leads (into the next stage, the boss stage or a goal game), and the DS's wide
  black doorway is taken out of the room's tiles (the owner: "I think we can drop the wide black doors and replace them with
  regular mirrors since these look very odd in-game"): a 2 by 2 doorway on the door's cell and the one left of it, in the front
  grid for 43 of them (blocks 738, 739 over 770, 771), cleared; 4-4-9's in the third grid, cleared; 1-2-5's a cave in the main
  grid's cliff, drawn over with the cliff right of it. Before, a goal leading on kept the bare doorway, and one into a goal game
  had its mirror on the doorway's right half, the left half showing beside it (5-5-1, room 0x516).
- **The finale chain** (`KatAMExpansionSqueakLevels.cs`' `SqueakFinale()`, `SqueakShardExits()`; `squeak.c`'s `SqueakDestroy()`):
  Squeak Squad's final stage (`SqueakFinalStage`, 8-4) isn't world 8's boss stage: its boss (Dark Nebula) leaves no shard
  (`SQUEAK_FINALE_SHARD`: beaten, `NidlDanceOut()`), its way out its `gNidlShardExits` entry. With NiDL's finale in, Nightmare's
  ending flight's room step and his shard exit lead to 8-4's start, and Dark Nebula's way out is where they led (the Fountain of
  Dreams hub); without it, Central Circle's Dimension Mirror leads to 8-4's start and his way out is where it led (0x38D).
- **The chases** (`squeak/gimmicks.c`, `squeak.h`'s "The chases"; the owner: "an invisible trigger wall on the position of the
  chest, the height of the wall will be of the entire room ... whenever the player passes that threshold, the chase will activate";
  and for KatAM's own rooms, random and optional, the Squeaks "faster, more aggressive and go for Kirby"): a room's gimmick list
  carries its chase's key (the header's `unused[0]`) and two kinds of entries of the expansion's own: `0xF0`, the wall (its x the
  big chest's: a Kirby in the room crossing that x either way, at any height, turns the chase on), and `0xF1`, a Squeak the chase
  brings (`par[0]` the character, `par[1]` its variant), made (type 0x2C, `unk12` | `SQUEAK_CHASE_SPAWN`) once the chase is on and
  its spot is within 64 pixels of the screen, once while the room's loaded; and `0xF6`, a chest the chase brings (`ChaseChest()`:
  `par[0]` its item, `par[1]` its number, `par[2-3]` its mark, `par[4]` its half flag, `par[5]` its type), made at its spot as the
  room's Squeak Squad chests are once the chase is on, once while the room's loaded. The chase on is kept in the rooms' mail slots' spare
  bytes (slot 0's key, slot 1's its complement: not saved); it lasts till the player's Kirby (player 0) goes into a room of another
  key or none (`SqueakChaseRoom()`, from `rooms.c`'s `BigFillLevelInfo`). A chase's Squeak (`chase` in `SqueakObject`) is never
  culled off screen (flag `0x2000000`), takes no chest (`SqueakNearestChest()` gives none, so every Squeak goes for Kirby), goes at
  Squeak Squad's angry pace (the DS's when Kirby carries the stage's chest: Spinni and Doc `PACE_FAST`, Storo its fastest,
  Squeakers' hops 1.5 times; Spinni waits 40 frames between moves, Squeakers 20, all of their colors attack) and is steered at pits
  (a cell ahead with no floor 6 cells down, `SqueakPitAhead()`; a Squeaker looks two cells ahead, its hops being long): its own jump
  is followed through the room (`SqueakJumpLands()`: its speeds and gravity frame by frame, stopped across by walls, landing where its
  feet come onto a floor from the open), and it jumps if that lands (Spinni's jump, Storo's climb jump; a Squeaker picks whichever of
  its low and high hops lands, through its wind-up, `ChaseHop()`), goes down if the pit has a floor below and Kirby is lower
  (`SqueakDropToKirby()`), else waits on its side (`SqueakPit()`, `SqueakChaseSteer()`). One that still falls to the room's
  bottom is put back on the last floor it stood on, dropping onto it (`SqueakChaseSteer()`). In the Squeak Squad stages
  (`KatAMExpansionSqueakRooms.cs`'s `SqueakChaseOf()`): each stage with chase spawns gets a key (1 on), every room of it carries
  it, the rooms with a big chest a wall there, and the DS's chase spawns (the Squeaks among its objects with flag 4, away from the
  doors, at most 6 a room) become `0xF1` entries. In KatAM's rooms (Add Chase Sequences to KatAM, `KatAMExpansionChases.cs`): of
  its rooms with a big chest and no mini-boss or boss (the tutorial's left out), half, picked by the seed, each a chase of its own
  (key `0x80` on, `gSqueakChaseRooms`: a room id and its list), its wall at the chest, one to three Squeaks (Spinni, Doc, Storo 0-2,
  a Squeaker of any color) at its own enemies' spots at least 96 pixels from the chest (none such: its other objects' spots).
- **An anim per character, 256 frames each**: KatAM gives an object the frame its type's AnimInfo table lists for its frame index
  (`unk83`), at most 256 per type. The object drawing (`sub_0809A7A4`, USA `0x0809A824`) is patched to ask `SqueakAnimFor`: type
  0x2C objects show their character's own anim, with the frame index as the variant. Variants 0-2 are the hurt frame (the type's
  hurt indexes 0 and 1, and 2), then one per cell of the character's `.cea`.
- **Graphics** (`KatAMExpansionSqueak.cs`): the NDS file system's `enemy/<name>.cga` (tiles), `.cla` (palettes), `.cea` (cells and
  animations). Each cell is drawn from its DS OAM pieces onto a canvas (the character's origin, its feet, at the center) and cut
  into KatAM pieces (`CutFrame`). A character's colors go in one palette when they fit in 15 (`MergeColors` draws a color as
  another to get there: the Squeakers' near-white). A character in other colors (`Palettes`: its other `.cla` files) gets a
  palette for each, in a frame of its own after its cells, which `SqueakUsePalette` loads under a key of its own (its anim ID
  `| n << 13`). A character whose body switches between sprites (`ExtraFile`, comma-separated: Spinni's `spin_at`, Storo's
  `stron_atack,stron_dmg`) gets all the files' cells and animations in one anim, in that order. Its frames' canvas (`Canvas`,
  128 pixels a side, its spot in the middle) can be bigger: Storo's raised mallet, 96 pixels over its feet, takes 192. Pieces in palettes past what fits (`Layers`: up to
  two groups of the DS palettes, Spinni's shuriken in its hands, Doc's saucer and its robot arm) go to layers: an effect anim of
  their own each with their palette, its frame per cell in `gSqueakData`'s layer table, drawn by `SqueakShowLayer` on the
  character's spot in front of it or behind it, as the DS draws them. `WholeCells` (Meta Knight's sword, his body's pieces
  in its palettes too) keeps a cell whose pieces are mostly a layer's whole in that layer, its body's pieces with them (a cell
  split in two would draw his arm and sword apart), and merges the DS palettes that share cells into one when their colors fit
  15. The layer table is a `u16` per cell (its frame + 1, | 0x8000 drawn in front: Meta Knight's layers run past 127 frames),
  and a composite's body part sets its layers too (`SqueakShowComposite`), as a plain animation's cell does.
- **Palette slots**: KatAM keeps 7 palettes for a room's enemies (`sub_0803DFAC`: past them, each new one overwrites the last
  slot's). A sprite whose colors are all in one DS palette (or in DS palettes of the same colors) keeps that palette as it is, and
  every sprite built with the same colors (effects, layers, a character's things) loads it under the first one's key and frame
  (`SqueakPaletteKey`), so they share a slot: Doc's body, saucer, arm, rings and bomb take 5, its things and effects none of
  their own. A character whose cells are in different DS palettes (Doc's things: its bolts, missile, bomb, lid, saucer) gets a
  palette key per animation (`SqueakGraphics.palette`), loaded by `SqueakPlay`.
- **Held effects** (`SqueakHoldEffect`): an effect that stays on the character, looping, till the character ends it
  (`SqueakEndHeldEffect`) or is gone: the other parts of Doc's composite attacks (the bomb in its claw, its cannon's smoke).
- **Animations**: Squeak Squad's own (cell, frames, offset) steps, in `gSqueakData`, played by `SqueakPlay`/`SqueakAnimate` (a
  looping animation still flags that it got through once, as Squeak Squad's flag 8 that characters wait for). A step's offset
  moves its drawing (Squeak Squad adds it to the cell's position when it builds the OAM; KatAM's `objBase54`/`55`), across turned
  with the character as Squeak Squad's (its offsets are as it faces right). The cells are mirrored left to right: Squeak Squad's
  face right, KatAM's enemies' frames left (KatAM flips them when the object faces right).
- **Composite animations** (`.mca`, `SqueakPlayComposite`): a character with an `.mca` also plays compositions of its own
  animations, each part where the composition puts it (turned with the character) and at its own time (the frames since the
  composite animation started, or its time within it). Part 0 is the character's own sprite, on top; each other part is a
  `SqueakPartFx` effect object drawn behind it in their order, placed by the character every frame, hidden while KatAM shows its
  hurt frames, gone with the composite animation or the character. They're played live (not drawn into frames ahead of time): some
  characters' parts loop at lengths whose combinations would run to thousands of frames. Each part's sprite takes the VRAM its own
  frames need (the composition table's `tiles` byte: its animation's biggest frame; Part VRAM below), not the character's
  biggest frame; in a character with several palettes each part takes its animation's. `BodyLast` (King Dedede's `.mca` lists his
  body after its sparkles) moves a composition's last part first, so the character's own sprite, with its layers, shows the body.
  Layers keep the `.mca` (an empty composite table would have the character read garbage: `SqueakPlayComposite` now plays none).
  A character drawn from several files (`ExtraFile`) gets their `.mca`'s composites after its own (their parts' animations moved
  on), and `KeepComposites` keeps them through `OnlyAnims` (Mrs. Moley's dive and throws are in her other two files; a composition
  whose parts' animations aren't all kept is dropped, its composites with it: Meta Knight's ext keeps the 7 of 9 he uses). Files whose
  palettes don't all fit in 16 share the identical ones (Mrs. Moley's three have the same 16).
- **Part VRAM**: KatAM's object VRAM heap in a stage is 512 tiles (`gVramHeapMaxTileSlots`), and `VramMalloc` gives `ewram_end`
  when it's full (the sprite then draws other sprites' tiles: Mecha-Kracko's field and bolts showed HUD letters and a grey Kirby).
  A part's sprite takes its own animation's frames' VRAM (the part's `tiles`), is made again when another composition's part in
  its place needs a different size (its old one let go, freed a frame later), and asks for VRAM again each frame till it gets
  it, not drawn meanwhile. It's made on the cell it shows (`SqueakCreatePart`'s `cell`): KatAM's sprite copies its first frame's
  tiles as it's made, and the character's cell 0 (Meta Knight's tornado's 103-tile column) written into a small part's VRAM ran
  over the sprites after it till they changed frames (his sword's layer garbled while his tornado was out). A character drawn with another's graphics (`GraphicsOf`) gives type 0x2C its whole biggest frame:
  Mecha-Kracko's eye is a character of its own (`OnlyAnims`, 9 tiles) rather than drawn with its 124-tile frames.
- **Own sprites**: each ported object's sprite takes as many tiles as its own character's frames need (`SqueakGraphics.tiles`, its
  hurt frames counted), not its type's (KatAM gives every object of a type the same: type 0x2C's the biggest of all the enemies,
  a mini-boss type's the biggest mid-boss's): a boss's legs and its showers of rocks and blocks fit KatAM's 512-tile heap.
- **Second forms**: a mid-boss's bar can show part of its HP (`SqueakObject.maxHp`, the bar's full HP; `hpBase`, HP held back
  under its empty: `NidlHpBarFill` shows the HP over it), so a form's HP running out isn't KatAM's defeat (Mecha-Kracko starts
  with both forms' HP, its second form's held back).
- **Palette slots**: KatAM keeps 7 enemy palettes a room (`sub_0803DFAC`); once they're full every new key overwrites the last
  slot, and the sprites on it show another's colors. A character in one palette whose colors an earlier sprite already has takes
  that sprite's slot (Mrs. Moley's dirt takes hers: its petals came out yellow-green and her shadow unseen on its own key),
  unless it has other colors of its own (`Palettes`: the Squeakers' alternates count from their own key).
- **Screen shakes**: KatAM's `RequestScreenShake` 1-3 shake 14, 18 and 24 frames; 4-6 shake on till `sub_080700D8` stops them.
  The ports use the timed ones (King Dedede, Big Metalun, Gao Gao and Tedhaun had asked for 5 and shook the screen till the
  fight's end).
- **Moves**: Squeak Squad's enemies are a body driven by a brain (a virtual pad: a direction, A and B); both are ported, state for
  state. A state function returning TRUE runs the next one in the same frame, as Squeak Squad's dispatcher does (`SqueakRun`).
  Speeds and positions are Squeak Squad's 20.12 (`SQUEAK_SPEED` converts), Y up as KatAM's speeds; terrain is KatAM's.
- **Squeak Squad's engine** (`squeak.c`, its enemies' shared functions): `SqueakMove` (acceleration, speed caps, friction, then the
  move and the terrain there and then, as Squeak Squad's states check the ground right after moving: KatAM's own move and terrain,
  `sub_0809D8C8`, which `ObjectMain` runs after the object's function, and `SqueakFinishFrame` leaves it nothing to move again),
  `SqueakStop`, `SqueakCapFall`, `SqueakSinkTo`, `SqueakTurnAround` (the brain's -1, "turn round", becomes 1 once done, as Squeak
  Squad's), the ground, a wall ahead, water (as its last move met them: KatAM's `unk62` and `unk58`), facing Kirby and Kirby within
  a box. Characters with moves in water are flagged swimmers. The enemies with Squeak Squad's shared water state (its base class's
  `+0x6C`/`+0x70`: `SqueakDrowns`) drown the DS's way from `SqueakMove` (`SqueakDrown`: one of its hurt looks, sinking 0.4 a frame for
  42 frames, then KatAM's poof; KatAM's own drowning, `sub_0809D710`, is kept off with `unkC` 1); the other characters drown as
  KatAM's engine has them. The object is `SQUEAK_OBJECT_SIZE`, 0x1A4.
- **A world's boss leaves its shard** (`SqueakDestroy`, `SqueakBossShard`): in its boss room its template's `unk11` is the shard
  + 1 (`KatAMExpansionSqueakRooms.cs`); beaten (no HP left, the player in the room), as it goes it leaves the shard through NiDL's
  `NidlBossShard` (a Maxim Tomato if owned), and `gNidlShardExits` takes Kirby to the Squeak Squad hub after its cutscene.
  `SqueakSpawn`'s copies of a template never carry it. The boss room holds no treasure (the owner: "The boss rooms can only have
  the shard/tomato spawn and that's it"): its chest, the DS's reward (a Boss Battle Badge), is moved into the room before it, the
  boss stage's first, on the floor nearest its door to the boss room and 3 cells from every door (`SqueakAntechamber`: rooms 0x42D,
  0x46A, 0x4AA, 0x4E9, 0x521, 0x56A, 0x5AA); the boss room keeps that chest's template off the stage (`OffStageX`), so its other
  templates' numbers stay, and the shard still comes over the chest's old spot.
- **Effects** (`SqueakSpawnEffect`): Squeak Squad's shared effects (`effect/efc_cmn0`, `efc_cmn1`), each the cells one of its
  animations shows as an anim of its own (`SqueakEffectDefinitions`), played once by an effect object at the character's spot plus
  an offset, facing as it does, and following it when Squeak Squad's does (`0x020218C0`). So far: the dust puff (type 3), and the
  water's splashes (`SqueakSplash`: the stage's `m_efc` file's animations 4 into the water and 5 out of it, the same in its clay,
  sand and snow files), placed on the surface as Squeak Squad's stage does (`0x02215A10`), and a thing bursting (`efc_cmn1`'s
  animation 1, `0x020B48E0`'s type 6).
- **Probes and thrown things**: `SqueakTouches` is the stage's box test (its vtable `+0x18`: the box's edge on a side, either or
  both ends solid) on KatAM's tiles; `SqueakSpawn` makes another character (a thrown rock) with its own template copy, drawn with
  its thrower's graphics when the C# list says so (`GraphicsOf`).
- **On the ground**: Squeak Squad's spawn records stand a character on the ground; `SqueakStandOnFloor` puts its feet on the first
  floor below its template's cell.
- **Inhaled**: Squeak Squad's inhale reaches an enemy as hit kinds 3-5 (the pull) and, at Kirby's mouth, as touch kind 6. A
  character with a reaction of its own (Maiga) is kept out of KatAM's inhale (`unk5C |= 0x20`: KatAM's inhale box is attack kind
  0x20, its `unk68`, and the hit check at `0x08036646` skips the kinds an object's `unk5C` has) and finds the Kirby inhaling it
  with `NidlInhaler()`. **Holding Kirby**: KatAM's own hold (`kirby->unk110`, as `minibosses/bugzzy.c`); every entry of the table
  carries 0x400, the letting go ones too (KatAM's hold drops Kirby on the holder's spot, hopping, on an entry without it when the
  holder's frame is past its type's hurt frame: any frame of type 0x2C).
- **Through the terrain**: Squeak Squad's flag 0x200 (`0x0201355C`: the terrain is met but its push out isn't added to the move,
  nor the snap to the floor) is KatAM's 0x100 here (`SqueakThroughTerrain`): `SqueakMove` skips KatAM's terrain and holds the
  character's spot inside the stage, as Squeak Squad's does.
- **Boxes per frame**: Squeak Squad's `.rca` gives each animation frame its boxes, tagged: 0x101 attacks (their kind first: 0 hurts
  by touch, 6 grabs), 0x102 where it's hit, 0x201 and 0x202 its body (layout in `Documentation/Squeak Squad Notes.md`).
  `SqueakAnimTime` is the time into the animation those frames go by. KatAM's hit box is `sub_0803E2B0`'s (`unk38`-`unk3B`: an offset
  and half sizes, which KatAM turns with the facing); `ObjectSetBounds`' (`unk3C`-`unk3F`) is the terrain's. A character's tables
  (`SqueakBoxes`: per frame, the box that's hit and the one that hurts in one, whether it hurts, its grabbing boxes) are made from its
  `.rca` by the notes' layout; `SqueakSetBoxes` sets each frame's, `SqueakGrabbing` finds a Kirby free to grab (KatAM's grabbers'
  test, `SqueakKirbyFree`) touching its grabbing boxes (Uja's glove, Bio Spark's jump). **Where it's hit**: Squeak Squad's test
  (`0x0201322C`) takes the frame's own 0x102 box, else the sprite's first one (animation 0's first frame, copied when its sprite is
  loaded: `0x02014218`), and refuses every attack when that box is flagged 0x8000, when the object's +0x18 has bit 2 (its grabs and
  holds) or while its +0xEC safe timer runs (checked on the DS: each boss's recorded states against that test). `SqueakBoxes`'
  `untouchable` words name the animations whose boxes are flagged; `SqueakSetBoxes` holds KatAM's hit level at 6 then
  (`SqueakUntouchable`, as Gordo's: no attack nor inhale takes it), and a character's own code does so for its holds.
- **File names**: `build.sh` names each object by its file's base name, so a file here must not share one with another folder's
  (`squeak/starman_kss.c`: NiDL's Starman is `enemies/starman.c`).
- **Animation ends** (`animEnded`, Squeak Squad's flag 8): the body's update sets it each frame from its sprite's (`0x02014590`
  asking `vtable +0x1C`, `0x01FFA8B8`): a held animation's from the frame it got through on, a looping one's only on the frames it
  starts over (a pulse: Bio Spark's brain waits three loops of its slash, 6 frames each, as logged on the DS); a composite one's
  as a plain one's (4 frames started at frame t: done at t + 4, Bubble Head's as logged).
- **Touching Kirby** (`SqueakTouchingKirby`): his hit box and the character's in whole pixels, edges touching, as KatAM's touch
  hurts him (logged), for what Squeak Squad's touch reactions do (Bubble Head's bubbles pop on him).
- **Mid-bosses**: on the mini-boss types NiDL's use (0x40-0x42, which KatAM treats as mini-bosses everywhere: HP bar, music,
  camera, defeat), told from NiDL's by the template's behavior byte (`SQUEAK_MIDBOSS`, 0x80, | the character; NiDL's are under
  0x60). The mini-bosses' spawn hands them to `CreateSqueakMidboss` and their resume to `SqueakStart`; the drawing, the HP bar's
  name and its fill take them as Squeak Squad characters (`SqueakIsSqueak`). As NiDL's and Mr. Frosty, one waits facing Kirby till
  he's in the template's area (its `unk1A`-`unk20`: left, top, width, height), then the camera locks there, the HP bar shows, the
  mini-boss music plays and it starts; in its fight it stays in that area (`SqueakMidbossUpdate`). Its frames have to fit its
  type's VRAM (`KatAMExpansionSqueak.cs` checks), and type 0x2C's VRAM is the regular characters' biggest frame. Their HP: Squeak
  Squad's mid-bosses have 140 (Big Metalun 300), KatAM's Mr. Frosty 30: so 30 (Big Metalun 64).
- **Enemies KatAM has** (`squeak/natives.c`): they stay KatAM's own objects (their look, moves, speeds, hurt, ability), and the
  behaviors Squeak Squad gives them that KatAM's lack are new variants of them (the template's `subtype1`):
  `KatAMExpansionSqueak.cs` points the type's spawn (the spawn table) and its resume after a hit or bump (its parameters' `+16`)
  at `natives.c`'s, keeping KatAM's own in `gSqueakNatives`; KatAM's own variants go to KatAM's. The new variants' brains keep
  their state in fields KatAM's hits never touch (`unkA0`-`unkA6`), so they go on after one. So far: the Waddle Dee, the Glunk, the Gordo and the Snooter (below).
  Squeak Squad's parasol (on Waddle Dees, Waddle Doos and Shotzos) is KatAM's own parasol (object `0xA2`): held while its holder
  floats down, let go when it lands or is hit, then flying off, or kept once landed; KatAM's Waddle Dee (variants 3 and 4), Waddle
  Doo and Shotzo all have it.

| Perara | enemy 0x0D | `squeak/perara.c` | Hovers; on the ground it waits for Kirby within 60 pixels, crouches (58 frames), launches 70 pixels up (two puffs of dust, one rising with it) and floats down swaying; checked against the DS game frame by frame (crouch, launch, apex, sway start). |

HP 2 (Squeak Squad's 10, a Waddle Dee's). Checked in KatAM (room 0x83's water): the line-up, dash, bonk, return, leap and splashes, the parts' sprites and their clean-up; not yet against a DS recording of it in water (no water save state). Its sound (the bonk) is Squeak Squad's (`SqueakSfx`). |

HP 2 (Squeak Squad's 10), its rock 1. Checked in KatAM (room 0x323's star block: grab, cling, climbing a short wall, the throw, rock and burst); not yet against a DS recording, nor on a tall wall. Its sounds are Squeak Squad's (`SqueakSfx`). |
| Maiga | enemy 0x0F | `squeak/maiga.c` | A little slime that hops (1.0 up, a big hop 2.1 with A; 0.8 across in the air), picking after each landing: in place, forward, turned round, or big hops till it next goes forward. Kirby's inhale can't swallow it: it leans in and slides at him at 2.0 a frame, bites his face 8 pixels from him and holds him there, bobbing 2 pixels; 120 frames, or fewer as he mashes (each pad change counts 4 more), and it flings him back at 2.6, hurt when he lands (KatAM's thrown-Kirby state: 1 HP; the DS's 6 of 36). HP 2 (Squeak Squad's 10). Checked against the DS (its hops, the pull, bite, bob, release and throw, by logging both) and in KatAM (room 0x323: the hops, the pull, the bite on Kirby and on a helper, mashing free in 42 frames, the throw and the hurt landing). Inhaled, it blows back instead; kept inhaling, its cloud is pulled off and it pops into KatAM's Gordo (30 in 100) or a treat (Maxim Tomato 15, meat 20, pop soda 20, small food 10, 1-UP 5: Squeak Squad's foods matched to KatAM's by how much they heal). Not checked: loops A and B, the fly-away, the ball's burst on screen, what the pop leaves in KatAM. | Uja | enemy 0x11 | `squeak/uja.c` | A dark puff that floats and bobs (as Cret: to 0.8 forward, the bob 0.1 every 20 frames), through the terrain; it shrinks into a scimitar (variant 1) or a glove (any other) for 600 frames and goes at Kirby, its point or fingers turning up or down to him. | Bio Spark | enemy 0x25 | `squeak/biospark.c` | A ninja. Its jump's hands grab Kirby (`biospark.rca`'s kind 6 boxes; the jump itself doesn't hurt): it holds him 12 frames, goes up through the terrain at 8.0 (32 frames, or to 16 pixels over the screen), hangs there 33 frames and drops at 8.0 through the terrain; landed, it throws him away (2.0, 2.8 up: KatAM's throw that hurts him as he lands, 1 HP) and the screen shakes (KatAM's shortest). It flies a circle 19 pixels across its middle, 120 frames a lap: forward at 1.0, then up, 3 degrees a frame (Squeak Squad's velocities from its sine table, kept to Squeak Squad's 1/4096 of a pixel so the circle closes), through everything, facing left as Squeak Squad's spawns it; a hit or bump lets it go on where it was. | Snooter (KatAM's) | enemy 0x07 | `squeak/natives.c` | KatAM's own Snooter (object 0x07) with Squeak Squad's standing brain, as its variant (`subtype1`) 5 (KatAM's own templates use 0; Squeak Squad's other brain, looking round and walking, is KatAM's own). It faces Kirby and stands where it is, never looking round or crawling; KatAM's own swallow, chew and spit (its mouth) and its rush after a hit stay KatAM's, then it stands again (after the rush, facing Kirby, as Squeak Squad's). A pre-callback puts it back whenever KatAM's code returns it to KatAM's idle. Checked against the DS (logged: both brains, 700 frames standing, the swallow and spit, the walk after a hit) and in KatAM (room 0x323, USA: 600 frames standing; Kirby walked into it: swallowed, spat out, standing again; a Sword hit (its HP raised by a RAM write): KatAM's rush; the rush's end forced by RAM writes: facing Kirby, standing; KatAM's own variant 0 unchanged). Not checked: a real rush to its end (it kept rushing into Kirby), eating an object. |
| Big Metalun | mid-boss 0x34 | `squeak/big_metalun.c` | A big metal walker, on mini-boss type 0x41 (the template's `subtype1` 0x94). Its fight starts as a mini-boss's (it waits for Kirby in its area, then the camera lock and its HP bar, "Big Metalun"); it waits 75 frames, untouchable, then its brain faces Kirby, rests 60, 120 or 180 frames, stomps 1, 2 or 4 steps (142 frames a step, moving 1 to 3 pixels on its step frames, two stomps shaking the screen; a wall turns it round) and charges: it sparkles, curls up, sits as a ball 30, 60 or 120 frames and rolls at 4.0 till a wall or the edge of its area (the one KatAM keeps its mini-bosses in), bonks (a shake), lies dazed 180 frames and stands up (a shake); then it turns, faces Kirby and, 30 in 100, charges again after 60 frames, else starts over. Off a ledge it falls (gravity 0.17, at most 3.6) and lands. Hit, it doesn't flinch (KatAM's mini-boss flash and shake, as Squeak Squad's doesn't either); its defeat is KatAM's mini-boss's. HP 64 (Squeak Squad's 300). Checked against the DS (logged, Kirby pinned away: the intro, walk, charge, ball, roll, the bonk at the stage's limit and at a wall, the daze, stand-up and the brain's waits, frame for frame) and in KatAM (room 0x323, USA, its area set in the template: the same frames, the walk's 284 counted frames (one KatAM lag frame between), the bonk at its area's edge and at a wall, charging again and starting over; Sword hits (64 to 45 HP, no flinch, its moves going on); the defeat (HP lowered by a RAM write, finished with Sword); screenshots). Beaten and inhaled, it gives Metal, as the DS's (`squeak.c`'s `sSqueakCharacters`; the owner: "Squeak Squad Minibosses do not give their supposed ability."; with the NiDL ROM, as the ability itself). |
